:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | tumhassa | (savibhattissa) | (ādesā) | tuvaṃ \| tvaṃ | aṃmhi | |
|        | `tumha` | (with its inflection) | (replaced by) | `tuvaṃ` \| `tvaṃ` | `aṃ` | |

The sutta is `tumhassa tuvaṃ tvaṃ aṃmhi`. `savibhattissa` and `ādesā` continue from the previous rules, but the sutta names `tumha` alone: `amha` gets nothing here. There is no option marker, yet the rule does not displace [§143 (235): Taṃ mamaṃmhi](#m143) and [§144 (234): Tavaṃ mamañca navā](#m144) — it adds to them, so that `tumha` + `aṃ` has four shapes in all, `taṃ`, `tavaṃ`, `tuvaṃ` and `tvaṃ`, as the Bālāvatāra lists them. `tuvaṃ` and `tvaṃ` are otherwise the shapes of the first form ①⨀ ([§140 (232): Tvamahaṃ simhi ca](#m140)); this rule lets them stand as the object too. The vutti's two examples are not found in the Tipiṭaka, where `tuvaṃ` and `tvaṃ` are regularly the subject.

:::

> Sabbassa tumhasaddassa savibhattissa tuvaṃ tvaṃ iccete ādesā honti aṃmhi vibhattimhi.

> Kaliṅgarassa tuvaṃ maññe,

> kaṭṭhassa tvaṃ maññe.

The whole of the word `tumha`, together with its inflection, is replaced by `tuvaṃ` and `tvaṃ` when the `aṃ` inflection (②⨀) follows.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️②⨀(tumha) | kaliṅgarassa tuvaṃ maññe | I take you for [a chip] of a log |
| ⚧️②⨀(tumha) | kaṭṭhassa tvaṃ maññe | I take you for [a piece] of wood |

|     | tumha + aṃ (②⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (tumha + aṃ → tuvaṃ)  | §146 |
| →   | **tuvaṃ**             |      |

|     | tumha + aṃ (②⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (tumha + aṃ → tvaṃ)   | §146 |
| →   | **tvaṃ**              |      |

:::

---

<a name="m147"></a>

### §147 (246): Padato dutiyā catutthī chaṭṭhīsu vo no (after a word, `vo`, `no` in the plural of ②, ④, ⑥) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| (navā) | padato | (tumhamhākaṃ savibhattīnaṃ) | (ādesā) | vo \| no | dutiyā catutthī chaṭṭhīsu (bahuvacanesu) | (yathāsaṅkhyaṃ) |
| (in some places) | after a word | (`tumha`, `amha` with their inflection) | (replaced by) | `vo` \| `no` | ②⨂ ④⨂ ⑥⨂ | (respectively) |

This rule begins the enclitic forms of the two pronouns, which run to §151. `padato` means "after a word" (`pada`): the pronoun must follow another word of its sentence, and a pronoun that opens the sentence keeps its full form. `dutiyā catutthī chaṭṭhīsu` names the second, fourth and sixth forms, and the vutti adds `bahuvacanesu`: only their plurals — the `yo` of ②⨂ and the `naṃ` of ④⨂ and ⑥⨂, which by [§161 (244): Tumhamhehi namākaṃ](#m161) and [§162 (237): Vā yvappaṭhamo](#m162) otherwise give `tumhākaṃ`, `amhākaṃ`. The subject, `tumhamhākaṃ savibhattīnaṃ` ("of `tumha` and `amha` with their inflection"), continues from [§139 (245): Tumhamhākaṃ tayi mayi](#m139); so does the `navā` that the vutti supplies, carried down from [§144 (234): Tavaṃ mamañca navā](#m144) — the Bālāvatāra makes the carry-over explicit (`navātveva`). `navā` restricts: `vo` and `no` are confined to the position and the forms the rule names, and even there the full forms are not excluded — the vutti's `eso amhākaṃ satthā` shows the option not taken. Ruiz-Falqués (§3.1 of the reference chapter) notes that here the form under `navā` is in fact the commoner one in the canon.

:::

> Sabbesaṃ tumhaamhasaddānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ yadā padasmā paresaṃ vo no ādesā honti navā yathāsaṅkhyaṃ dutiyā catutthīchaṭṭhīiccetesu bahuvacanesu.

> Pahāya vo bhikkhave gamissāmi,

> mā no ajja vikantiṃsu, rañño sūdā mahānase,

> evaṃ dutiyatthe.

> Dhammaṃ vo bhikkhave desessāmi,

> saṃvibhajetha no rajjena,

> evaṃ catutthyatthe.

> Tuṭṭhosmi vo bhikkhave pakatiyā,

> satthā no bhagavā anuppatto,

> evaṃ chaṭṭhyatthe.

> Navāti kimatthaṃ?

> Eso amhākaṃ satthā.

> Tumhamhākamiti kimatthaṃ?

> Ete isayo passasi.

> Padatoti kimatthaṃ?

> Tumhākaṃ satthā.

> Etesvīti kimatthaṃ?

> Gacchatha tumhe.

When the whole of the words `tumha` and `amha`, together with their inflection, comes after a word (`padato`), it is replaced — in some places (`navā`) — by `vo` and `no` respectively, in the plural of the second, fourth and sixth forms (②⨂, ④⨂, ⑥⨂).

:::tip[Examples]

The vutti gives two sentences for each form and labels them `evaṃ dutiyatthe` ("thus in the sense of the second form") and so on. Two of them (`pahāya vo …`, `tuṭṭhosmi vo …`) add a `bhikkhave` that the Tipiṭaka's verse does not have; the citations give the canonical wording.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️②⨂(tumha) | pahāya vo bhikkhave gamissāmi | leaving you, bhikkhus, I shall go |
| ⚧️②⨂(amha) | mā no ajja vikantiṃsu rañño sūdā mahānase | let the king's cooks not cut us up today in the kitchen |
| ⚧️④⨂(tumha) | dhammaṃ vo bhikkhave desessāmi | I shall teach you the Dhamma, bhikkhus |
| ⚧️④⨂(amha) | saṃvibhajetha no rajjena | may you (sir) share the kingdom with us |
| ⚧️⑥⨂(tumha) | tuṭṭhosmi vo bhikkhave pakatiyā | I am pleased, bhikkhus, with your natural way |
| ⚧️⑥⨂(amha) | satthā no bhagavā anuppatto | our teacher, the Blessed One, has arrived |

> `pahāya vo gamissāmi` \
> [(7D 3.20: Ānandayācanakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/7D/3/3.20#405) \
🔽(pahāya) ⚧️②⨂(tumha) ⏯👆⨀(gacchati) \
having left | you | I shall go

> `mā no ajja vikantiṃsu, rañño sūdā mahānase` \
> [(23J 21.1.2: Mahāhaṃsajātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/23J/21/21.1/21.1.2#895) \
🔼(mā) ⚧️②⨂(amha) 🔼(ajja) ⏮🤟⨂(vikantati) 🚹⑥⨀(rāja) 🚹①⨂(sūda) 🚻⑦⨀(mahānasa) \
not | us | today | let them cut up | the king's | cooks | in the kitchen

> `dhammaṃ vo, bhikkhave, desessāmi` \
> [(11M 5.6: Chachakkasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/11M/5/5.6#1192) \
🚹②⨀(dhamma) ⚧️④⨂(tumha) 🚹⓪⨂(bhikkhu) ⏯👆⨀(deseti) \
the teaching | to you | bhikkhus | I shall teach

> `saṃvibhajetha no rajjena` \
> [(7D 6.6.1: Rajjasaṃvibhajana)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/7D/6/6.6/6.6.1#687) \
⏯🤟⨀(saṃvibhajati) ⚧️④⨂(amha) 🚻③⨀(rajja) \
may (he) share | with us | the kingdom

> `tuṭṭhosmi vo pakatiyā` \
> [(23J 21.1.1: Cūḷahaṃsajātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/23J/21/21.1/21.1.1#850) \
🚹①⨀(tuṭṭha) ▶️👆⨀(atthi) ⚧️⑥⨂(tumha) 🚺③⨀(pakati) \
pleased | I am | with your | natural way

> `satthā no bhagavā anuppatto` \
> [(3V 10.4: Pācīnavaṃsadāyagamanakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/10/10.4#1992) \
🚹①⨀(satthu) ⚧️⑥⨂(amha) 🚹①⨀(bhagavantu) 🚹①⨀(anuppatta) \
the teacher | our | the Blessed One | has arrived

|     | tumha + yo (②⨂), after a word | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (tumha + yo → vo)             | §147 |
| →   | **vo**                        |      |

:::

:::danger[Counter-example]

By saying `navā` (in some places) the replacement is not made everywhere its conditions hold: after a word, in the plural of the sixth form, `amhākaṃ` may stand unchanged.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️⑥⨂(amha) | eso amhākaṃ satthā | this is our teacher |

> `yo amhākaṃ satthā` \
> [(9M 2.1: Cūḷasīhanādasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/9M/2/2.1#455) \
⚧️①⨀(ya) ⚧️⑥⨂(amha) 🚹①⨀(satthu) \
who | our | teacher

:::

:::danger[Counter-example]

By saying `tumhamhākaṃ` (of `tumha` and `amha`) — the word with which the CST prints the heading of [§139 (245): Tumhamhākaṃ tayi mayi](#m139), `Tumhamhākaṃ tayi mayi`, a genitive plural of the pair formed with the `ākaṃ` of [§161 (244): Tumhamhehi namākaṃ](#m161) — the rule reaches these two pronouns only. Any other word standing after a word keeps its plural inflection.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹②⨂(isi) | ete isayo passasi | you see these sages |

:::

:::danger[Counter-example]

By saying `padato` (after a word) a pronoun that opens its sentence is not replaced: `tumhākaṃ satthā`, never `vo satthā`. The enclitics do not stand first.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️⑥⨂(tumha) | tumhākaṃ satthā | your teacher |

:::

:::danger[Counter-example]

By saying `dutiyā catutthī chaṭṭhīsu` (in the second, fourth and sixth) — `etesu`, "in these", in the vutti's question — the `yo` of the first form (①⨂) is not covered, even after a word: `gacchatha tumhe`, not `gacchatha vo`. [§151 (250): Bahuvacanesu vo no](#m151) will nonetheless admit `vo`, `no` before that `yo`, by a second reading of its own wording.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️①⨂(tumha) | gacchatha tumhe | you go |

> `gacchatha tumhe, sāriputtā` \
> [(1V 1.2.13: Kuladūsakasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.2/1.2.13#1757) \
▶️🤘⨂(gacchati) ⚧️①⨂(tumha) 🚹⓪⨂(sāriputta) \
go | you | Sāriputta [and Moggallāna]

:::

---

<a name="m148"></a>

### §148 (247): Temekavacanesu ca (and `te`, `me` in the singular [of ④ and ⑥]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     | (padato) | (tumhamhākaṃ savibhattīnaṃ) | (ādesā) | te \| me | (catutthī chaṭṭhīsu) ekavacanesu | (yathāsaṅkhyaṃ) |
| and    | (after a word) | (`tumha`, `amha` with their inflection) | (replaced by) | `te` \| `me` | ④⨀ ⑥⨀ | (respectively) |

The sutta is `te me ekavacanesu ca`. `padato`, the subject and the forms `catutthī chaṭṭhīsu` continue from [§147 (246): Padato dutiyā catutthī chaṭṭhīsu vo no](#m147); by saying `ca` this rule is an extension of it: the same replacement after a word, now in the singular (`ekavacanesu`) instead of the plural, and with `te`, `me` in place of `vo`, `no`. Only the fourth and sixth forms come across — the `sa` (④⨀, ⑥⨀) that otherwise gives `tava`, `tuyhaṃ`, `mama`, `mayhaṃ` by [§141 (241): Tava mama se](#m141) and [§142 (242): Tuyhaṃ mayhañca](#m142); the singular of the second form, `aṃ`, is shut out by the next rule. The `navā` of §147 is not carried: the vutti states the replacement without an option.

:::

> Sabbesaṃ tumha amhasaddānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ yadā padasmā paresaṃ te me ādesā honti yathāsaṅkhyaṃ catutthīchaṭṭhīiccetesu ekavacanesu.

> Dadāmi te gāmavarāni pañca,

> dadāhi me gāmavaraṃ,

> idaṃ te raṭṭhaṃ,

> ayaṃ me putto.

> Padatoti kimatthaṃ?

> Tava ñāti,

> mama ñāti.

When the whole of the words `tumha` and `amha`, together with their inflection, comes after a word, it is replaced by `te` and `me` respectively in the singular of the fourth and sixth forms (④⨀, ⑥⨀).

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️④⨀(tumha) | dadāmi te gāmavarāni pañca | I give you five choice villages |
| ⚧️④⨀(amha) | dadāhi me gāmavaraṃ | give me a choice village |
| ⚧️⑥⨀(tumha) | idaṃ te raṭṭhaṃ | this kingdom is yours |
| ⚧️⑥⨀(amha) | ayaṃ me putto | this is my son |

> `dadāmi te gāmavarāni pañca` \
> [(22J 11.1.2: Juṇhajātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/22J/11/11.1/11.1.2#2131) \
▶️👆⨀(dadāti) ⚧️④⨀(tumha) 🚻②⨂(gāmavara) ⚧️②⨂(pañca) \
I give | to you | choice villages | five

> `idaṃ te raṭṭhaṃ sadhanaṃ sayoggaṃ` \
> [(23J 20.1.1: Kusajātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/23J/20/20.1/20.1.1#582) \
⚧️①⨀(ima) ⚧️⑥⨀(tumha) 🚻①⨀(raṭṭha) 🚻①⨀(sadhana) 🚻①⨀(sayogga) \
this | your | kingdom | with its wealth | with its equipage

|     | tumha + sa (④⨀), after a word | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (tumha + sa → te)             | §148 |
| →   | **te**                        |      |

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `padato` (after a word) the pronoun that opens its phrase is not replaced; it takes the full forms of [§141 (241): Tava mama se](#m141).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️⑥⨀(tumha) | tava ñāti | your kinsman |
| ⚧️⑥⨀(amha) | mama ñāti | my kinsman |

:::

:::note[Additional]

The Tipiṭaka is looser than the letter of the rule: after a word the full forms are common too — `paripakko vayo mayhaṃ, parittaṃ mama jīvitaṃ` stands in the same verse as `pahāya vo gamissāmi, kataṃ me saraṇamattano` [(7D 3.20: Ānandayācanakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/7D/3/3.20#405). The rule records where `te` and `me` may occur, not where they must.

:::

---

<a name="m149"></a>

### §149 (248): Na aṃmhi (not before `aṃ`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| na     | (padato) | (tumhamhākaṃ savibhattīnaṃ) | na (ādesā) | ~~te \| me~~ | aṃmhi | |
| not    | (after a word) | (`tumha`, `amha` with their inflection) | no (replacement) | ~~`te` \| `me`~~ | `aṃ` | |

`te me`, `padato` and the subject continue from [§148 (247): Temekavacanesu ca](#m148), and `na` negates them: before the `aṃ` of the second form (②⨀) the enclitics are not used, even after a word; the pronoun keeps `taṃ`, `maṃ` ([§143 (235): Taṃ mamaṃmhi](#m143)) or the other shapes of §144 and §146. The prohibition is needed because §147 named the second form among the three that take enclitics and §148 then extended the enclitics to the singular: without this rule `te`, `me` could be read as the singular of the second form as well. Being a negation, the rule has no option marker; it is absolute.

:::

> Sabbesaṃ tumha amhasaddānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ yadā padasmā paresaṃ te me ādesā na honti aṃmhi vibhattimhi.

> Passeyya taṃ vassasataṃ arogaṃ,

> so maṃ bravīti.

When the whole of the words `tumha` and `amha`, together with their inflection, comes after a word, it is not replaced by `te` and `me` when the `aṃ` inflection (②⨀) follows.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️②⨀(tumha) | passeyya taṃ vassasataṃ arogaṃ | may he see you healthy for a hundred years |
| ⚧️②⨀(amha) | so maṃ bravīti | he speaks to me |

> `passeyya taṃ vassasataṃ arogaṃ` \
> [(23J 21.1.5: Mahāsutasomajātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/23J/21/21.1/21.1.5#1269) \
⏯🤟⨀(passati) ⚧️②⨀(tumha) 🚻②⨀(vassasata) 🚹②⨀(aroga) \
may he see | you | for a hundred years | healthy

> `passāsi maṃ vassasataṃ arogaṃ` \
> [(23J 21.1.5: Mahāsutasomajātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/23J/21/21.1/21.1.5#1270) \
▶️🤘⨀(passati) ⚧️②⨀(amha) 🚻②⨀(vassasata) 🚹②⨀(aroga) \
you see | me | for a hundred years | healthy

:::

---

<a name="m150"></a>

### §150 (249): Vā tatiye ca (and optionally in the third [singular]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| vā     | (padato) | (tumhamhākaṃ savibhattīnaṃ) | (ādesā) | (te \| me) | tatiye (ekavacane) | ca |
| optionally | (after a word) | (`tumha`, `amha` with their inflection) | (replaced by) | (`te` \| `me`) | ③⨀ | and |

`te me`, `padato`, the subject and `ekavacane` continue from [§148 (247): Temekavacanesu ca](#m148) — the `na` of [§149 (248): Na aṃmhi](#m149) does not, having belonged to `aṃmhi` alone. By saying `ca` this rule is an extension of §148 to a further form, the singular of the third (`tatiye`, ③⨀). The option marker `vā` opens an alternative to [§145 (238): Nāmhi tayā mayā](#m145): after a word the full forms `tayā`, `mayā` are as good as the enclitics, and the vutti sets `kataṃ te pāpaṃ` and `kataṃ tayā pāpaṃ` side by side. Both are common in the canon.

:::

> Sabbesaṃ tumhaamhasaddānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ yadā padasmā paresaṃ te me ādesā honti vā yathāsaṅkhyaṃ tatiyekavacane pare.

> Kataṃ te pāpaṃ,

> kataṃ me pāpaṃ,

> kataṃ tayā pāpaṃ,

> kataṃ mayā pāpaṃ.

> Padatoti kimatthaṃ?

> Tayā kataṃ,

> mayā kataṃ.

When the whole of the words `tumha` and `amha`, together with their inflection, comes after a word, it is optionally replaced by `te` and `me` respectively when the singular inflection of the third form (③⨀, `nā`) follows.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️③⨀(tumha) | kataṃ te pāpaṃ | evil has been done by you |
| ⚧️③⨀(amha) | kataṃ me pāpaṃ | evil has been done by me |
| ⚧️③⨀(tumha) | kataṃ tayā pāpaṃ | evil has been done by you (by §145) |
| ⚧️③⨀(amha) | kataṃ mayā pāpaṃ | evil has been done by me (by §145) |

> `kataṃ me saraṇamattano` \
> [(7D 3.20: Ānandayācanakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/7D/3/3.20#405) \
🚻①⨀(kata) ⚧️③⨀(amha) 🚻①⨀(saraṇa) 🚹⑥⨀(atta) \
made | by me | a refuge | of myself

|     | amha + nā (③⨀), after a word  | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (amha + nā → me)              | §150 |
| →   | **me**                        |      |

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `padato` (after a word) the pronoun that opens its phrase is not replaced; only the `tayā`, `mayā` of [§145 (238): Nāmhi tayā mayā](#m145) can stand there.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️③⨀(tumha) | tayā kataṃ | done by you |
| ⚧️③⨀(amha) | mayā kataṃ | done by me |

:::

---

<a name="m151"></a>

### §151 (250): Bahuvacanesu vo no (`vo`, `no` in the plural [of the third]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | (padato) | (tumhamhākaṃ savibhattīnaṃ) | (ādesā) | vo \| no | (tatiyā) bahuvacanesu | (yathāsaṅkhyaṃ) |
|        | (after a word) | (`tumha`, `amha` with their inflection) | (replaced by) | `vo` \| `no` | ③⨂ | (respectively) |

`padato`, the subject and `tatiyā` continue from [§150 (249): Vā tatiye ca](#m150); `bahuvacanesu` takes the place of its `ekavacane`, and `vo`, `no` the place of its `te`, `me`: after a word the `hi` of the third form (③⨂) becomes `vo`, `no`. The `vā` of §150 is not carried — the vutti states the plural replacement without an option. The vutti then reads a second rule out of the word `bahuvacanesu` (`bahuvacanaggahaṇena`): because the sutta says "in the plurals" rather than "in the plural of the third", `vo` and `no` also stand for the `yo` of the first form (①⨂) after a word — the very case that the `etesu` of [§147 (246): Padato dutiyā catutthī chaṭṭhīsu vo no](#m147) had excluded (`gacchatha tumhe`). With that the enclitic system is complete: after a word, `vo`/`no` are the plural of forms ①, ②, ③, ④ and ⑥, and `te`/`me` the singular of ③, ④ and ⑥; the fifth and seventh forms and the singular of the second have none.

:::

> Sabbesaṃ tumhaamhasaddānaṃ savibhattīnaṃ yadā padasmā paresaṃ vo no ādesā honti yathāsaṅkhyaṃ tatiyābahuvacanesu paresu.

> Kataṃ vo kammaṃ,

> kataṃ no kammaṃ.

> Padatoti kimatthaṃ?

> Tumhehi kataṃ,

> amhehi kataṃ.

When the whole of the words `tumha` and `amha`, together with their inflection, comes after a word, it is replaced by `vo` and `no` respectively when the plural inflection of the third form (③⨂, `hi`) follows.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️③⨂(tumha) | kataṃ vo kammaṃ | the work was done by you |
| ⚧️③⨂(amha) | kataṃ no kammaṃ | the work was done by us |

|     | tumha + hi (③⨂), after a word | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (tumha + hi → vo)             | §151 |
| →   | **vo**                        |      |

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `padato` (after a word) the pronoun that opens its phrase keeps `hi`, its `a` becoming `e` by [§101 (80): Suhisvakāro e](#m101).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️③⨂(tumha) | tumhehi kataṃ | done by you (plural) |
| ⚧️③⨂(amha) | amhehi kataṃ | done by us |

:::

> Bahuvacanaggahaṇena yomhi paṭhame vo noādesā honti.

> Gāmaṃ vo gaccheyyātha, gāmaṃ no gaccheyyāma.

By the mention of `bahuvacanesu` (in the plurals) `vo` and `no` are also the replacements before the `yo` of the first form (①⨂).

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️①⨂(tumha) | gāmaṃ vo gaccheyyātha | you should go to the village |
| ⚧️①⨂(amha) | gāmaṃ no gaccheyyāma | we should go to the village |

|     | tumha + yo (①⨂), after a word | rule |
| --- | ----------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (tumha + yo → vo)             | §151 |
| →   | **vo**                        |      |

:::

---

<a name="m152"></a>

### §152 (136): Pumantassā simhi (`ā` for the final of `puma` before `si`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | puma   | antassa (savibhattissa) | (ādeso) | ā | simhi | |
|        | `puma` | final (with its inflection) | (replaced by) | `ā` | `si` | |

The sutta is `puma-antassa ā simhi`. A new subject: the stem `puma` ("male", "man"), whose forms occupy §152–§159. `antassa`, "of the final", is the final `a` of `puma`, and `savibhattissa`, which the vutti supplies, takes the `si` along with it: `pum` + `ā` gives `pumā`. There is no option marker; the rule is obligatory. The vutti then reads a second rule out of the word `anta` (`antaggahaṇena`): the finals of `maghava` (Maghavā, a name of Sakka) and `yuva` ("youth"), and of stems like them, take the same `ā` with their `si` — `maghavā`, `yuvā`.[^82]

:::

> Pumaiccevamantassa savibhattissa ā ādeso hoti simhi vibhattimhi.

> Pumā tiṭṭhati.

> Simhīti kimatthaṃ?

> Pumāno tiṭṭhanti.

The final [`a`] of the word `puma` ("male", "man"), together with its inflection, is replaced by `ā` when the `si` inflection (①⨀) follows.

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(puma) | pumā tiṭṭhati | the man stands |

|     | puma + si (①⨀)        | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pum + (a + si → ā)    | §152 |
| →   | **pumā**              |      |

> `nevitthī na pumā āsiṃ` \
> [(23J 22.1.8: Mahānāradakassapajātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/23J/22/22.1/22.1.8#2606) \
🔼(na eva) 🚺①⨀(itthī) 🔼(na) 🚹①⨀(puma) ⏮👆⨀(atthi) \
neither | a woman | nor | a man | I was

:::

:::danger[Counter-example]

By saying `simhi` (before `si`) the plural is not touched: before `yo` the final becomes `āno` by [§155 (137): Yosvāno](#m155).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(puma) | pumāno tiṭṭhanti | the men stand |

:::

> Antaggahaṇena maghava yuvaiccevamādīnamantassasavibhattissa āādeso hoti.

> Maghavā, yuvā.

By the mention of `anta` (final), the final of `maghava`, `yuva` and the like, together with its inflection, is also replaced by `ā` [before `si`].

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(maghava) | maghavā | Maghavā (Sakka) |
| 🚹①⨀(yuva) | yuvā | a youth |

> `idaṃ vatvāna maghavā devarājā sujampati` \
> [(12S1 11.2.8: Gahaṭṭhavandanāsutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/12S1/11/11.2/11.2.8#1663) \
⚧️②⨀(ima) 🔽(vatvāna) 🚹①⨀(maghava) 🚹①⨀(devarāja) 🚹①⨀(sujampati) \
this | having said | Maghavā | king of the gods | Sujā's husband

> `daharo yuvā maṇḍanakajātiko` \
> [(1V 1.1.3: Tatiyapārājikasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.1/1.1.3#378) \
🚹①⨀(dahara) 🚹①⨀(yuva) 🚹①⨀(maṇḍanakajātika) \
young | a youth | fond of adornment

:::

[^82]: The Padarūpasiddhi-ṭīkā (on Rūpasiddhi 197) extends the `antaggahaṇa` of this rule to `addha` ("time"), which gives the frequent Tipiṭaka form `atīto addhā` ("the past"), and `addhāno` before `yo` — an attested case of "and the like".

---

<a name="m153"></a>

### §153 (138): Amālapanekavacane (`aṃ` in the vocative singular) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | (puma) | (antassa savibhattissa) | (ādeso) | aṃ | ālapanekavacane | |
|        | (`puma`) | (final with its inflection) | (replaced by) | `aṃ` | ⓪⨀ | |

The sutta is `aṃ ālapana-ekavacane`. `puma`, `antassa`, `savibhattissa` and `ādeso` continue from [§152 (136): Pumantassā simhi](#m152). `ālapana` is the vocative, whose singular `si` is called `ga` by [§57 (71): Ālapane si ga sañño](#m57): the final `a` of `puma` and that `si` together become `aṃ`, `pumaṃ`. There is no option marker. The vutti's two questions mark off the two neighbouring forms: the plain `si` of the first form, which gives `pumā` (§152), and the vocative plural, which gives `pumāno` ([§155 (137): Yosvāno](#m155)).

:::

> Pumaiccevamantassa savibhattissa aṃ ādeso hoti ālapanekavacane pare.

> He pumaṃ.

> Ālapaneti kimatthaṃ?

> Pumā.

> Ekavacaneti kimatthaṃ?

> He pumāno.

The final of the word `puma`, together with its inflection, is replaced by `aṃ` when the vocative singular (⓪⨀) follows.

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⓪⨀(puma) | he pumaṃ | O man! |

|     | puma + si (⓪⨀)        | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pum + (a + si → aṃ)   | §153 |
| →   | **pumaṃ**             |      |

The vocative `pumaṃ` is not attested in the Tipiṭaka; the same form occurs there as the object (②⨀).

:::

:::danger[Counter-example]

By saying `ālapane` (in the vocative) the `si` of the first form is excluded; it takes `ā` by [§152 (136): Pumantassā simhi](#m152).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(puma) | pumā | a man |

:::

:::danger[Counter-example]

By saying `ekavacane` (in the singular) the vocative plural is excluded; it takes `āno` by [§155 (137): Yosvāno](#m155).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⓪⨂(puma) | he pumāno | O men! |

:::

---

<a name="m154"></a>

### §154 (0): Samāse ca vibhāsā (and optionally in a compound) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| vibhāsā | (puma) | (antassa) | (ādeso) | (aṃ) | samāse | ca |
| optionally | (`puma`) | (final) | (replaced by) | (`aṃ`) | in a compound | and |

`puma`, `antassa` and the replacement `aṃ` continue from [§153 (138): Amālapanekavacane](#m153); by saying `ca` this rule is an extension of it from the vocative to a new setting, the compound (`samāse`) — once the compound has been formed (`samāse kate`), so that the `aṃ` is here no inflection but part of the stem inside the compound. `vibhāsā` is Kaccāyana's second word for an open option, the equivalent of `vā`; it occurs only here and in the Samāsakappa ([§323 (360): Vibhāsā rukkha tiṇa pasu dhana dhañña janapadā dīnañca](/kaccayana/4-samasakappa#m323)), both times in a compound (Ruiz-Falqués §3.2). Both results are therefore good Pāli by the rule: `puma` may take the `aṃ` as a member of a compound or stay `puma`. The sutta is missing from the Rūpasiddhi, hence `(0)`, and Ruiz-Falqués notes that it "is not based on canonical evidence": no compound with `pumaṃ` inside it is attested.

:::

> Pumaiccevamantassa samāse ca aṃ ādeso hoti vibhāsā samāse kate.

> Itthī ca pumā ca napuṃsakaṃ ca itthipumannapuṃsakāni[^20],

> itthipumanapuṃsakānaṃ samūho itthipumannapuṃsakasamūho.

> Vibhāsāti kimatthaṃ?

> Itthipumanapuṃsakāni.

And in a compound the final of the word `puma` is optionally replaced by `aṃ`, once the compound has been formed.

:::tip[Examples]

The vutti gives each compound with its analysis (`viggaha`): `itthī ca pumā ca napuṃsakaṃ ca`, "a woman and a man and a neuter", makes the `dvanda` compound `itthipumannapuṃsakāni`; `itthipumanapuṃsakānaṃ samūho`, "a collection of women, men and neuters", makes the `tappurisa` compound `itthipumannapuṃsakasamūho`.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻①⨂(itthī + puma + napuṃsaka) | itthipumannapuṃsakāni | women, men and neuters |
| 🚹①⨀(itthipumannapuṃsaka + samūha) | itthipumannapuṃsakasamūho | the collection of women, men and neuters |

|     | itthi + puma + napuṃsaka (compound)  | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | itthi + pum + (a → aṃ) + napuṃsaka   | §154 |
| →   | itthipuma(ṃ → n) + napuṃsaka         | §31  |
| →   | **itthipumannapuṃsaka**              |      |

With the niggahīta assimilated by [§31 (49): Vaggantaṃ vā vagge](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m31) the compound is `itthipumannapuṃsaka`, plural `itthipumannapuṃsakāni`: the double `nn` is the only visible trace of the rule.

:::

:::danger[Counter-example]

By saying `vibhāsā` (optionally) the compound may equally be formed without the `aṃ`, `puma` standing unchanged: `itthipumanapuṃsakāni` — the shape the adhikāra sutta [§131 (0): Itthipumanapuṃsakasaṅkhyaṃ](#m131) itself uses.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻①⨂(itthī + puma + napuṃsaka) | itthipumanapuṃsakāni | women, men and neuters |

:::

[^20]: CST4 prints the two examples as `itthipumanapuṃsakāni` and `itthipumanapuṃsakasamūho`, identical with the counter-example and showing no trace of the `aṃ` the rule teaches. A. Thitzana's edition and translation (Motilal Banarsidass, 2016) reads `itthipumannapuṁsakāni`, `itthipumannapuṁsakasamūho`, with the `aṃ` turned into `n` before `n` by §31 `Vaggantaṃ vā vagge`; that reading is adopted here. The CST's form would arise only if the niggahīta were dropped again by §39 `Byañjane ca`.

---

<a name="m155"></a>

### §155 (137): Yosvāno (`āno` before `yo`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | (puma) | (antassa savibhattissa) | (ādeso) | āno | yosu | |
|        | (`puma`) | (final with its inflection) | (replaced by) | `āno` | `yo` | |

The sutta is `yosu āno`. `puma`, `antassa`, `savibhattissa` and `ādeso` continue from [§152 (136): Pumantassā simhi](#m152) and [§153 (138): Amālapanekavacane](#m153) — §154 was a one-rule digression on the compound. `yosu`, "before the `yo`s", covers the `yo` of both the first and the second form, and with the first its vocative: `pumāno` serves for ①⨂, ②⨂ and ⓪⨂. There is no option marker; the rule is obligatory.

:::

> Pumaiccevamantassa savibhattissa āno ādeso hoti yo su vibhattīsu.

> Pumāno,

> he pumāno.

> Yosvīti kimatthaṃ?

> Pumā.

The final of the word `puma`, together with its inflection, is replaced by `āno` when the `yo` inflections (①⨂, ②⨂) follow.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(puma) | pumāno | men |
| 🚹⓪⨂(puma) | he pumāno | O men! |

|     | puma + yo (①⨂)        | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pum + (a + yo → āno)  | §155 |
| →   | **pumāno**            |      |

:::

:::danger[Counter-example]

By saying `yosu` (before `yo`) the singular `si` is excluded; it takes `ā` by [§152 (136): Pumantassā simhi](#m152).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(puma) | pumā | a man |

:::

:::note[Additional]

`pumāno` is not found in the Tipiṭaka. Where the canon needs the plural of `puma` it has `pumā`, the ordinary `a`-stem plural by [§107 (69): Sabbayonīnamāe](#m107): `thiyova tassa jāyanti, na pumā jāyare kule` [(22J 8.1.6: Cetiyajātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/22J/8/8.1/8.1.6#1731), "only women are born to him, no males are born in the family".

:::

---

<a name="m156"></a>

### §156 (142): Āne smiṃmhi vā (optionally `āne` before `smiṃ`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| vā     | (puma) | (antassa savibhattissa) | (ādeso) | āne | smiṃmhi | |
| optionally | (`puma`) | (final with its inflection) | (replaced by) | `āne` | `smiṃ` | |

The subject continues from [§155 (137): Yosvāno](#m155). The option marker `vā` opens an alternative to [§108 (90): Smāsmiṃnaṃ vā](#m108), by which the `smiṃ` of an `a`-stem becomes `e`: `pumāne` or `pume`, the vutti's own `pume vā`. Both are good Pāli by the rule, though, as Ruiz-Falqués remarks (§3.3), neither occurs in the canon: the `vā` here opens a choice between two "ghost forms".

:::

> Pumaiccevamantassa savibhattissa āne ādeso hoti vā smiṃmhi vibhattimhi.

> Pumāne, pume vā.

The final of the word `puma`, together with its inflection, is optionally replaced by `āne` when the `smiṃ` inflection (⑦⨀) follows.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨀(puma) | pumāne | in a man |
| 🚹⑦⨀(puma) | pume | in a man (by §108) |

|     | puma + smiṃ (⑦⨀)        | rule |
| --- | ----------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pum + (a + smiṃ → āne)  | §156 |
| →   | **pumāne**              |      |

|     | puma + smiṃ (⑦⨀)        | rule |
| --- | ----------------------- | ---- |
| →   | puma + (smiṃ → e)       | §108 |
| →   | pum + (~~a~~ + e)       | §12  |
| →   | **pume**                |      |

The second derivation elides the stem's `a` before the `e` by [§12 (13): Sarā sare lopaṃ](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m12).

:::

---

<a name="m157"></a>

### §157 (140): Hivibhattimhi ca (and before the `hi` inflection) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     | (puma) | (antassa) | (ādeso) | (āne) | hivibhattimhi | |
| and    | (`puma`) | (final) | (replaced by) | (`āne`) | `hi` | |

`puma`, `antassa` and the replacement `āne` continue from [§156 (142): Āne smiṃmhi vā](#m156); by saying `ca` this rule is an extension of it to a further environment, the `hi` inflection (③⨂ and ⑤⨂). Two things do not continue. The `vā` of §156 is dropped: the vutti states the rule without an option. And `savibhattissa` is dropped too — which is why the sutta says `hivibhattimhi`, "before the `hi` inflection", and not a bare `himhi`: as the vutti explains in its question and answer, the repetition of the word `vibhatti` cancels the `savibhattissa` carried over since §152 (`savibhattiggahaṇanivattanatthaṃ`), so that `āne` replaces the final `a` alone and the `hi` stays — `pumānehi`, and `pumānebhi` by [§99 (81): Smāhismiṃnaṃ mhābhimhi vā](#m99). The vutti finally draws two further rules from `ca` (`caggahaṇena`): `āna` for the final of `maghava`, `yuva` and the like before `si`, `yo`, `aṃ` and `yo`; and `u` for the final of `puma`, `kamma` ("action") and `thāma` ("strength") before `sa` and `smā`. In both the inflection again stays, and takes its ordinary shape after the new final.

:::

> Pumaiccevamantassa hivibhattimhi ca āne ādeso hoti.

> Pumānehi,

> pumānebhi.

> Puna vibhattiggahaṇaṃ kimatthaṃ?

> Savibhattiggahaṇanivattanatthaṃ.

> Pumānehi.

And the final of the word `puma` is replaced by `āne` when the `hi` inflection (③⨂, ⑤⨂) follows.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨂(puma) | pumānehi | by men |
| 🚹③⨂(puma) | pumānebhi | by men (by §99) |

|     | puma + hi (③⨂)          | rule |
| --- | ----------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pum + (a → āne) + hi    | §157 |
| →   | **pumānehi**            |      |

|     | puma + hi (③⨂)          | rule |
| --- | ----------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pum + (a → āne) + hi    | §157 |
| →   | pumāne + (hi → bhi)     | §99  |
| →   | **pumānebhi**           |      |

Neither form is attested in the Tipiṭaka.

:::

:::tip[Purpose]

Why is `vibhatti` mentioned again — `hivibhattimhi`, not `himhi`? To cancel the `savibhattissa` ("together with its inflection") carried over from the previous rules: `āne` replaces the final `a` only, and the `hi` remains — `pumānehi`, not `pumāne`.

:::

> Caggahaṇena maghava yuvaiccevamādīnamantassa āna ādeso hoti si yo aṃ yo iccetesu vibhattīsu.

> Pumakammathāmantassa cukāro hoti sasmāsu vibhattīsu.

> Maghavāno, maghavānā.

> Maghavānaṃ, maghavāne.

> Yuvāno, yuvānā,

> yuvānaṃ, yuvāne,

> pumuno, pumunā.

> Kammuno, kammunā,

> thāmuno, thāmunā.

By the mention of `ca`, `āna` is the replacement of the final of `maghava`, `yuva` and the like when the inflections `si`, `yo`, `aṃ` and `yo` (①⨀, ①⨂, ②⨀, ②⨂) follow;[^83] and `u` is the replacement of the final of `puma`, `kamma` and `thāma` when the inflections `sa` and `smā` (④⨀ and ⑥⨀, ⑤⨀) follow.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(maghava) | maghavāno | Maghavā |
| 🚹①⨂(maghava) | maghavānā | Maghavās |
| 🚹②⨀(maghava) | maghavānaṃ | Maghavā |
| 🚹②⨂(maghava) | maghavāne | Maghavās |
| 🚹①⨀(yuva) | yuvāno | a youth |
| 🚹①⨂(yuva) | yuvānā | youths |
| 🚹②⨀(yuva) | yuvānaṃ | a youth |
| 🚹②⨂(yuva) | yuvāne | youths |

|     | maghava + si (①⨀)           | rule |
| --- | --------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | maghav + (a → āna) + si     | §157 |
| →   | maghavāna + (si → o)        | §104 |
| →   | maghavān + (~~a~~ + o)      | §12  |
| →   | **maghavāno**               |      |

The `si` becomes `o` by [§104 (66): So](#m104) and the `yo` become `ā`, `e` by [§107 (69): Sabbayonīnamāe](#m107), as after any stem in `a`; the stem's final vowel drops before them by [§12 (13): Sarā sare lopaṃ](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m12). The `si` forms `maghavāno`, `yuvāno` stand beside the `maghavā`, `yuvā` of [§152 (136): Pumantassā simhi](#m152). None of these eight forms is attested in the Tipiṭaka.

:::

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑥⨀(puma) | pumuno | of a man |
| 🚹⑤⨀(puma) | pumunā | from a man |
| 🚻⑥⨀(kamma) | kammuno | of an action |
| 🚻⑤⨀(kamma) | kammunā | from an action |
| 🚹⑥⨀(thāma) | thāmuno | of strength |
| 🚹⑤⨀(thāma) | thāmunā | from strength |

|     | puma + sa (⑥⨀)          | rule |
| --- | ----------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pum + (a → u) + sa      | §157 |
| →   | pumu + (sa → no)        | §117 |
| →   | **pumuno**              |      |

|     | puma + smā (⑤⨀)         | rule |
| --- | ----------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pum + (a → u) + smā     | §157 |
| →   | pumu + (smā → nā)       | §215 |
| →   | **pumunā**              |      |

With the final in `u` the stem is a `la`-word, so the `sa` becomes `no` by [§117 (124): Jhalato sassano vā](#m117) and the `smā` becomes `nā` by [§215 (141): Jhalato ca](#m215). The vutti's `sasmāsu` is the locative plural of the pair `sa` + `smā`, like `sasmiṃnāsu` in [§127 (102): To titā sasmiṃnāsu](#m127) and `sanāsu` in [§198 (123): Uttaṃ sanāsu](#m198); `su` is not one of the inflections named, which is why the examples show only `sa` and `smā` forms — the `su` of `puma` is provided for by [§158 (143): Susmimā vā](#m158). The `sa` forms serve for both the fourth and the sixth (④⨀, ⑥⨀).

> `pañca ca kammuno satāni` \
> [(6D 2.3.2: Makkhaligosālavāda)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/6D/2/2.3/2.3.2#247) \
⚧️①⨂(pañca) 🔼(ca) 🚻⑥⨀(kamma) 🚻①⨂(sata) \
five | and | of action | hundreds

> `kisā hāyanti thāmunā` \
> [(23J 22.1.1: Mūgapakkhajātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/23J/22/22.1/22.1.1#1384) \
🚹①⨂(kisa) ▶️🤟⨂(hāyati) 🚹③⨀(thāma) \
the lean | fall short | in strength

The Tipiṭaka has the `u` form of `thāma` before `nā` (③⨀), an inflection the vutti does not list here; for `puma` and `kamma` the `nā` is dealt with in [§159 (139): Unāmhi ca](#m159) and [§160 (197): A kammantassa ca](#m160).

:::

[^83]: The Rūpasiddhi reads `sabbāsu vibhattīsu`, "in all the inflections", where Kaccāyana's vutti names `si`, `yo`, `aṃ` and `yo` only; it thus generalises `āna` to the whole paradigm of `maghava` and `yuva`, whereas Kaccāyana leaves their remaining inflections to the ordinary rules for stems in `a`.

---

<a name="m158"></a>

### §158 (143): Susmimā vā (optionally `ā` before `su`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| vā     | (puma) | (antassa) | (ādeso) | ā | susmiṃ | |
| optionally | (`puma`) | (final) | (replaced by) | `ā` | `su` | |

The sutta is `su-smiṃ ā vā`: `susmiṃ` is the locative of the inflection-name `su`, "before `su`" (⑦⨂). `puma` and `antassa` continue from [§157 (140): Hivibhattimhi ca](#m157), and so does its cancelling of `savibhattissa`: the `su` remains. The option marker `vā` opens an alternative to [§101 (80): Suhisvakāro e](#m101), by which the `a` before `su` becomes `e`: `pumāsu` or `pumesu`. Neither is attested in the Tipiṭaka.

:::

> Pumaiccevamantassa suiccetasmiṃ vibhattimhi ā ādeso hoti vā.

> Pumāsu, pumesu vā.

The final of the word `puma` is optionally replaced by `ā` when the `su` inflection (⑦⨂) follows.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨂(puma) | pumāsu | among men |
| 🚹⑦⨂(puma) | pumesu | among men (by §101) |

|     | puma + su (⑦⨂)          | rule |
| --- | ----------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pum + (a → ā) + su      | §158 |
| →   | **pumāsu**              |      |

|     | puma + su (⑦⨂)          | rule |
| --- | ----------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pum + (a → e) + su      | §101 |
| →   | **pumesu**              |      |

:::

---

<a name="m159"></a>

### §159 (139): Unāmhi ca (and `u` before `nā`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| (vā)   | (puma) | (antassa) | (ādeso) | u (ā) | nāmhi | ca |
| (optionally) | (`puma`) | (final) | (replaced by) | `u` (`ā`) | `nā` | and |

The sutta is `u nāmhi ca`. `puma` and `antassa` continue. `ca` here is `anukaḍḍhana`, as the vutti says: it pulls the `ā` and the `vā` of [§158 (143): Susmimā vā](#m158) along into this rule, so that before `nā` (③⨀) the final of `puma` becomes `ā` or `u`, and optionally — the alternative being [§103 (79): Ato nena](#m103), by which the `nā` after `a` becomes `ena`. Three forms result, `pumānā`, `pumunā` and `pumena`; of them only `pumunā` is found in the Tipiṭaka.

:::

> Pumaiccevamantassa ā u ādesā honti vā nāmhi vibhattimhi.

> Pumānā, pumunā, pumena vā.

> Caggahaṇamanukaḍḍhanatthaṃ.

And the final of the word `puma` is optionally replaced by `ā` and `u` when the `nā` inflection (③⨀) follows.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(puma) | pumānā | by a man |
| 🚹③⨀(puma) | pumunā | by a man |
| 🚹③⨀(puma) | pumena | by a man (by §103) |

|     | puma + nā (③⨀)          | rule |
| --- | ----------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pum + (a → u) + nā      | §159 |
| →   | **pumunā**              |      |

|     | puma + nā (③⨀)          | rule |
| --- | ----------------------- | ---- |
| →   | puma + (nā → ena)       | §103 |
| →   | pum + (~~a~~ + ena)     | §12  |
| →   | **pumena**              |      |

> `labbhā hi pumunā idaṃ` \
> [(23J 22.1.3: Suvaṇṇasāmajātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/23J/22/22.1/22.1.3#1634) \
🔼(labbhā) 🔼(hi) 🚹③⨀(puma) ⚧️①⨀(ima) \
possible | for | by a man | this

:::

:::note[Additional]

`Caggahaṇamanukaḍḍhanatthaṃ`: the word `ca` is included for pulling along (`anukaḍḍhana`) the `ā` and the `vā` of the previous rule, which the sutta itself does not repeat.

:::

---

<a name="m160"></a>

### §160 (197): A kammantassa ca (and `a` for the final of `kamma` [before `nā`]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| (vā)   | kamma  | antassa | (ādeso)  | a (u)      | (nāmhi) | ca |
| (optionally) | `kamma` | final | (replaced by) | `a` (`u`) | (`nā`) | and |

The sutta is `a kamma-antassa ca`. `nāmhi`, `vā` and the `u` of [§159 (139): Unāmhi ca](#m159) continue; `ca` carries them to a new stem, `kamma` ("action", "deed"): before `nā` its final becomes `u` or `a`, or — the alternative that `vā` keeps open — the `nā` becomes `ena` by [§103 (79): Ato nena](#m103): `kammunā`, `kammanā`, `kammena`. The `a` is a replacement of `a` by `a`, taught only to block §103 and so admit `kammanā`. The vutti then extracts a further rule from `ca` (`caggahaṇena`): `ā` for the final of `maghava` and `yuva` before `nā` and `su`, but `kvaci`, "in some places". `kvaci`, like `navā`, marks a restriction (Ruiz-Falqués §2 and §3.4): the `ā` forms `maghavānā`, `maghavāsu`, `yuvānā`, `yuvāsu` belong to particular words in particular places and are no general option for stems in `a`; even for these two words the vutti keeps the ordinary `maghavena`, `maghavesu` (§103, [§101 (80): Suhisvakāro e](#m101)) beside them, so the restriction says where the `ā` may appear, not that it must. None of the eight `maghava`/`yuva` forms is attested in the Tipiṭaka.

:::

> Kammaiccevamantassa ca u a ādesā honti vā nāmhi vibhattimhi.

> Kammunā, kammanā, kammena vā.

And the final of the word `kamma` ("action") is optionally replaced by `u` and `a` when the `nā` inflection (③⨀) follows.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻③⨀(kamma) | kammunā | by action |
| 🚻③⨀(kamma) | kammanā | by action |
| 🚻③⨀(kamma) | kammena | by action (by §103) |

|     | kamma + nā (③⨀)         | rule |
| --- | ----------------------- | ---- |
| →   | kamm + (a → u) + nā     | §160 |
| →   | **kammunā**             |      |

|     | kamma + nā (③⨀)         | rule |
| --- | ----------------------- | ---- |
| →   | kamm + (a → a) + nā     | §160 |
| →   | **kammanā**             |      |

> `kammunā vasalo hoti, kammunā hoti brāhmaṇo` \
> [(18Sn 1.7: Vasalasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Sn/1/1.7#156) \
🚻③⨀(kamma) 🚹①⨀(vasala) ▶️🤟⨀(hoti) 🚻③⨀(kamma) ▶️🤟⨀(hoti) 🚹①⨀(brāhmaṇa) \
by action | an outcaste | one is | by action | one is | a brahmin

> `ñatticatutthena kammena` \
> [(1V 1.1.1.3: Santhatabhāṇavāra)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.1/1.1.1/1.1.1.3#87) \
🚻③⨀(ñatticatuttha) 🚻③⨀(kamma) \
with the motion as fourth | by a formal act

`kammanā` is not attested in the Tipiṭaka.

:::

> Caggahaṇena maghavayuvaiccevamantassa ā ādeso hoti kvaci nā su iccetesu vibhattīsu.

> Maghavānā, maghavāsu, maghavesu, maghavena vā.

> Yuvānā, yuvāsu, yuvesu, yuvena vā.

By the mention of `ca`, `ā` is — in some places (`kvaci`) — the replacement of the final of `maghava` and `yuva` when the `nā` and `su` inflections (③⨀, ⑦⨂) follow.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(maghava) | maghavānā | by Maghavā |
| 🚹⑦⨂(maghava) | maghavāsu | among Maghavās |
| 🚹⑦⨂(maghava) | maghavesu | among Maghavās (by §101) |
| 🚹③⨀(maghava) | maghavena | by Maghavā (by §103) |
| 🚹③⨀(yuva) | yuvānā | by a youth |
| 🚹⑦⨂(yuva) | yuvāsu | among youths |
| 🚹⑦⨂(yuva) | yuvesu | among youths (by §101) |
| 🚹③⨀(yuva) | yuvena | by a youth (by §103) |

|     | maghava + nā (③⨀)         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | maghav + (a → ā) + nā     | §160 |
| →   | **maghavānā**             |      |

:::

---

> _Iti nāmakappe dutiyo kaṇḍo._

Thus ends the second section of the nāma chapter.

---

## Tatiyakaṇḍa (Third Section)

<a name="m161"></a>

### §161 (244): Tumhamhehi namākaṃ (`ākaṃ` for `naṃ` after `tumha`, `amha`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | tumhamhehi | naṃ (vacanassa) | (ādeso) | ākaṃ | | |
|        | after `tumha`, `amha` | `naṃ` inflection (④⨂, ⑥⨂) | (replaced by) | `ākaṃ` | | |

`tumhamhehi` is `tumha-amhehi` in sandhi: after the two personal pronouns `tumha` ("you") and `amha` ("I", "we"), whose forms occupy [§139 (245): Tumhamhākaṃ tayi mayi](#m139) to [§151 (250): Bahuvacanesu vo no](#m151) and to which the vutti's `tehi` ("after those") points back. `namākaṃ` is `naṃ ākaṃ`; `vacanassa` and `hoti` are supplied by the vutti. There is no option marker: after these two pronouns `naṃ` is always `ākaṃ`. As `naṃ` serves both the fourth and the sixth form (④⨂, ⑥⨂), one form `tumhākaṃ` does for both.

:::

> Tehi tumhaamhehi naṃvacanassa ākaṃ hoti.

> Tumhākaṃ, amhākaṃ.

> Namiti kimatthaṃ?

> Tumhehi, amhehi.

After those [two pronouns] `tumha` and `amha`, the inflection `naṃ` (④⨂ and ⑥⨂) becomes `ākaṃ`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️⑥⨂(tumha) | tumhākaṃ | your; to you (⨂) |
| ⚧️⑥⨂(amha) | amhākaṃ | our; to us |

|     | tumha + naṃ (⑥⨂)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | tumha + (naṃ→ākaṃ)     | §161 |
| →   | tumh + (~~a~~ + ākaṃ)  | §83  |
| →   | **tumhākaṃ**           |      |

The stem vowel is elided before the vowel-initial replacement by [§83 (67): Saralopo’ mādesa paccayādimhi saralope tu pakati](#m83).

> `yo amhākaṃ satthā` \
> [(9M 2.1: Cūḷasīhanādasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/9M/2/2.1#455) \
🚹①⨀(ya) ⚧️⑥⨂(amha) 🚹①⨀(satthu) \
who | our | teacher

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `naṃ` the rule applies to the `naṃ` inflection only; the `hi` of the third and fifth forms (③⨂, ⑤⨂) keeps its shape, the stem's `a` becoming `e` by [§101 (80): Suhisvakāro e](#m101).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️③⨂(tumha) | tumhehi | by you (⨂) |
| ⚧️③⨂(amha) | amhehi | by us |

:::

---

<a name="m162"></a>

### §162 (237): Vā yvappaṭhamo (Optionally `ākaṃ` for the `yo` that is not first) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| vā     | (tumhamhehi) | yo appaṭhamo | (ādeso) | (ākaṃ) | | |
| optionally | (after `tumha`, `amha`) | the `yo` that is not first (②⨂) | (replaced by) | (`ākaṃ`) | | |

`yvappaṭhamo` is `yo appaṭhamo` in sandhi, the `o` becoming `v` before a vowel by [§18 (20): Vamodudantānaṃ](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m18): the `yo` that is not `paṭhama` ("first"), that is, the `yo` of the second form ②⨂. `tumhamhehi` and `ākaṃ` continue from [§161 (244): Tumhamhehi namākaṃ](#m161). The option marker `vā` indicates this is an alternate to [§107 (69): Sabbayonīnamāe](#m107), by which the second `yo` becomes `e`: `tumhākaṃ passāmi` and `tumhe passāmi` are both good Pāli. The vutti also stretches this `vā` to admit `aṃ` and `ānaṃ` for the same `yo` (see the Additional note below).

:::

> Tehi tumhaamhehi yo appaṭhamo ākaṃhoti vā.

> Tumhākaṃ passāmi, tumhe passāmi vā.

> Amhākaṃ passasi, amhe passasi vā.

> Yoti kimatthaṃ?

> Tumhehi, amhehi.

> Appaṭhamoti kimatthaṃ?

> Gacchatha tumhe,

> gacchāma mayaṃ.

> Vātivikappanatthena yonaṃ aṃ ānaṃ honti.

> Tumhaṃ tumhānaṃ.

> Amhaṃ, amhānaṃ.

After `tumha` and `amha`, the `yo` that is not the first — the `yo` of the second form ②⨂ — optionally becomes `ākaṃ`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️②⨂(tumha) | tumhākaṃ passāmi | I see you (⨂) |
| ⚧️②⨂(tumha) | tumhe passāmi | I see you (⨂) (by §107) |
| ⚧️②⨂(amha) | amhākaṃ passasi | you see us |
| ⚧️②⨂(amha) | amhe passasi | you see us (by §107) |

|     | tumha + yo (②⨂)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | tumha + (yo→ākaṃ)     | §162 |
| →   | tumh + (~~a~~ + ākaṃ) | §83  |
| →   | **tumhākaṃ**          |      |

|     | tumha + yo (②⨂)    | rule |
| --- | ------------------ | ---- |
| →   | tumha + (yo→e)     | §107 |
| →   | tumh + (~~a~~ + e) | §83  |
| →   | **tumhe**          |      |

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `yo` the rule does not reach other inflections.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️③⨂(tumha) | tumhehi | by you (⨂) |
| ⚧️③⨂(amha) | amhehi | by us |

By saying `appaṭhamo` (not the first) the `yo` of the first form ①⨂ is excluded: there `tumha` takes `e` by [§164 (200): Sabbanāmakārate paṭhamo](#m164) and `amha` becomes `mayaṃ` by [§121 (233): Mayaṃ yomhi paṭhame](#m121).

> `gacchatha tumhe` \
▶️🤘⨂(gacchati) ⚧️①⨂(tumha) \
you go | you

> `gacchāma mayaṃ` \
▶️👆⨂(gacchati) ⚧️①⨂(amha) \
we go | we

:::

:::note[Additional]

Because `vā` has the sense of an open option (`vikappana`), the vutti lets the same `yo` also become `aṃ` and `ānaṃ`:

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️②⨂(tumha) | tumhaṃ | you (⨂) |
| ⚧️②⨂(tumha) | tumhānaṃ | you (⨂) |
| ⚧️②⨂(amha) | amhaṃ | us |
| ⚧️②⨂(amha) | amhānaṃ | us |

:::

---

<a name="m163"></a>

### §163 (240): Sassaṃ (`aṃ` for `sa` [after `tumha`, `amha`]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| (vā)   | (tumhamhehi) | sassa (vibhattissa) | (ādeso) | aṃ | | |
| (optionally) | (after `tumha`, `amha`) | `sa` inflection (④⨀, ⑥⨀) | (replaced by) | `aṃ` | | |

`sassaṃ` is `sassa aṃ`: `aṃ` for [the inflection] `sa`. `tumhamhehi` continues from [§161 (244): Tumhamhehi namākaṃ](#m161), and so does the `vā` of [§162 (237): Vā yvappaṭhamo](#m162) — the vutti says `hoti vā`. The option marker opens an alternative to the other replacements of `sa` after these pronouns: `tava`, `mama` by [§141 (241): Tava mama se](#m141), `tuyhaṃ`, `mayhaṃ` by [§142 (242): Tuyhaṃ mayhañca](#m142) and, for `amha`, `mamaṃ` by [§120 (243): Amhassa mamaṃ savibhattissa se](#m120). `tumhaṃ`, `tava` and `tuyhaṃ` are all good Pāli for ④⨀ and ⑥⨀.

:::

> Tehi tumhaamhehi[^30] sassa vibhattissa aṃ ādeso hoti vā.

> Tumhaṃ dīyate, tava dīyate.

> Tumhaṃ pariggaho, tava pariggaho.

> Amhaṃ dīyate, mama dīyate.

> Amhaṃ pariggaho, mama pariggaho.

> Sasseti kimatthaṃ?

> Tumhesu,

> amhesu.

After `tumha` and `amha`, the inflection `sa` (④⨀ and ⑥⨀) is optionally replaced by `aṃ`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️④⨀(tumha) | tumhaṃ dīyate | it is given to you |
| ⚧️④⨀(tumha) | tava dīyate | it is given to you (by §141) |
| ⚧️⑥⨀(tumha) | tumhaṃ pariggaho | your possession |
| ⚧️⑥⨀(tumha) | tava pariggaho | your possession (by §141) |
| ⚧️④⨀(amha) | amhaṃ dīyate | it is given to me |
| ⚧️④⨀(amha) | mama dīyate | it is given to me (by §141) |
| ⚧️⑥⨀(amha) | amhaṃ pariggaho | my possession |
| ⚧️⑥⨀(amha) | mama pariggaho | my possession (by §141) |

|     | tumha + sa (④⨀)     | rule |
| --- | ------------------- | ---- |
| →   | tumha + (sa→aṃ)     | §163 |
| →   | tumh + (~~a~~ + aṃ) | §83  |
| →   | **tumhaṃ**          |      |

> `tumhaṃ yevassa tena antarāyo` \
> [(6D 1.1: Paribbājakakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/6D/1/1.1#6) \
⚧️④⨀(tumha) 🔼(eva) ⏯🤟⨀(atthi) 🚹③⨀(ta) 🚹①⨀(antarāya) \
for you | just | would be | by that | an obstacle

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `sassa` (of `sa`) the rule does not reach other inflections.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️⑦⨂(tumha) | tumhesu | in you (⨂) |
| ⚧️⑦⨂(amha) | amhesu | in us |

:::

[^30]: CST4 prints `humhaamhehi`.

---

<a name="m164"></a>

### §164 (200): Sabbanāmakārate paṭhamo (`e` for the first `yo` after a pronoun in `a`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | sabbanāma-akārato | (yo) paṭhamo | (ettamāpajjate) | e | | |
|        | after the final `a` of a pronoun | the first `yo` (①⨂) | (becomes) | `e` | | |

`sabbanāmakārate` resolves as `sabbanāma-akārato e`: after the `a` [that ends] a `sabbanāma` (pronoun), [the `yo` of] the first [form] becomes `e`; `yo` is supplied by the vutti. There is no option marker, and the rule overrides [§107 (69): Sabbayonīnamāe](#m107), which gives `ā` for the first `yo` of a noun (`purisā`): a pronoun has `sabbe`, never `sabbā`, in the masculine ①⨂. Since §107 already gives `e` for the second `yo`, the first and second forms of a pronoun coincide (`te` ①⨂ and ②⨂).

:::

> Sabbesaṃ sabbanāmānaṃ akārato yo paṭhamo ettamāpajjate.

> Sabbe, ye, te, ke, tumhe, amhe, ime.

> Sabbanāmāti kimatthaṃ?

> Devā, asurā, nāgā, gandhabbā, manussā.

> Akāratoti kimatthaṃ?

> Amū purisā tiṭṭhanti.

> Yoti kimatthaṃ?

> Sabbo, yo, so, ko, ayaṃ.

> Paṭhamaggahaṇaṃ uttarasuttatthaṃ.

After the final `a` of every pronoun (`sabbanāma`), the `yo` of the first form (①⨂) becomes `e`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(sabba) | sabbe | all |
| 🚹①⨂(ya) | ye | who, whoever |
| 🚹①⨂(ta) | te | they, those |
| 🚹①⨂(ka) | ke | who? |
| ⚧️①⨂(tumha) | tumhe | you (⨂) |
| ⚧️①⨂(amha) | amhe | we |
| 🚹①⨂(ima) | ime | these |

In use the first form of `amha` is `mayaṃ` by [§121 (233): Mayaṃ yomhi paṭhame](#m121), which replaces the pronoun together with its `yo`; `amhe` remains the second form ②⨂ (§107, §162).

|     | sabba + yo (①⨂)    | rule |
| --- | ------------------ | ---- |
| →   | sabba + (yo→e)     | §164 |
| →   | sabb + (~~a~~ + e) | §83  |
| →   | **sabbe**          |      |

> `sabbe saṅkhārā aniccā` \
> [(9M 4.5: Cūḷasaccakasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/9M/4/4.5#1201) \
🚹①⨂(sabba) 🚹①⨂(saṅkhāra) 🚹①⨂(anicca) \
all | conditioned things | impermanent

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `sabbanāmānaṃ` (of pronouns) the rule does not apply to nouns, which take `ā` by [§107 (69): Sabbayonīnamāe](#m107).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(deva) | devā | gods |
| 🚹①⨂(asura) | asurā | asuras |
| 🚹①⨂(nāga) | nāgā | nāgas |
| 🚹①⨂(gandhabba) | gandhabbā | gandhabbas |
| 🚹①⨂(manussa) | manussā | humans |

By saying `akārato` (after `a`) the rule does not apply to a pronoun ending in another vowel: after `amu` the `yo` is elided by [§118 (146): Ghapato ca yonaṃ lopo](#m118) and the `u` lengthened by [§88 (147): Yosu katanikāralopesu dīghaṃ](#m88).

> `amū purisā tiṭṭhanti` \
🚹①⨂(amu) 🚹①⨂(purisa) ▶️🤟⨂(tiṭṭhati) \
those yonder | men | stand

By saying `yo` the rule does not apply to the singular `si`, which becomes `o` by [§104 (66): So](#m104), or is replaced with the pronoun by [§172 (218): Anapuṃsakassā yaṃ simhī](#m172).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(sabba) | sabbo | all, every |
| 🚹①⨀(ya) | yo | who, whoever |
| 🚹①⨀(ta) | so | he, that |
| 🚹①⨀(ka) | ko | who? |
| 🚹①⨀(ima) | ayaṃ | this |

:::

:::note[Additional]

`Paṭhamaggahaṇaṃ uttarasuttatthaṃ`: the word `paṭhama` ("first") is stated for the sake of the next sutta, [§165 (208): Dvandaṭṭhā vā](#m165), where the option must be confined to the first `yo` — for this rule itself it changes nothing, since the second `yo` becomes `e` anyway by §107.

The pronouns (`sabbanāma`), as the Rūpasiddhi lists them, are `sabba`, `katara`, `katama`, `ubhaya`, `itara`, `añña`, `aññatara`, `aññatama`, `pubba`, `para`, `apara`, `dakkhiṇa`, `uttara`, `adhara`, `ya`, `ta`, `eta`, `ima`, `amu`, `kiṃ`, `eka`, `ubha`, `dvi`, `ti`, `catu`, `tumha` and `amha`.

:::

---

<a name="m165"></a>

### §165 (208): Dvandaṭṭhā vā (Optionally [`e`] after [a pronoun] standing in a `dvanda`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| vā     | (sabbanāma-akārato) dvandaṭṭhā | (yo paṭhamo) | (ettamāpajjate) | (e) | | |
| optionally | after the `a` of a pronoun standing in a `dvanda` compound | (the first `yo`) | (becomes) | (`e`) | | |

`dvandaṭṭhā` is `dvanda-ṭhā`, "[after one] standing in a `dvanda`" — a co-ordinative compound ("X and Y"). Everything else (`sabbanāma`, `akārato`, `yo paṭhamo`, `ettamāpajjate`) continues from [§164 (200): Sabbanāmakārate paṭhamo](#m164), to which the vutti's `tasmā` points back. The option marker `vā` indicates this is an alternate to [§107 (69): Sabbayonīnamāe](#m107): a `dvanda` of pronouns may take the pronominal `e` (`katarakatame`) or the nominal `ā` (`katarakatamā`) in the first form, where a single pronoun takes `e` only.

:::

> Tasmā sabbanāma’kārato dvandaṭṭhā yo paṭhamo ettamāpajjate vā.

> Katarakatame, katarakatamā vā.

> Sabbanāmāti kimatthaṃ?

> Devāsuranāgagandhabbamanussā[^31].

> Dvandaṭṭhāti kimatthaṃ?

> Te, sabbe.

After the final `a` of a pronoun standing in a `dvanda` compound, the `yo` of the first form optionally becomes `e`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(katara + katama) | katarakatame | which and which? |
| 🚹①⨂(katara + katama) | katarakatamā | which and which? (by §107) |

|     | katarakatama + yo (①⨂)    | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | katarakatama + (yo→e)     | §165 |
| →   | katarakatam + (~~a~~ + e) | §83  |
| →   | **katarakatame**          |      |

|     | katarakatama + yo (①⨂)    | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | katarakatama + (yo→ā)     | §107 |
| →   | katarakatam + (~~a~~ + ā) | §83  |
| →   | **katarakatamā**          |      |

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `sabbanāma` (pronoun) a `dvanda` of nouns is excluded: it takes `ā` by §107 only.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(deva + asura + nāga + gandhabba + manussa) | devāsuranāgagandhabbamanussā | gods, asuras, nāgas, gandhabbas and humans |

By saying `dvandaṭṭhā` (standing in a `dvanda`) a pronoun on its own is excluded: for it §164 applies without option, and `tā`, `sabbā` are not masculine first forms.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(ta) | te | they |
| 🚹①⨂(sabba) | sabbe | all |

:::

[^31]: CST4 prints the compound with a space, `Devāsuranāga gandhabbamanussā`.

---

<a name="m166"></a>

### §166 (209): Nāññaṃ sabbanāmikaṃ (No other pronominal operation [in a `dvanda`]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | (dvandaṭṭhe) | sabbanāmikaṃ (kāriyaṃ) | na (hoti) | ~~aññaṃ~~ | | |
|        | (in a `dvanda` compound) | pronominal (operation) | does not (take place) | ~~any other~~ | | |

`nāññaṃ` is `na aññaṃ` ("no other") and `sabbanāmikaṃ [kāriyaṃ]` "pronominal [operation]"; `dvandaṭṭhe` ("in a `dvanda`") continues from [§165 (208): Dvandaṭṭhā vā](#m165). The sutta is a prohibition (`paṭisedha`) and has no option marker: apart from the optional `e` of §165, a pronoun that is a member of a `dvanda` is inflected like an ordinary noun. Its `naṃ` stays `ānaṃ` with the lengthening of [§89 (87): Sunaṃhisu ca](#m89) instead of the `saṃ`, `sānaṃ` of [§168 (203): Sabbato naṃ saṃ sānaṃ](#m168), and its `smā`, `smiṃ`, `sa` are free to take the `ā`, `e`, `āya` that [§110 (201): Tayo neva ca sabbanāmehi](#m110) forbids to a pronoun standing alone.

:::

> Sabbanāmikānaṃ dvandaṭṭhe nāññaṃ kāriyaṃ hoti,

> Pubbāparānaṃ, pubbuttarānaṃ, adharuttarānaṃ.

For pronouns standing in a `dvanda` compound, no other pronominal operation takes place.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑥⨂(pubba + apara) | pubbāparānaṃ | of the former and the latter |
| 🚹⑥⨂(pubba + uttara) | pubbuttarānaṃ | of the eastern and the northern |
| 🚹⑥⨂(adhara + uttara) | adharuttarānaṃ | of the lower and the upper |

|     | pubbāpara + naṃ (⑥⨂)      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pubbāpar + (a→ā) + naṃ    | §89  |
| →   | **pubbāparānaṃ**          |      |

The pronominal `saṃ`, `sānaṃ` of §168 (which would give `pubbāparesaṃ`) does not apply. The compounds themselves are joined by sandhi: `pubba + apara` with [§12 (13): Sarā sare lopaṃ](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m12) and [§15 (17): Dīghaṃ](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m15), `pubba + uttara` with §12.

:::

---

<a name="m167"></a>

### §167 (210): Bahubbīhimhi ca (And in a `bahubbīhi`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     | bahubbīhimhi (samāse) | (sabbanāmikaṃ kāriyaṃ) | (na hoti) | (~~aññaṃ~~) | | |
| and    | in a `bahubbīhi` compound | (pronominal operation) | (does not take place) | (~~any other~~) | | |

By saying `ca` this rule is an extension of [§166 (209): Nāññaṃ sabbanāmikaṃ](#m166) from the `dvanda` to the `bahubbīhi` — a compound that denotes something outside its own members ("having X as Y"): a pronoun that ends a `bahubbīhi` is inflected as a noun. The vutti's answer to `Ceti kimatthaṃ?` gives the `ca` a second job, that of marking the prohibition as not absolute: in the compass-point compounds `dakkhiṇapubbā` ("south-east") and `uttarapubbā` ("north-east") the pronominal treatment does occur (`dakkhiṇapubbassaṃ` like `sabbassaṃ`). For that class the `ca` does the work of a `vā`, much as Pāṇini forbids the pronominal inflection in a `bahuvrīhi` in general but allows it optionally in a compound of directions.

:::

> Bahubbīhimhi ca samāse sabbanāmavidhānañca nāññaṃ kāriyaṃ hoti.

> Piyapubbāya, piyapubbānaṃ, piyapubbe, piyapubbassa.

> Ceti kimatthaṃ?

> Sabbanāmavidhānaṃ hoti.

> dakkhiṇapubbassaṃ[^32], dakkhiṇapubbassā, uttarapubbassaṃ, uttarapubbassā.

And in a `bahubbīhi` compound too, no other pronominal operation takes place.

:::tip[Examples]

`piyapubba` ("whose former [spouse] was dear") is the stock example of a `bahubbīhi` ending in the pronoun `pubba`.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺⑥⨀(piya + pubba) | piyapubbāya | of the woman whose former [spouse] was dear |
| 🚺⑥⨂(piya + pubba) | piyapubbānaṃ | of the women whose former [spouses] were dear |
| 🚹⑦⨀(piya + pubba) | piyapubbe | in the man whose former [spouse] was dear |
| 🚹⑥⨀(piya + pubba) | piyapubbassa | of the man whose former [spouse] was dear |

`piyapubbāya` has the `āya` of [§111 (179): Ghato nādīnaṃ](#m111) where a pronoun would have `ssā` ([§179 (204): Ghapato smiṃ sānaṃ saṃsā](#m179)); `piyapubbānaṃ` has `ānaṃ` where a pronoun would have `sānaṃ` (§168); `piyapubbe` has the `e` for `smiṃ` of [§108 (90): Smāsmiṃnaṃ vā](#m108), which [§110 (201): Tayo neva ca sabbanāmehi](#m110) denies to a pronoun.

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `ca` the vutti signals that the pronominal treatment is not excluded altogether: in a `bahubbīhi` that names a direction it does take place.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺⑦⨀(dakkhiṇa + pubba) | dakkhiṇapubbassaṃ | in the south-east |
| 🚺⑥⨀(dakkhiṇa + pubba) | dakkhiṇapubbassā | of the south-east |
| 🚺⑦⨀(uttara + pubba) | uttarapubbassaṃ | in the north-east |
| 🚺⑥⨀(uttara + pubba) | uttarapubbassā | of the north-east |

|     | dakkhiṇapubbā + smiṃ (⑦⨀)         | rule |
| --- | --------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | dakkhiṇapubbā + (smiṃ→saṃ)        | §179 |
| →   | dakkhiṇapubb + (ā→a) + saṃ        | §66  |
| →   | dakkhiṇapubba + (s) + saṃ         | §62  |
| →   | **dakkhiṇapubbassaṃ**             |      |

:::

[^32]: CST4 prints `dakkhiṇa pubbassaṃ`.

---

<a name="m168"></a>

### §168 (203): Sabbato naṃ saṃ sānaṃ (`saṃ`, `sānaṃ` for `naṃ` after any pronoun) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | sabbato (sabbanāmato) | naṃ (vacanassa) | (ādesā) | saṃ \| sānaṃ | | |
|        | after every pronoun | `naṃ` inflection (④⨂, ⑥⨂) | (replaced by) | `saṃ` \| `sānaṃ` | | |

`sabbato` ("after all") is glossed by the vutti as "after every pronoun" (`sabbanāmato`), the topic of §164–§167. There is no option marker: after a pronoun `naṃ` never keeps its shape, and the two replacements `saṃ` and `sānaṃ` are both always available (`tesaṃ`, `tesānaṃ`). In the masculine and neuter the stem's `a` becomes `e` before `naṃ` by [§102 (202): Sabbanāmānaṃ naṃmhi ca](#m102); in the feminine the `ā` stays; `amu` is lengthened by [§89 (87): Sunaṃhisu ca](#m89). A pronoun inside a `dvanda` or `bahubbīhi` is exempt ([§166 (209): Nāññaṃ sabbanāmikaṃ](#m166), [§167 (210): Bahubbīhimhi ca](#m167)), and `tumha`, `amha` take `ākaṃ` instead ([§161 (244): Tumhamhehi namākaṃ](#m161)).

:::

> Sabbato sabbanāmato naṃvacanassa saṃsānaṃiccete ādesā honti.

> Sabbesaṃ, sabbesānaṃ, sabbāsaṃ, sabbāsānaṃ.

> Yesaṃ, yesānaṃ, yāsaṃ, yāsānaṃ.

> Tesaṃ, tesānaṃ, tāsaṃ, tāsānaṃ.

> Kesaṃ, kesānaṃ, kāsaṃ, kāsānaṃ.

> Imesaṃ, imesānaṃ, imāsaṃ, imāsānaṃ.

> Amūsaṃ, amūsānaṃ.

> Namiti kimatthaṃ?

> Sabbassa, yassa, tassa, kassa.

> Evaṃ sabbattha.

After every pronoun (`sabbanāma`), the inflection `naṃ` (④⨂ and ⑥⨂) is replaced by `saṃ` or `sānaṃ`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑥⨂(sabba) | sabbesaṃ | of all |
| 🚹⑥⨂(sabba) | sabbesānaṃ | of all |
| 🚺⑥⨂(sabba) | sabbāsaṃ | of all (women) |
| 🚺⑥⨂(sabba) | sabbāsānaṃ | of all (women) |
| 🚹⑥⨂(ya) | yesaṃ | of whom |
| 🚹⑥⨂(ya) | yesānaṃ | of whom |
| 🚺⑥⨂(ya) | yāsaṃ | of whom (women) |
| 🚺⑥⨂(ya) | yāsānaṃ | of whom (women) |
| 🚹⑥⨂(ta) | tesaṃ | of those |
| 🚹⑥⨂(ta) | tesānaṃ | of those |
| 🚺⑥⨂(ta) | tāsaṃ | of those (women) |
| 🚺⑥⨂(ta) | tāsānaṃ | of those (women) |
| 🚹⑥⨂(ka) | kesaṃ | of whom? |
| 🚹⑥⨂(ka) | kesānaṃ | of whom? |
| 🚺⑥⨂(ka) | kāsaṃ | of which (women)? |
| 🚺⑥⨂(ka) | kāsānaṃ | of which (women)? |
| 🚹⑥⨂(ima) | imesaṃ | of these |
| 🚹⑥⨂(ima) | imesānaṃ | of these |
| 🚺⑥⨂(ima) | imāsaṃ | of these (women) |
| 🚺⑥⨂(ima) | imāsānaṃ | of these (women) |
| 🚹⑥⨂(amu) | amūsaṃ | of those yonder |
| 🚹⑥⨂(amu) | amūsānaṃ | of those yonder |

|     | sabba + naṃ (⑥⨂)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sabba + (naṃ→saṃ)      | §168 |
| →   | sabb + (a→e) + saṃ     | §102 |
| →   | **sabbesaṃ**           |      |

|     | sabbā + naṃ (⑥⨂)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sabbā + (naṃ→sānaṃ)    | §168 |
| →   | **sabbāsānaṃ**         |      |

|     | amu + naṃ (⑥⨂)         | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | amu + (naṃ→saṃ)        | §168 |
| →   | am + (u→ū) + saṃ       | §89  |
| →   | **amūsaṃ**             |      |

> `tesaṃ vūpasamo sukho` \
> [(7D 3.36: Parinibbutakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/7D/3/3.36#493) \
🚹⑥⨂(ta) 🚹①⨀(vūpasama) 🚹①⨀(sukha) \
of them | the stilling | happiness

> `tāsaṃ itthīnaṃ` \
> [(1V 1.2.3: Duṭṭhullavācāsikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.2/1.2.3#1133) \
🚺⑥⨂(ta) 🚺⑥⨂(itthī) \
of those | women

> `amūsaṃ bhedāya` \
> [(6D 1.2.1: Cūḷasīla)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/6D/1/1.2/1.2.1#14) \
🚹⑥⨂(amu) 🚹④⨀(bheda) \
of those | for the division

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `naṃ` the rule does not reach other inflections: the singular `sa` keeps its shape, with the `s` inserted by [§61 (86): Sāgamo se](#m61). `Evaṃ sabbattha` — "and so throughout": the same holds for every pronoun.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑥⨀(sabba) | sabbassa | of all |
| 🚹⑥⨀(ya) | yassa | of whom |
| 🚹⑥⨀(ta) | tassa | of that |
| 🚹⑥⨀(ka) | kassa | of whom? |

:::

---

<a name="m169"></a>

### §169 (117): Rājassa rāju sunaṃhisu ca (`rāju` for `rāja` before `su`, `naṃ`, `hi`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     |        | rājassa (sabbassa) | (ādeso) | rāju | sunaṃhisu | |
| and    |        | the whole of `rāja` | (replaced by) | `rāju` | `su`, `naṃ`, `hi` inflections | |

`sabbassa` ("the whole") is supplied by the vutti: the entire stem `rāja` becomes `rāju`, whose `u` is then lengthened before `su`, `naṃ`, `hi` by [§89 (87): Sunaṃhisu ca](#m89) and whose `hi` may become `bhi` by [§99 (81): Smāhismiṃnaṃ mhābhimhi vā](#m99). The vutti glosses the `ca` as `avadhāraṇa`: it limits the rule, marking that the replacement is not invariable, so that the regular forms `rājesu`, `rājānaṃ`, `rājehi` — with `e` by [§101 (80): Suhisvakāro e](#m101) and `ā` by §89 — remain in use; here the `ca` does the work of a `vā`, and the Bālāvatāra reads it so (`cakārena kvaci na`, "by `ca`, sometimes not"). The sutta belongs with the other `rāja` rules, [§135 (118): Rājassa rañño rājino se](#m135) to [§138 (121): Smiṃmhi raññe rājini](#m138), which the Rūpasiddhi numbers together with it (116–121).

:::

> Sabbasseva rājasaddassa rājuādeso hoti sunaṃhiiccetesu.

> Rājūsu, rājūnaṃ, rājūhi, rājūbhi.

> Sunaṃhisūti kimatthaṃ?

> Rājā.

> Caggahaṇamavadhāraṇatthaṃ.

> Rājesu, rājānaṃ, rājehi rājebhi.

The whole of the word `rāja` ("king") is replaced by `rāju` before the inflections `su`, `naṃ` and `hi`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨂(rāja) | rājūsu | among kings |
| 🚹⑥⨂(rāja) | rājūnaṃ | of kings |
| 🚹③⨂(rāja) | rājūhi | by kings |
| 🚹③⨂(rāja) | rājūbhi | by kings (by §99) |

|     | rāja + su (⑦⨂)        | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (rāja→rāju) + su      | §169 |
| →   | rāj + (u→ū) + su      | §89  |
| →   | **rājūsu**            |      |

> `rājūnaṃ ādinnadaṇḍānaṃ ādinnasatthānaṃ` \
> [(3V 10.2: Dīghāvuvatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/10/10.2#1976) \
🚹⑥⨂(rāja) 🚹⑥⨂(ādinnadaṇḍa) 🚹⑥⨂(ādinnasattha) \
of kings | who have taken up the rod | who have taken up the sword

> `rājūhi vā corehi vā` \
> [(1V 1.4.1.6: Aññātakaviññattisikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.4/1.4.1/1.4.1.6#1991) \
🚹③⨂(rāja) 🔼(vā) 🚹③⨂(cora) 🔼(vā) \
by kings | or | by thieves | or

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `sunaṃhisu` (before `su`, `naṃ`, `hi`) the rule does not reach other inflections: before `si` the stem takes `ā` by [§189 (113): Syā ca](#m189).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(rāja) | rājā | king |

By saying `ca` the applicability of the rule is limited (`avadhāraṇa`): the forms without `rāju` also occur.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨂(rāja) | rājesu | among kings |
| 🚹⑥⨂(rāja) | rājānaṃ | of kings |
| 🚹③⨂(rāja) | rājehi | by kings |
| 🚹③⨂(rāja) | rājebhi | by kings |

:::

---

<a name="m170"></a>

### §170 (220): Sabbassimasse vā (Optionally `e` for the whole of `ima` [before `su`, `naṃ`, `hi`]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| vā     |        | sabbassa imassa | (ādeso) | e | (sunaṃhisu) | |
| optionally |    | the whole of `ima` | (replaced by) | `e` | (`su`, `naṃ`, `hi` inflections) | |

`sabbassimasse` is `sabbassa imassa e`: `e` for the whole of `ima`. `sunaṃhisu` continues from [§169 (117): Rājassa rāju sunaṃhisu ca](#m169). The option marker `vā` indicates this is an alternate to the regular forms, in which only the final `a` becomes `e`: `imesu`, `imehi` by [§101 (80): Suhisvakāro e](#m101), `imesaṃ` by [§102 (202): Sabbanāmānaṃ naṃmhi ca](#m102) with [§168 (203): Sabbato naṃ saṃ sānaṃ](#m168). When the whole stem is `e`, the inflection follows it directly: `esu`, `esaṃ`, `ehi`.

:::

> Sabbasseva imasaddassa ekāro hoti vā sunaṃhiiccetesu.

> Esu, imesu,

> esaṃ, imesaṃ,

> ehi, ebhi,

> imehi, imebhi.

> Imasseti kimatthaṃ?

> Etesu, etesaṃ, etehi, etebhi.

The whole of the word `ima` ("this") optionally becomes `e` before the inflections `su`, `naṃ` and `hi`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨂(ima) | esu | in these |
| 🚹⑦⨂(ima) | imesu | in these (by §101) |
| 🚹⑥⨂(ima) | esaṃ | of these |
| 🚹⑥⨂(ima) | imesaṃ | of these (by §102, §168) |
| 🚹③⨂(ima) | ehi | by these |
| 🚹③⨂(ima) | ebhi | by these (by §99) |
| 🚹③⨂(ima) | imehi | by these (by §101) |
| 🚹③⨂(ima) | imebhi | by these (by §101, §99) |

|     | ima + su (⑦⨂)    | rule |
| --- | ---------------- | ---- |
| →   | (ima→e) + su     | §170 |
| →   | **esu**          |      |

|     | ima + su (⑦⨂)      | rule |
| --- | ------------------ | ---- |
| →   | im + (a→e) + su    | §101 |
| →   | **imesu**          |      |

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `imassa` (of `ima`) the rule does not apply to `eta`, which keeps its stem.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨂(eta) | etesu | in these |
| 🚹⑥⨂(eta) | etesaṃ | of these |
| 🚹③⨂(eta) | etehi | by these |
| 🚹③⨂(eta) | etebhi | by these |

:::

---

<a name="m171"></a>

### §171 (219): Animi nāmhi ca (`ana`, `imi` for `ima` before `nā`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     |        | imasaddassa (sabbassa) | (ādesā) | ana \| imi | nāmhi | |
| and    |        | the whole of `ima` | (replaced by) | `ana` \| `imi` | `nā` inflection (③⨀) | |

`animi` is `ana imi`, two replacements for the whole of `ima`. `sabbassa` continues from [§170 (220): Sabbassimasse vā](#m170), and the `ca` draws it in (`anukaḍḍhana`): it is the whole word, not just its final, that is replaced. Both results are always available and there is no `vā`. After `ana` the `nā` becomes `ena` by [§103 (79): Ato nena](#m103), giving `anena`; after `imi` it keeps its shape, giving `iminā`.

:::

> Imasaddassa sabbasseva ana imiiccete ādesā honti nāmhi vibhattimhi.

> Anena dhammadānena, sukhitā hotu sā pajā.

> Iminā buddhapūjena, patvāna amataṃ padaṃ.

> Nāmhīti kimatthaṃ?

> Imesu, imesaṃ, imehi, imebhi.

The whole of the word `ima` is replaced by `ana` or `imi` before the inflection `nā` (③⨀).

:::tip[Examples]

The vutti quotes two dedication verses (not from the Tipiṭaka):

> `anena dhammadānena, sukhitā hotu sā pajā` \
🚹③⨀(ima) 🚻③⨀(dhammadāna) 🚺①⨀(sukhita) ⏯🤟⨀(hoti) 🚺①⨀(ta) 🚺①⨀(pajā) \
by this | gift of the Dhamma | happy | may be | that | people

> `iminā buddhapūjena, patvāna amataṃ padaṃ` \
🚹③⨀(ima) 🚹③⨀(buddhapūja) 🔽(patvāna) 🚻②⨀(amata) 🚻②⨀(pada) \
by this | veneration of the Buddha | having reached | the deathless | state

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(ima) | anena | by this |
| 🚹③⨀(ima) | iminā | by this |

|     | ima + nā (③⨀)        | rule |
| --- | -------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (ima→ana) + nā       | §171 |
| →   | ana + (nā→ena)       | §103 |
| →   | an + (~~a~~ + ena)   | §83  |
| →   | **anena**            |      |

|     | ima + nā (③⨀)        | rule |
| --- | -------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (ima→imi) + nā       | §171 |
| →   | **iminā**            |      |

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `nāmhi` (before `nā`) the rule does not reach other inflections.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨂(ima) | imesu | in these |
| 🚹⑥⨂(ima) | imesaṃ | of these |
| 🚹③⨂(ima) | imehi | by these |
| 🚹③⨂(ima) | imebhi | by these |

:::

---

<a name="m172"></a>

### §172 (218): Anapuṃsakassā yaṃ simhī (`ayaṃ` for the non-neuter [`ima`] before `si`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
