|        |        | (imasaddassa sabbassa) anapuṃsakassa | (ādeso) | ayaṃ | simhi | |
|        |        | the whole of `ima` when not neuter | (replaced by) | `ayaṃ` | `si` inflection (①⨀) | |

`anapuṃsakassāyaṃ` is `anapuṃsakassa ayaṃ`: `ayaṃ` for [`ima`] that is not neuter (`na-puṃsaka`). `imasaddassa sabbassa` continues from [§171 (219): Animi nāmhi ca](#m171). There is no option marker: the masculine and feminine first form singular of `ima` is always `ayaṃ`, after which the `si` is elided by [§220 (74): Sesato lopaṃ gasipi](#m220). The neuter has `idaṃ` by [§129 (222): Imassidamaṃsisu napuṃsake](#m129).

:::

> Imasaddassa sabbasseva anapuṃsakassa ayaṃādeso hoti simhi vibhattimhi.

> Ayaṃ puriso,

> ayaṃ itthī.

> Anapuṃsakasseti kimatthaṃ?

> Idaṃ cittaṃ tiṭṭhati.

> Simhīti kimatthaṃ?

> Imaṃ purisaṃ passasi tvaṃ.

The whole of the word `ima`, when it is not neuter, is replaced by `ayaṃ` before the inflection `si` (①⨀).

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(ima) | ayaṃ puriso | this man |
| 🚺①⨀(ima) | ayaṃ itthī | this woman |

|     | ima + si (①⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (ima→ayaṃ) + si     | §172 |
| →   | ayaṃ + ~~si~~       | §220 |
| →   | **ayaṃ**            |      |

> `pariyuṭṭhito ayaṃ puriso` \
> [(2V 1.5.5.3: Sabhojanasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/2V/1/1.5/1.5.5/1.5.5.3#894) \
🚹①⨀(pariyuṭṭhita) 🚹①⨀(ima) 🚹①⨀(purisa) \
obsessed | this | man

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `anapuṃsakassa` (of the non-neuter) the neuter is excluded; it takes `idaṃ` by §129.

> `pubbe kho me idaṃ cittaṃ parittaṃ ahosi` \
> [(17A10 5.1.9: Karajakāyasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/17A10/5/5.1/5.1.9#1490) \
🔼(pubbe) 🔼(kho) ⚧️⑥⨀(amha) 🚻①⨀(ima) 🚻①⨀(citta) 🚻①⨀(paritta) ⏮🤟⨀(hoti) \
formerly | indeed | my | this | mind | limited | was

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻①⨀(ima) | idaṃ cittaṃ tiṭṭhati | this mind stands |

By saying `simhi` (before `si`) other inflections are excluded.

> `imaṃ purisaṃ passasi tvaṃ` \
🚹②⨀(ima) 🚹②⨀(purisa) ▶️🤘⨀(passati) ⚧️①⨀(tumha) \
this | man | see | you

:::

---

<a name="m173"></a>

### §173 (223): Amussa mo saṃ (`s` for the `m` of `amu` [before `si`]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| (vā)   |        | amussa (anapuṃsakassa) mo | (āpajjate) | saṃ | (simhi) | |
| (optionally) |  | the `m` of `amu` when not neuter | (becomes) | `s` | (`si` inflection) | |

`mo` is the `m` [of `amu`] and `saṃ` the letter `s` it becomes. `anapuṃsakassa` and `simhi` continue from [§172 (218): Anapuṃsakassā yaṃ simhī](#m172), and the `vā` of [§170 (220): Sabbassimasse vā](#m170) is still in force — the vutti says `vā`. The option is between `asu` and the form without the change, `amu`, which the vutti shows as `amuko` with the augment `ka` of [§178 (224): Sabbato ko](#m178). In either case the `si` is elided by [§220 (74): Sesato lopaṃ gasipi](#m220) after `asu`, or becomes `o` after `amuka` by [§104 (66): So](#m104). The neuter is `aduṃ` by [§130 (225): Amussāduṃ](#m130).

:::

> Amusaddassa anapuṃsakassa makāro sakāramāpajjate vā simhi vibhattimhi.

> Asu rājā,

> asu itthī,

> amuko rājā,

> amukā itthī.

> Anapuṃsakasseti kimatthaṃ?

> Aduṃ pupphaṃ virocati.

> Amusseti kimatthaṃ?

> Ayaṃ puriso tiṭṭhati.

> Simhīti kimatthaṃ?

> Amuṃ[^33] purisaṃ passasi.

The `m` of the word `amu` ("that yonder", "such and such"), when it is not neuter, optionally becomes `s` before the inflection `si` (①⨀).

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(amu) | asu rājā | that king |
| 🚺①⨀(amu) | asu itthī | that woman |
| 🚹①⨀(amu) | amuko rājā | that king (with `ka`, §178) |
| 🚺①⨀(amu) | amukā itthī | that woman (with `ka`, §178) |

|     | amu + si (①⨀)         | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | a + (m→s) + u + si    | §173 |
| →   | asu + ~~si~~          | §220 |
| →   | **asu**               |      |

> `asu amutra upapanno` \
> [(7D 5.1: Nātikiyādibyākaraṇa)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/7D/5/5.1#596) \
🚹①⨀(amu) 🔼(amutra) 🚹①⨀(upapanna) \
that one | there | reborn

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `anapuṃsakassa` (of the non-neuter) the neuter is excluded; it takes `aduṃ` by §130.

> `aduṃ pupphaṃ virocati` \
🚻①⨀(amu) 🚻①⨀(puppha) ▶️🤟⨀(virocati) \
that | flower | shines

By saying `amussa` (of `amu`) other pronouns are excluded: `ima` takes `ayaṃ` by §172.[^84]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(ima) | ayaṃ puriso tiṭṭhati | this man stands |

By saying `simhi` (before `si`) other inflections are excluded.

> `amuṃ purisaṃ passasi` \
🚹②⨀(amu) 🚹②⨀(purisa) ▶️🤘⨀(passati) \
that | man | you see

:::

[^33]: CST4 prints `Amhaṃ`; the counter-example needs `amu` before an inflection other than `si`, and `amuṃ` is its ②⨀, as in the vutti of §130 (`Amuṃ rājānaṃ passasi`).

[^84]: U Nandisena (2005) prints and translates both questions with `amha` ("Why 'of `amha`'?" … `amhaṃ purisaṃ passasi`), reproducing a corrupt reading without comment. The sutta names `amu`, not `amha`; `ayaṃ puriso` answers `amussa` with another pronoun, `ima`; and `amhaṃ purisaṃ passasi` makes no sense, whereas `amuṃ` is the ②⨀ of `amu` that the vutti of §130 uses.

---

<a name="m174"></a>

### §174 (211): Etatesaṃ to (`s` for the `t` of `eta`, `ta` [before `si`]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        |        | etatesaṃ (anapuṃsakānaṃ) to | (āpajjate) | (saṃ) | (simhi) | |
|        |        | the `t` of `eta`, `ta` when not neuter | (becomes) | (`s`) | (`si` inflection) | |

`etatesaṃ` is `eta-ta-etesaṃ`, "of these two, `eta` and `ta`", and `to` is their `t`. What it becomes — `saṃ`, the letter `s` — continues from [§173 (223): Amussa mo saṃ](#m173), as do `anapuṃsakānaṃ` and `simhi` from §172–§173. The `vā` does not continue: the vutti states the rule without it, and `so`, `sā`, `eso`, `esā` are the only first-form singulars of these two pronouns. After `sa` the `si` becomes `o` by [§104 (66): So](#m104); after the feminine `sā` it is elided by [§220 (74): Sesato lopaṃ gasipi](#m220).

:::

> Eta taiccetesaṃ anapuṃsakānaṃ takāro sakāramāpajjate simhi vibhattimhi.

> Eso puriso,

> esā itthī,

> so puriso,

> sā itthī.

> Etatesamiti kimatthaṃ?

> Itaro puriso,

> itarā itthī.

> Anapuṃsakānamiti kimatthaṃ?

> Etaṃ cittaṃ, etaṃ rūpaṃ.

> Taṃ cittaṃ, taṃ rūpaṃ.

The `t` of `eta` ("this") and `ta` ("that"), when they are not neuter, becomes `s` before the inflection `si` (①⨀).

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(eta) | eso puriso | this man |
| 🚺①⨀(eta) | esā itthī | this woman |
| 🚹①⨀(ta) | so puriso | that man |
| 🚺①⨀(ta) | sā itthī | that woman |

|     | ta + si (①⨀)         | rule |
| --- | -------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (t→s) + a + si       | §174 |
| →   | sa + (si→o)          | §104 |
| →   | s + (~~a~~ + o)      | §83  |
| →   | **so**               |      |

|     | tā + si (①⨀)         | rule |
| --- | -------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (t→s) + ā + si       | §174 |
| →   | sā + ~~si~~          | §220 |
| →   | **sā**               |      |

> `atha kho sā itthī sallakkhetvā` \
> [(2V 1.5.5.3: Sabhojanasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/2V/1/1.5/1.5.5/1.5.5.3#894) \
🔼(atha kho) 🚺①⨀(ta) 🚺①⨀(itthī) 🔽(sallakkhetvā) \
then | that | woman | having considered

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `etatesaṃ` (of `eta` and `ta`) other pronouns are excluded.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(itara) | itaro puriso | the other man |
| 🚺①⨀(itara) | itarā itthī | the other woman |

By saying `anapuṃsakānaṃ` (of the non-neuter) the neuter is excluded; its `si` becomes `aṃ` by [§125 (198): Aṃ napuṃsake](#m125).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻①⨀(eta) | etaṃ cittaṃ | this mind |
| 🚻①⨀(eta) | etaṃ rūpaṃ | this form |
| 🚻①⨀(ta) | taṃ cittaṃ | that mind |
| 🚻①⨀(ta) | taṃ rūpaṃ | that form |

:::

---

<a name="m175"></a>

### §175 (212): Tassa vā nattaṃ sabbattha (Optionally `n` for the `t` of `ta` everywhere) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| vā     |        | tassa (sabbanāmassa) takārassa | (hoti) | nattaṃ | | sabbattha (liṅgesu) |
| optionally |    | the `t` of the pronoun `ta` | (becomes) | `n` | | everywhere, in all genders |

`nattaṃ` is `na-ttaṃ`, "the state of being `n`"; `sabbattha` ("everywhere") is glossed by the vutti as "in all genders" (`liṅgesu`), and the examples show that it holds for all inflections too. `tassa` here is the pronoun `ta` itself ("of `ta`"), not the anaphoric "of that". The option marker `vā` indicates this is an alternate to the unchanged forms with `t`: `naṃ` and `taṃ`, `ne` and `te` are both good Pāli. The change does not reach the first form singular, where [§174 (211): Etatesaṃ to](#m174) has already made the `t` an `s` (`so`, `sā`).

:::

> Tassa sabbanāmassa takārassa nattaṃ hoti vā sabbattha liṅgesu.

> Nāya, tāya,

> naṃ, taṃ,

> ne, te,

> nesu, tesu,

> namhi, tamhi,

> nāhi, tāhi,

> nābhi, tābhi.

The `t` of the pronoun `ta` optionally becomes `n`, everywhere — in all genders [and all inflections].

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺③⨀(ta) | nāya | by her |
| 🚺③⨀(ta) | tāya | by her |
| 🚹②⨀(ta) | naṃ | him |
| 🚹②⨀(ta) | taṃ | him |
| 🚹①⨂(ta) | ne | they |
| 🚹①⨂(ta) | te | they |
| 🚹⑦⨂(ta) | nesu | in them |
| 🚹⑦⨂(ta) | tesu | in them |
| 🚹⑦⨀(ta) | namhi | in him |
| 🚹⑦⨀(ta) | tamhi | in him |
| 🚺③⨂(ta) | nāhi | by them (women) |
| 🚺③⨂(ta) | tāhi | by them (women) |
| 🚺③⨂(ta) | nābhi | by them (women) |
| 🚺③⨂(ta) | tābhi | by them (women) |

|     | ta + yo (①⨂)        | rule |
| --- | ------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (t→n) + a + yo      | §175 |
| →   | na + (yo→e)         | §164 |
| →   | n + (~~a~~ + e)     | §83  |
| →   | **ne**              |      |

> `tato naṃ dukkhamanveti` \
> [(18Dh 1.1: Cakkhupālattheravatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Dh/1/1.1#2) \
🔼(tato) 🚹②⨀(ta) 🚻①⨀(dukkha) ▶️🤟⨀(anveti) \
then | him | suffering | follows

> `detha nesaṃ cīvarānī` \
> [(1V 1.4.1.6: Aññātakaviññattisikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.4/1.4.1/1.4.1.6#1982) \
▶️🤘⨂(deti) 🚹④⨂(ta) 🚻②⨂(cīvara) \
give | to them | robes

The second citation shows the change in the `naṃ` inflection, replaced by `saṃ` under [§168 (203): Sabbato naṃ saṃ sānaṃ](#m168).

:::

---

<a name="m176"></a>

### §176 (213): Sasmāsmiṃsaṃsāsvattaṃ (`a` [for `ta`] before `sa`, `smā`, `smiṃ`, `saṃ`, `sā`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| (vā)   |        | (tassa sabbanāmassa takārassa) sabbassa | (hoti) | attaṃ | sasmāsmiṃsaṃsāsu | (sabbattha liṅgesu) |
| (optionally) |  | the whole `ta` — its `t` with its vowel | (becomes) | `a` | `sa`, `smā`, `smiṃ`, `saṃ`, `sā` | (in all genders) |

`sasmāsmiṃsaṃsāsvattaṃ` is `sa-smā-smiṃ-saṃ-sāsu attaṃ`: "before `sa`, `smā`, `smiṃ`, `saṃ` and `sā`, the state of being `a`". `tassa sabbanāmassa takārassa`, the `vā` and `sabbattha liṅgesu` all continue from [§175 (212): Tassa vā nattaṃ sabbattha](#m175); `sabbassa` ("the whole") is the vutti's addition — the `t` together with its vowel, the syllable `ta` (in the feminine `tā`), is replaced by `a`. The option marker `vā` indicates this is an alternate to the forms that keep `ta`: `assa` beside `tassa`, `asmiṃ` beside `tasmiṃ`. The `a`-forms do not take `mhā`, `mhi` for `smā`, `smiṃ`, which [§100 (214): Na timehi katākārehi](#m100) forbids. `saṃ` and `sā` are the feminine replacements of `smiṃ` and `sa` by [§179 (204): Ghapato smiṃ sānaṃ saṃsā](#m179), with the `s` of [§62 (206): Saṃsāsvekavacanesu ca](#m62) inserted before them.

:::

> Tassa sabbanāmassa takārassa sabbasseva attaṃ hoti vā sasmāsmiṃ saṃsāiccetesu sabbattha liṅgesu.

> Assa, tassa,

> asmā, tasmā,

> asmiṃ, tasmiṃ,

> assaṃ, tassaṃ,

> assā, tassā.

> Takārasseti kimatthaṃ?

> Amussaṃ, amussā.

> Etesvīti kimatthaṃ?

> Nesu, tesu.

The whole of the `t` [syllable] of the pronoun `ta` optionally becomes `a` before the inflections `sa`, `smā`, `smiṃ` and the replacements `saṃ`, `sā`, in all genders.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(ta) | assa | to him; of him |
| 🚹④⨀(ta) | tassa | to him; of him |
| 🚹⑤⨀(ta) | asmā | from that |
| 🚹⑤⨀(ta) | tasmā | from that |
| 🚹⑦⨀(ta) | asmiṃ | in that |
| 🚹⑦⨀(ta) | tasmiṃ | in that |
| 🚺⑦⨀(ta) | assaṃ | in her |
| 🚺⑦⨀(ta) | tassaṃ | in her |
| 🚺⑥⨀(ta) | assā | of her |
| 🚺⑥⨀(ta) | tassā | of her |

|     | ta + sa (④⨀)        | rule |
| --- | ------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (ta→a) + sa         | §176 |
| →   | a + (s) + sa        | §61  |
| →   | **assa**            |      |

|     | tā + smiṃ (⑦⨀)        | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | tā + (smiṃ→saṃ)       | §179 |
| →   | t + (ā→a) + saṃ       | §66  |
| →   | ta + (s) + saṃ        | §62  |
| →   | (ta→a) + ssaṃ         | §176 |
| →   | **assaṃ**             |      |

> `na cassa hoti āpatti` \
> [(5V 20.3: Pācittiyādipañhā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/5V/20/20.3#2370) \
🔼(na) 🔼(ca) 🚹④⨀(ta) ▶️🤟⨀(hoti) 🚺①⨀(āpatti) \
not | and | for him | there is | an offence

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `takārassa` (of the `t`) a pronoun without a `t` is excluded: `amu` keeps its stem before `saṃ`, `sā`.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺⑦⨀(amu) | amussaṃ | in her yonder |
| 🚺⑥⨀(amu) | amussā | of her yonder |

By saying `etesu` (before these) other inflections are excluded: before `su` the `t` stays, or becomes `n` by §175.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨂(ta) | nesu | in them |
| 🚹⑦⨂(ta) | tesu | in them |

:::

---

<a name="m177"></a>

### §177 (221): Imasaddassa ca (And for the word `ima`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     |        | imasaddassa (sabbassa) | (hoti) | (attaṃ) | (sasmāsmiṃsaṃsāsu) | (sabbattha liṅgesu) |
| and    |        | the whole of `ima` | (becomes) | (`a`) | (`sa`, `smā`, `smiṃ`, `saṃ`, `sā`) | (in all genders) |

By saying `ca` this rule is an extension of [§176 (213): Sasmāsmiṃsaṃsāsvattaṃ](#m176) to the pronoun `ima`: everything but the stem — `sabbassa attaṃ hoti vā sasmāsmiṃsaṃsāsu sabbattha liṅgesu` — is carried over, the `vā` included. The option is between `assa` and `imassa`, `asmiṃ` and `imasmiṃ`; the feminine `imissaṃ`, `imissā` have `i` for `ā` by [§63 (217): Etimāsami](#m63). As with `ta`, the `a`-forms take no `mhā`, `mhi` ([§100 (214): Na timehi katākārehi](#m100)). `assa`, `asmā`, `asmiṃ`, `assaṃ`, `assā` thus serve both `ta` and `ima`; the Tipiṭaka's `asmiṃ loke` is "in this world".

:::

> Imasaddassa ca sabbasseva attaṃ hoti vā sasmāsmiṃ saṃ sāiccetesu sabbattha liṅgesu.

> Assa, imassa,

> asmā, imasmā,

> asmiṃ, imasmiṃ,

> assaṃ, imissaṃ,

> assā, imissā.

> Imasaddasseti kimatthaṃ?

> Etissaṃ, etissā.

And the whole of the word `ima` optionally becomes `a` before the inflections `sa`, `smā`, `smiṃ` and the replacements `saṃ`, `sā`, in all genders.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(ima) | assa | to this; of this |
| 🚹④⨀(ima) | imassa | to this; of this |
| 🚹⑤⨀(ima) | asmā | from this |
| 🚹⑤⨀(ima) | imasmā | from this |
| 🚹⑦⨀(ima) | asmiṃ | in this |
| 🚹⑦⨀(ima) | imasmiṃ | in this |
| 🚺⑦⨀(ima) | assaṃ | in this (woman) |
| 🚺⑦⨀(ima) | imissaṃ | in this (woman) |
| 🚺⑥⨀(ima) | assā | of this (woman) |
| 🚺⑥⨀(ima) | imissā | of this (woman) |

|     | imā + smiṃ (⑦⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | imā + (smiṃ→saṃ)      | §179 |
| →   | im + (ā→i) + saṃ      | §63  |
| →   | imi + (s) + saṃ       | §62  |
| →   | **imissaṃ**           |      |

|     | imā + smiṃ (⑦⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | imā + (smiṃ→saṃ)      | §179 |
| →   | (imā→a) + (s) + saṃ   | §177, §62 |
| →   | **assaṃ**             |      |

> `asmiṃ loke paramhi ca` \
> [(10M 5.8: Vāseṭṭhasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/10M/5/5.8#1443) \
🚹⑦⨀(ima) 🚹⑦⨀(loka) 🚹⑦⨀(para) 🔼(ca) \
in this | world | in the other | and

> `asmā lokā paramhā ca` \
> [(8D 8.10: Ālasyassa cha ādīnavā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/8D/8/8.10#595) \
🚹⑤⨀(ima) 🚹⑤⨀(loka) 🚹⑤⨀(para) 🔼(ca) \
from this | world | from the other | and

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `imasaddassa` (of the word `ima`) other pronouns are excluded: `eta` keeps its stem, with the `i` of §63.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺⑦⨀(eta) | etissaṃ | in this (woman) |
| 🚺⑥⨀(eta) | etissā | of this (woman) |

:::

---

<a name="m178"></a>

### §178 (224): Sabbato ko (Optionally `ka` after any pronoun [before `si`]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| (vā)   | sabbato (sabbanāmato) | | (kakāra)āgamo | ko | (simhi) | |
| (optionally) | after every pronoun | | augment | `k` | (`si` inflection) | |

`ko` names the augment `k` (`kakārāgama`), which comes with its vowel `a`: `sabbako` is `sabba` + `ka` + the `o` that `si` becomes by [§104 (66): So](#m104). `sabbato` is glossed as `sabbanāmato`, as in [§168 (203): Sabbato naṃ saṃ sānaṃ](#m168). The `vā` of §176–§177 and `simhi` are supplied by the vutti, whose `Vāti kimatthaṃ?` confirms the option: `sabbako` beside `sabbo`, `amuko` beside `amu`. The vutti further takes the repeated `sabbato` ("after all") as licence for the augment after words that are not pronouns (`hīnako`, `potako`), where the `ka` is in effect a suffix of diminution or contempt.

:::

> Sabbato sabbanāmato kakārāgamo hoti vā simhi vibhattimhi.

> Sabbako, yako, sako, amuko, asuko.

> Vāti kimatthaṃ?

> Sabbo, yo, so, ko.

> Sabbanāmatoti kimatthaṃ?

> Puriso.

> Puna sabbatoggahaṇena aññasmāpi kakārāgamo hoti.

> hīnako,

> potako.

After every pronoun (`sabbanāma`), the augment `k` is optionally inserted before the inflection `si` (①⨀).

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(sabba) | sabbako | all, every |
| 🚹①⨀(ya) | yako | who, whoever |
| 🚹①⨀(ta) | sako | he, that one |
| 🚹①⨀(amu) | amuko | that one yonder |
| 🚹①⨀(amu) | asuko | that one yonder (with `s`, §173) |

|     | amu + si (①⨀)         | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | amu + (ka) + si       | §178 |
| →   | amuka + (si→o)        | §104 |
| →   | amuk + (~~a~~ + o)    | §83  |
| →   | **amuko**             |      |

> `asuko bhikkhu paṭhamassa jhānassa lābhī` \
> [(1V 1.1.4: Catutthapārājikasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.1/1.1.4#537) \
🚹①⨀(amu) 🚹①⨀(bhikkhu) 🚻⑥⨀(paṭhama) 🚻⑥⨀(jhāna) 🚹①⨀(lābhī) \
such-and-such | bhikkhu | of the first | jhāna | an obtainer

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `vā` the rule is optional: the forms without the augment stand beside it.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(sabba) | sabbo | all, every |
| 🚹①⨀(ya) | yo | who, whoever |
| 🚹①⨀(ta) | so | he, that |
| 🚹①⨀(ka) | ko | who? |

By saying `sabbanāmato` (after a pronoun) nouns are excluded.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(purisa) | puriso | man |

:::

:::note[Additional]

`Puna sabbatoggahaṇena aññasmāpi kakārāgamo hoti`: by the repeated mention of `sabbato` ("after all"), the augment `k` also occurs after other [words].

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(hīna) | hīnako | a low one |
| 🚹①⨀(pota) | potako | a young one |

:::

<a name="m179"></a>

### §179 (204): Ghapato smiṃ sānaṃ saṃsā (`saṃ`, `sā` for `smiṃ`, `sa` after `gha`, `pa` pronouns) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| (vā)   | (sabbato sabbanāmato) ghapato | smiṃ sānaṃ | (ādesā) | saṃ \| sā | | yathāsaṅkhyaṃ |
| (optionally) | after a pronoun that is `gha` or `pa` | `smiṃ`, `sa` inflections | (replaced by) | `saṃ` \| `sā` | | respectively |

The sutta's first word is `ghapato`, "after a `gha` or a `pa`", as the vutti's `ghapasaññato`, the CST's own sutta list and the Rūpasiddhi have it; the CST's vutti section prints `Yapato`, a misprint this chapter does not follow. A `gha` is a feminine stem in `ā` ([§60 (177): Ā gho](#m60)) and a `pa` a feminine stem in `i`, `ī`, `u` or `ū` ([§59 (182): Te itthikhyā po](#m59)); `sabbato sabbanāmato` continues from [§178 (224): Sabbato ko](#m178), so the rule concerns the feminine pronouns `sabbā`, `imā`, `amu` and their like. `smiṃsānaṃ saṃsā … yathāsaṅkhyaṃ`: `smiṃ` becomes `saṃ` and `sa` becomes `sā`, respectively; an `s` is inserted before both by [§62 (206): Saṃsāsvekavacanesu ca](#m62), the `ā` of a `gha` is shortened by [§66 (205): Gho rassaṃ](#m66), and `etā`, `imā` take `i` by [§63 (217): Etimāsami](#m63). The `vā` (in the vutti) indicates this is an alternate to the ordinary feminine replacements: `yaṃ` for `smiṃ` by [§216 (180): Ghapato smiṃ yaṃ vā](#m216) (`sabbāyaṃ`, `imāyaṃ`, `amuyaṃ`) and `āya`, `yā` for `sa` by [§111 (179): Ghato nādīnaṃ](#m111) and [§112 (183): Pato yā](#m112) (`sabbāya`, `imāya`, `amuyā`).

:::

> Sabbato sabbanāmato ghapasaññato smiṃsaiccetesaṃ saṃsā ādesā honti vā yathāsaṅkhyaṃ.

> Sabbassaṃ, sabbassā, sabbāyaṃ, sabbāya,

> imissaṃ, imissā, imāyaṃ, imāya,

> amussaṃ, amussā, amuyaṃ, amuyā.

> Sabbanāmatoti kimatthaṃ?

> Itthiyaṃ, itthiyā.

> Smiṃsānamiti kimatthaṃ?

> Amuyo.

After every pronoun that is `gha` or `pa` [a feminine stem], the inflections `smiṃ` (⑦⨀) and `sa` (④⨀, ⑥⨀) are optionally replaced by `saṃ` and `sā` respectively.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺⑦⨀(sabba) | sabbassaṃ | in all |
| 🚺⑥⨀(sabba) | sabbassā | of all |
| 🚺⑦⨀(sabba) | sabbāyaṃ | in all (by §216) |
| 🚺⑥⨀(sabba) | sabbāya | of all (by §111) |
| 🚺⑦⨀(ima) | imissaṃ | in this |
| 🚺⑥⨀(ima) | imissā | of this |
| 🚺⑦⨀(ima) | imāyaṃ | in this (by §216) |
| 🚺⑥⨀(ima) | imāya | of this (by §111) |
| 🚺⑦⨀(amu) | amussaṃ | in that yonder |
| 🚺⑥⨀(amu) | amussā | of that yonder |
| 🚺⑦⨀(amu) | amuyaṃ | in that yonder (by §216) |
| 🚺⑥⨀(amu) | amuyā | of that yonder (by §112) |

|     | sabbā + smiṃ (⑦⨀)      | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sabbā + (smiṃ→saṃ)     | §179 |
| →   | sabb + (ā→a) + saṃ     | §66  |
| →   | sabba + (s) + saṃ      | §62  |
| →   | **sabbassaṃ**          |      |

|     | amu + sa (⑥⨀)          | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | amu + (sa→sā)          | §179 |
| →   | amu + (s) + sā         | §62  |
| →   | **amussā**             |      |

> `imissā makkaṭiyā` \
> [(1V 1.1.1.2: Makkaṭīvatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.1/1.1.1/1.1.1.2#75) \
🚺⑥⨀(ima) 🚺⑥⨀(makkaṭī) \
of this | female monkey

> `amussā itthiyā sāratto` \
> [(11M 1.1: Devadahasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/11M/1/1.1#35) \
🚺⑥⨀(amu) 🚺⑥⨀(itthī) 🚹①⨀(sāratta) \
of that | woman | enamoured

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `sabbanāmato` (after a pronoun) feminine nouns are excluded: they take only `yaṃ` (§216) and `yā` (§112).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺⑦⨀(itthī) | itthiyaṃ | in a woman |
| 🚺⑥⨀(itthī) | itthiyā | of a woman |

By saying `smiṃsānaṃ` (of `smiṃ` and `sa`) other inflections are excluded.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺①⨂(amu) | amuyo | those (women) yonder |

:::

<a name="m180"></a>

### §180 (207): Netāhi smimāya yā (No `āya`, `yā` for `smiṃ` after these) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | etāhi (sabbanāmehi ghapasaññehi) | smiṃ (vacanassa) | na (ādesā) | ~~āya \| yā~~ | | |
|        | after these pronouns that are `gha` or `pa` | `smiṃ` inflection (⑦⨀) | not (replaced by) | ~~`āya` \| `yā`~~ | | |

`netāhi smimāyayā` is `na etāhi smiṃ āya yā`: after these, no `āya` or `yā` for `smiṃ`. `etāhi` ("after these") are the `gha` and `pa` pronouns of [§179 (204): Ghapato smiṃ sānaṃ saṃsā](#m179). The rule is a prohibition (`paṭisedha`) without option marker: the ⑦⨀ of a feminine pronoun is `saṃ` (§179) or `yaṃ` ([§216 (180): Ghapato smiṃ yaṃ vā](#m216)) only — `etissaṃ`, `etāyaṃ` — never the `āya` that [§111 (179): Ghato nādīnaṃ](#m111) gives a noun in `ā` (`kaññāya`, "in the girl") nor the `yā` that [§112 (183): Pato yā](#m112) gives a noun in `i`, `u` (`rattiyā`). The fourth and sixth forms are untouched: `tāya`, `etāya`, `imāya` for `sa` remain, as the counter-example `tāya itthiyā mukhaṃ` shows.

:::

> Etehi sabbanāmehi ghapasaññehi smiṃvacanassa neva āya yāādesā honti.

> Etissaṃ, etāyaṃ,

> imissaṃ, imāyaṃ,

> amussaṃ, amuyaṃ.

> Smiṃnti kimatthaṃ?

> Tāya itthiyā mukhaṃ.

> Etāhīti kimatthaṃ?

> Kaññāya, vīṇāya, gaṅgāya, kapālikāya.

After these pronouns that are `gha` or `pa`, the inflection `smiṃ` (⑦⨀) is not replaced by `āya` or `yā`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺⑦⨀(eta) | etissaṃ | in this |
| 🚺⑦⨀(eta) | etāyaṃ | in this (by §216) |
| 🚺⑦⨀(ima) | imissaṃ | in this |
| 🚺⑦⨀(ima) | imāyaṃ | in this (by §216) |
| 🚺⑦⨀(amu) | amussaṃ | in that yonder |
| 🚺⑦⨀(amu) | amuyaṃ | in that yonder (by §216) |

> `imissaṃ parisāyaṃ` \
> [(6D 4.5: Brāhmaṇapaññatti)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/6D/4/4.5#485) \
🚺⑦⨀(ima) 🚺⑦⨀(parisā) \
in this | assembly

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `smiṃ` the prohibition is confined to that inflection: `āya` for `sa` (§111) is allowed.

> `tāya itthiyā mukhaṃ` \
🚺⑥⨀(ta) 🚺⑥⨀(itthī) 🚻①⨀(mukha) \
of that | woman | the face

By saying `etāhi` (after these) nouns are excluded: a noun in `ā` takes `āya` for `smiṃ` by §111.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺⑦⨀(kaññā) | kaññāya | in the girl |
| 🚺⑦⨀(vīṇā) | vīṇāya | in the lute |
| 🚺⑦⨀(gaṅgā) | gaṅgāya | in the Ganges |
| 🚺⑦⨀(kapālikā) | kapālikāya | in the small bowl |

:::

<a name="m181"></a>

### §181 (95): Manogaṇādito smiṃnānamiā (`i`, `ā` for `smiṃ`, `nā` after the `mano`-group) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| vā     | manogaṇādito | smiṃ nānaṃ | (ādesā) | i \| ā | | yathāsaṅkhyaṃ |
| optionally | after the `mano`-group and the like | `smiṃ`, `nā` inflections | (replaced by) | `i` \| `ā` | | respectively |

`manogaṇādito` is "after the `mano`-group (`manogaṇa`) and the like (`ādi`)": the old `-as` stems `mana` (mind), `vaca` (speech), `sira` (head), `sara` (lake), `tapa` (austerity), `vaya` (age), `yasa` (fame), `teja` (fire), `ura` (chest), `thāma` (strength), `tama` (darkness), `ceta` (mind), `oja` (vigour), `paya` (milk), `raja` (dust), `aya` (iron) and the like, which the Bālāvatāra lists in a verse (¶137). `smiṃnānamiā` is `smiṃ-nānaṃ i ā`, and the vutti adds `yathāsaṅkhyaṃ` ("respectively"): `smiṃ` becomes `i`, `nā` becomes `ā`. Both replacements begin with a vowel, so the `s` of [§184 (96): Sa sare vāgamo](#m184) is inserted before them: `manasi`, `manasā`. The option marker `vā` indicates this is an alternate to the regular `a`-stem forms `manasmiṃ` (or `manamhi` by [§99 (81): Smāhismiṃnaṃ mhābhimhi vā](#m99), `mane` by [§108 (90): Smāsmiṃnaṃ vā](#m108)) and `manena` ([§103 (79): Ato nena](#m103)). The vutti's `tasmā` is a connective ("so, after that group"), not a word carried over.

:::

> Tasmā manogaṇādito smiṃnāiccetesaṃ ikāraākārādesā honti vā yathāsaṅkhyaṃ.

> Manasi, manasmiṃ[^34],

> sirasi, sirasmiṃ,

> manasā, manena,

> vacasā, vacena,

> sirasā, sirena,

> sarasā, sarena,

> tapasā, tapena,

> vayasā, vayena,

> yasasā, yasena,

> tejasā, tejena,

> urasā, urena,

> thāmasā, thāmena.

> Smiṃnānamiti kimatthaṃ?

> Mano, siro, tamo, tapo, tejo.

> Ādiggahaṇena aññāsmāpi smiṃnānaṃ ikāraākārādesā honti.

> bilasi, bilasā, padasi, padasā.

After the words of the `mano`-group and the like, the inflections `smiṃ` (⑦⨀) and `nā` (③⨀) are optionally replaced by `i` and `ā` respectively.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨀(mana) | manasi | in the mind |
| 🚹⑦⨀(mana) | manasmiṃ | in the mind (rule not applied) |
| 🚹⑦⨀(sira) | sirasi | on the head |
| 🚹⑦⨀(sira) | sirasmiṃ | on the head (rule not applied) |
| 🚹③⨀(mana) | manasā | with the mind |
| 🚹③⨀(mana) | manena | with the mind (by §103) |
| 🚹③⨀(vaca) | vacasā | with speech |
| 🚹③⨀(vaca) | vacena | with speech (by §103) |
| 🚹③⨀(sira) | sirasā | with the head |
| 🚹③⨀(sira) | sirena | with the head (by §103) |
| 🚹③⨀(sara) | sarasā | by the lake |
| 🚹③⨀(sara) | sarena | by the lake (by §103) |
| 🚹③⨀(tapa) | tapasā | by austerity |
| 🚹③⨀(tapa) | tapena | by austerity (by §103) |
| 🚹③⨀(vaya) | vayasā | by age |
| 🚹③⨀(vaya) | vayena | by age (by §103) |
| 🚹③⨀(yasa) | yasasā | by fame |
| 🚹③⨀(yasa) | yasena | by fame (by §103) |
| 🚹③⨀(teja) | tejasā | by fire |
| 🚹③⨀(teja) | tejena | by fire (by §103) |
| 🚹③⨀(ura) | urasā | with the chest |
| 🚹③⨀(ura) | urena | with the chest (by §103) |
| 🚹③⨀(thāma) | thāmasā | with strength |
| 🚹③⨀(thāma) | thāmena | with strength (by §103) |

|     | mana + smiṃ (⑦⨀)      | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | mana + (smiṃ→i)       | §181 |
| →   | mana + (s) + i        | §184 |
| →   | **manasi**            |      |

|     | mana + nā (③⨀)        | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | mana + (nā→ā)         | §181 |
| →   | mana + (s) + ā        | §184 |
| →   | **manasā**            |      |

> `sādhukaṃ manasi karotha` \
> [(6D 6.2.4: Dvepabbajitavatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/6D/6/6.2/6.2.4#619) \
🔼(sādhukaṃ) 🚹⑦⨀(mana) ▶️🤘⨂(karoti) \
well | in mind | make

> `manasā ce paduṭṭhena` \
> [(18Dh 1.1: Cakkhupālattheravatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Dh/1/1.1#2) \
🚹③⨀(mana) 🔼(ce) 🚹③⨀(paduṭṭha) \
with a mind | if | corrupted

> `paṭhamena vayasā` \
> [(6D 4.3: Buddhaguṇakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/6D/4/4.3#446) \
🚹③⨀(paṭhama) 🚹③⨀(vaya) \
in the first | stage of life

> `uttamaṅge sirasmiṃ` \
> [(4V 10.1.2: Aṭṭhagarudhamma)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/4V/10/10.1/10.1.2#1989) \
🚻⑦⨀(uttamaṅga) 🚹⑦⨀(sira) \
on the highest limb | on the head

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `smiṃnānaṃ` (of `smiṃ` and `nā`) other inflections are excluded: before `si` these words have the `si` elided by [§220 (74): Sesato lopaṃ gasipi](#m220) and their final made `o` by [§183 (48): Etesamo lope](#m183).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(mana) | mano | mind |
| 🚹①⨀(sira) | siro | head |
| 🚹①⨀(tama) | tamo | darkness |
| 🚹①⨀(tapa) | tapo | austerity |
| 🚹①⨀(teja) | tejo | fire |

:::

:::note[Additional]

`Ādiggahaṇena aññāsmāpi smiṃnānaṃ ikāraākārādesā honti`: by the word `ādi` ("and the like"), the replacements `i` and `ā` for `smiṃ` and `nā` occur after other words too.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻⑦⨀(bila) | bilasi | in the hole |
| 🚻③⨀(bila) | bilasā | by the hole |
| 🚻⑦⨀(pada) | padasi | on foot |
| 🚻③⨀(pada) | padasā | on foot |

> `padasā nessāmīti` \
> [(1V 1.1.2: Dutiyapārājikasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.1/1.1.2#248) \
🚻③⨀(pada) ⏯👆⨀(neti) 🔼(iti) \
on foot | I will carry off | thus

:::

[^34]: CST4 prints `manusmiṃ`.

<a name="m182"></a>

### §182 (97): Sassa co (`o` for `sa` [after the `mano`-group]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     | (manogaṇādito) | sassa | (hoti) | o | | |
| and    | (after the `mano`-group) | `sa` inflection (④⨀, ⑥⨀) | (becomes) | `o` | | |

`co` is `ca o`. `manogaṇādito` continues from [§181 (95): Manogaṇādito smiṃnānamiā](#m181), and the `ca` adds `sa` to the inflections replaced there, so that `manaso` stands beside `manasi` and `manasā`; the `s` is again the augment of [§184 (96): Sa sare vāgamo](#m184). The sutta has no `vā` of its own and the vutti states it without one; the Bālāvatāra glosses its `ca` further as giving `ā` for `smā` as well (`manasā`, "from the mind"). The regular `manassa` ([§61 (86): Sāgamo se](#m61)) is nonetheless also found in the Tipiṭaka, so the `vā` of §181 is best taken to continue.

:::

> Tasmā manogaṇādito sassa ca okāro hoti.

> Manaso, thāmaso, tapaso.

And after the words of the `mano`-group, the inflection `sa` (④⨀, ⑥⨀) becomes `o`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(mana) | manaso | to the mind |
| 🚹⑥⨀(thāma) | thāmaso | of strength |
| 🚹⑥⨀(tapa) | tapaso | of austerity |

|     | mana + sa (④⨀)        | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | mana + (sa→o)         | §182 |
| →   | mana + (s) + o        | §184 |
| →   | **manaso**            |      |

> `manaso piyā hadayagāminiyo` \
> [(8D 7.28--29: Lakkhaṇasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/8D/7/7.28--29#550) \
🚹④⨀(mana) 🚺①⨂(piyā) 🚺①⨂(hadayagāminī) \
to the mind | dear | going to the heart

> `thāmaso parāmāso na hoti` \
> [(13S3 1.1.5.4: Dutiyaaniccasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/13S3/1/1.1/1.1.5/1.1.5.4#202) \
🚹⑥⨀(thāma) 🚹①⨀(parāmāsa) 🔼(na) ▶️🤟⨀(hoti) \
of obstinacy | the grasping | not | is

:::

---

<a name="m183"></a>

### §183 (48): Etesamo lope (`o` for the final of these when the inflection is elided) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | etesaṃ (manogaṇādīnaṃ) | (anto) | (āpajjate) | o | | (vibhatti)lope (kate) |
|        | of these, the `mano`-group | (the final `a`) | (becomes) | `o` | | when the inflection has been elided |

`etesamo lope` is `etesaṃ o lope`: "of these, `o`, when [the inflection] is elided". `etesaṃ` ("of these") are the `mano`-group words of §181–§182; `anto` ("the final") and `vibhatti` are supplied by the vutti. The elision meant is that of the inflection inside a compound ([§317 (332): Tesaṃ vibhattiyo lopā ca](/kaccayana/4-samasakappa#m317), in the Samāsa chapter): `manasā pakataṃ` ("made by mind") becomes `manomayaṃ`. There is no option marker: the compound form is always `mano-`, `tejo-`, `tapo-`, `siro-`. The Rūpasiddhi places this rule in its Sandhi section (48) and uses it also for the first form singular `mano`, `tejo`, where the `si` has been elided by [§220 (74): Sesato lopaṃ gasipi](#m220); that is why the vutti of [§184 (96): Sa sare vāgamo](#m184) can say that no vowel-initial replacement follows in `mano`.

:::

> Etesaṃ manogaṇādīnaṃ anto ottamāpajjate vibhattilope kate.

> Manomayaṃ, ayomayaṃ, tejosamena, tapoguṇena, siroruhena.

> Ādiggahaṇaṃkimatthaṃ?

> Aññesamanto ottamāpajjate.

> Āposamena, vāyosamena.

> Lopeti kimatthaṃ?

> Padasā[^35], tapasā, yasasā, vacasā, manasā, evamaññepi yojetabbā.

The final [`a`] of these words of the `mano`-group becomes `o` when the inflection has been elided.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻①⨀(mana + maya) | manomayaṃ | mind-made |
| 🚻①⨀(aya + maya) | ayomayaṃ | made of iron |
| 🚹③⨀(teja + sama) | tejosamena | [with a mind] like fire |
| 🚹③⨀(tapa + guṇa) | tapoguṇena | by the quality of austerity |
| 🚹③⨀(sira + ruha) | siroruhena | by the hair |

|     | mana + [nā] + maya (+ si, 🚻)     | rule |
| --- | --------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | mana + ~~nā~~ + maya              | §317 |
| →   | man + (a→o) + maya                | §183 |
| →   | manomaya + (si→aṃ)                | §125 |
| →   | **manomayaṃ**                     |      |

The neuter `si` becomes `aṃ` by [§125 (198): Aṃ napuṃsake](#m125).

> `manomayaṃ kāyaṃ upapanno` \
> [(4V 7.1.2: Devadattavatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/4V/7/7.1/7.1.2#1504) \
🚹②⨀(manomaya) 🚹②⨀(kāya) 🚹①⨀(upapanna) \
mind-made | body | reborn into

> `tejosamena cetasā viharāmi` \
> [(17A9 1.2.1: Sīhanādasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/17A9/1/1.2/1.2.1#92) \
🚻③⨀(tejosama) 🚻③⨀(ceta) ▶️👆⨀(viharati) \
like fire | with a mind | I dwell

> `kena tapoguṇena` \
> [(22J 14.1.8: Mahāmorajātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/22J/14/14.1/14.1.8#2708) \
🚹③⨀(ka) 🚹③⨀(tapoguṇa) \
by what | quality of austerity

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `ādi` ("and the like") the vutti extends the change to the final of other words too (`Aññesamanto ottamāpajjate`).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻③⨀(āpa + sama) | āposamena | [with a mind] like water |
| 🚻③⨀(vāya + sama) | vāyosamena | [with a mind] like wind |

> `āposamena cetasā viharāmi` \
> [(17A9 1.2.1: Sīhanādasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/17A9/1/1.2/1.2.1#91) \
🚻③⨀(āposama) 🚻③⨀(ceta) ▶️👆⨀(viharati) \
like water | with a mind | I dwell

By saying `lope` (when elided) the rule does not apply where the inflection is replaced rather than elided: in the third form the `nā` becomes `ā` ([§181 (95): Manogaṇādito smiṃnānamiā](#m181)) with the `s` of §184, and the final stays `a`. `Evamaññepi yojetabbā` — "others too are to be construed in the same way".

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻③⨀(pada) | padasā | on foot |
| 🚹③⨀(tapa) | tapasā | by austerity |
| 🚹③⨀(yasa) | yasasā | by fame |
| 🚹③⨀(vaca) | vacasā | with speech |
| 🚹③⨀(mana) | manasā | with the mind |

:::

[^35]: CST4 prints `Padayā`; `padasā` is the form the same vutti gives under §181 (`padasi, padasā`) and the one the Tipiṭaka attests.

---

<a name="m184"></a>

### §184 (96): Sa sare vāgamo (Optionally `s` inserted before a vowel) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| vā     | (eteheva manogaṇādīhi) | | āgamo | sa | sare (vibhattādese) | |
| optionally | after these same `mano`-group words | | augment | `s` | before a vowel that replaces an inflection | |

`sa sare vāgamo` is `sa sare vā āgamo`: "the augment `s`, optionally, before a vowel". The vutti confines the vowel to a `vibhattādesa`, a replacement of an inflection — the `i`, `ā` of [§181 (95): Manogaṇādito smiṃnānamiā](#m181) and the `o` of [§182 (97): Sassa co](#m182) — and the stems to the `mano`-group (`eteheva`, "after these very ones"). The option marker `vā` indicates this is an alternate to the forms without the augment: when no `s` is inserted the inflection takes its ordinary shape, `manena` by [§103 (79): Ato nena](#m103) rather than `manasā`, and these are what the vutti's `Vāti kimatthaṃ?` answers with. In `mano` the `si` is elided ([§220 (74): Sesato lopaṃ gasipi](#m220)) and the final made `o` by [§183 (48): Etesamo lope](#m183), so no replacement vowel follows and `sare` does not reach it. The vutti's last remark extends the augment, through `ādi`, to a following suffix (`paccaya`): `mānasika`, `vācasika` with the taddhita `ṇika`.

:::

> Eteheva manogaṇādīhi vibhattādese sare pare sakārāgamo hoti vā.

> Manasā, vacasā, manasi, vacasi.

> Vāti kimatthaṃ?

> Manena, tejena, vasena,

> Sareti kimatthaṃ?

> Mano, tejo, yaso.

> Puna ādiggahaṇena aññasmimpi paccaye pare sakārāgamo hoti.

> Mānasikaṃ, vācasikaṃ.

After these same words of the `mano`-group, when a vowel that replaces an inflection follows, the augment `s` is optionally inserted.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(mana) | manasā | with the mind |
| 🚹③⨀(vaca) | vacasā | with speech |
| 🚹⑦⨀(mana) | manasi | in the mind |
| 🚹⑦⨀(vaca) | vacasi | in speech |

|     | vaca + nā (③⨀)        | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vaca + (nā→ā)         | §181 |
| →   | vaca + (s) + ā        | §184 |
| →   | **vacasā**            |      |

> `vacasā paricitā manasā anupekkhitā` \
> [(2V 1.5.3.1: Ovādasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/2V/1/1.5/1.5.3/1.5.3.1#496) \
🚹③⨀(vaca) 🚹①⨂(paricita) 🚹③⨀(mana) 🚹①⨂(anupekkhita) \
by speech | rehearsed | by mind | examined

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `vā` the rule is optional: without the augment the `nā` becomes `ena` by §103.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(mana) | manena | with the mind |
| 🚹③⨀(teja) | tejena | by fire |
| 🚹③⨀(vasa) | vasena | by the power |

> `cittassa vasena vattāmi` \
> [(3V 10.4: Pācīnavaṃsadāyagamanakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/10/10.4#1994) \
🚻⑥⨀(citta) 🚹③⨀(vasa) ▶️👆⨀(vattati) \
of the mind | under the control | I proceed

By saying `sare` (before a vowel) the first form is excluded: its `si` is elided, not replaced, and the final becomes `o` by §183.[^85]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(mana) | mano | mind |
| 🚹①⨀(teja) | tejo | fire |
| 🚹①⨀(yasa) | yaso | fame |

:::

:::note[Additional]

`Puna ādiggahaṇena aññasmimpi paccaye pare sakārāgamo hoti`: by the repeated word `ādi`, the augment `s` also comes before another suffix — here the taddhita `ṇika`, with the first vowel strengthened.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻①⨀(mana + ṇika) | mānasikaṃ | mental |
| 🚻①⨀(vaca + ṇika) | vācasikaṃ | verbal |

> `āyasmā vācasikaṃ sikkhassū` \
> [(4V 9.8: Codakenapaccavekkhitabbadhamma)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/4V/9/9.8#1931) \
🚹①⨀(āyasmantu) 🚻②⨀(vācasika) ⏯🤘⨀(sikkhati) \
venerable one | verbal [conduct] | train in

:::

[^85]: U Nandisena (2005) holds this `Sareti kimatthaṃ?` and its answer to be an intrusion into the vutti, noting that the Nyāsa passes over it in silence. The sentence stands in the CST and is translated here; the explanation offered for it is this translation's, not the vutti's.

---

<a name="m185"></a>

### §185 (112): Santasaddassa so bhe bo cante (`sa` for `santa` before `bh`, with `b` inserted) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        |        | santasaddassa (sabbassa) | (ādeso) | so | (bhe) | |
| ca     |        |        | (bakāra)āgamo | bo | | ante |
|        |        | the whole of `santa` | (replaced by) | `sa` | (before `bh`) | |
| and    |        |        | augment | `b` | | at the end [of `sa`] |

`santa` is "good", "existing" — the present participle of `atthi` and the word for a good person; `sabbassa` ("the whole") is supplied by the vutti. `so` is the replacement `sa`, `bo` the augment `b`, `ante` "at the end [of `sa`]". The condition "before `bh`" (`bhe`) is what the vutti's `bhakāre pare` and its question `Bheti kimatthaṃ?` answer to, and the reading of the Rūpasiddhi; the CST's vutti section prints `te` in the sutta, a misprint this chapter does not follow. `bh` is chiefly the `bhi` of the third and fifth forms ([§99 (81): Smāhismiṃnaṃ mhābhimhi vā](#m99)): `santa` + `bhi` gives `sa` + `b` + `bhi`, `sabbhi`; but it is also the `bh` of a following compound member (`sabbhūto`, `sabbhāvo`). There is no option marker: with `bhi` the form is always `sabbhi`, while `hi` gives `santehi`. The vutti's last remark gives the `ca` a further job — `sa` alone, without the `b`, in some words: `sakkāro`, `sakkato` (Sanskrit `satkāra`, `satkṛta`), where the following `k` is doubled by [§28 (40): Para dvebhāvo ṭhāne](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m28).

:::

> Sabbassa santasaddassa sakārādeso hoti bhakāre pare, ante ca bakārāgamo hoti.

> Sabbhireva samāsetha,

> Sabbhikubbetha santhavaṃ.

> Sataṃ saddhammamaññāya,

> Seyyo hoti na pāpiyo.

> Jīranti ve rājarathā sucittā.

> Atho sarīrampi jaraṃ[^86] upeti.

> Satañca dhammo na jaraṃ upeti,

> Santo have sabbhi pavedayanti.

> Sabbhūto, sabbhāvo.

> Bheti kimatthaṃ?

> Santehi pūjito bhagavā.

> Caggahaṇaṃ kvaci sakārasseva pasiddhatthaṃ.

> Sakkāro, sakkato.

The whole of the word `santa` is replaced by `sa` when `bh` follows, and `b` is inserted at its end.

:::tip[Examples]

> `sabbhireva samāsetha, sabbhi kubbetha santhavaṃ; sataṃ saddhammamaññāya, seyyo hoti na pāpiyo` \
> [(12S1 1.4.1: Sabbhisutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/12S1/1/1.4/1.4.1#128) \
🚹③⨂(santa) 🔼(eva) ⏯🤘⨂(samāseti) 🚹③⨂(santa) ⏯🤘⨂(kubbati) 🚹②⨀(santhava) 🚹⑥⨂(santa) 🚹②⨀(saddhamma) 🔽(aññāya) 🚹①⨀(seyya) ▶️🤟⨀(hoti) 🔼(na) 🚹①⨀(pāpiya) \
with the good | only | you should associate | with the good | you should make | intimacy | of the good | the true Dhamma | having understood | better | one becomes | not | worse

> `jīranti ve rājarathā sucittā, atho sarīrampi jaraṃ upeti; satañca dhammo na jaraṃ upeti, santo have sabbhi pavedayanti` \
> [(18Dh 11.6: Mallikādevīvatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Dh/11/11.6#162) \
▶️🤟⨂(jīrati) 🔼(ve) 🚹①⨂(rājaratha) 🚹①⨂(sucitta) 🔼(atho) 🚻①⨀(sarīra) 🔼(pi) 🚺②⨀(jarā) ▶️🤟⨀(upeti) 🚹⑥⨂(santa) 🔼(ca) 🚹①⨀(dhamma) 🔼(na) 🚺②⨀(jarā) ▶️🤟⨀(upeti) 🚹①⨂(santa) 🔼(have) 🚹③⨂(santa) ▶️🤟⨂(pavedeti) \
wear out | indeed | royal chariots | well-adorned | and then | the body | too | old age | reaches | of the good | and | the Dhamma | not | old age | reaches | the good | truly | with the good | make known

The `sataṃ` and `satañca` of the verses are the sixth form plural of `santa` without the change, since no `bh` follows.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨂(santa) | sabbhi | with the good |
| 🚹①⨀(santa + bhūta) | sabbhūto | real, genuine |
| 🚹①⨀(santa + bhāva) | sabbhāvo | true nature; existence |

|     | santa + hi (③⨂)         | rule |
| --- | ----------------------- | ---- |
| →   | santa + (hi→bhi)        | §99  |
| →   | (santa→sa) + bhi        | §185 |
| →   | sa + (b) + bhi          | §185 |
| →   | **sabbhi**              |      |

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `bhe` (before `bh`) the rule does not apply when `hi` keeps its shape.

> `santehi pūjito bhagavā` \
🚹③⨂(santa) 🚹①⨀(pūjita) 🚹①⨀(bhagavantu) \
by the good | honoured | the Blessed One

> `santo santehi saha purāṇajaṭilehi` \
> [(3V 1.13: Bimbisārasamāgamakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/1/1.13#214) \
🚹①⨀(santa) 🚹③⨂(santa) 🔼(saha) 🚹③⨂(purāṇajaṭila) \
the good one | with the good | together | with the former matted-hair ascetics

:::

:::note[Additional]

`Caggahaṇaṃ kvaci sakārasseva pasiddhatthaṃ`: the word `ca` is to establish that in some places `sa` alone, without the `b`, is the accepted form. The `k` that follows is doubled by [§28 (40): Para dvebhāvo ṭhāne](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m28).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(santa + kāra) | sakkāro | honour |
| 🚹①⨀(santa + kata) | sakkato | honoured |

> `sabbo me sakkāro bhañjissati` \
> [(3V 8.1: Jīvakavatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/8/8.1#1560) \
🚹①⨀(sabba) ⚧️⑥⨀(amha) 🚹①⨀(sakkāra) ⏯🤟⨀(bhañjati) \
all | my | honour | will be broken

> `sakkato garukato mānito pūjito` \
> [(1V 1.1.4: Catutthapārājikasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.1/1.1.4#542) \
🚹①⨀(sakkata) 🚹①⨀(garukata) 🚹①⨀(mānita) 🚹①⨀(pūjita) \
honoured | respected | esteemed | venerated

:::

[^86]: Nandisena's edition twice reads `rajaṃ upeti` ("reaches dust") in this verse. Dhp 151 has `jaraṃ`, and the verse is about ageing (`jīranti` … `jaraṃ`); `rajaṃ` is a misprint or corruption in his source.

---

<a name="m186"></a>

### §186 (107): Simhigacchantādīnaṃ ntasaddo aṃ (`nta` of `gacchanta` and the like becomes `aṃ` before `si`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| (vā)   | gacchantādīnaṃ | ntasaddo | (āpajjate) | aṃ | simhi | |
| (optionally) | of `gacchanta` and the like | the sound `nta` | (becomes) | `aṃ` | before `si` | |

`gacchantādīnaṃ` ("of `gacchanta` and the like") names the present participles formed with the suffix `anta` ([§565 (646): Vattamāne mānantā](/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa#m565), in the Kibbidhāna chapter): `gacchanta` ("going"), `caranta` ("wandering"), `khādanta` ("eating"), and with them the adjective `mahanta` ("great"). `ntasaddo` is their final `nta`; before the `si` of the first form ①⨀ it becomes `aṃ`, and the `si` is then elided by [§220 (74): Sesato lopaṃ gasipi](#m220). The sutta carries no option marker of its own; the vutti supplies `vā`, carried over from [§184 (96): Sa sare vāgamo](#m184), the last `vā` of the section. It opens an option: when the `nta` is kept, `si` becomes `o` by [§104 (66): So](#m104) as after any stem in `a`, so `gacchaṃ` and `gacchanto` are both good Pāli.

:::

> Simhi gacchantādīnaṃ ntasaddo amāpajjate vā.

> Gacchaṃ, gacchanto,

> mahaṃ, mahanto,

> caraṃ, caranto,

> khādaṃ, khādanto.

> Gacchantādīnamiti kimatthaṃ?

> Anto, danto, vanto, santo.

Optionally, before the `si` inflection the sound `nta` of `gacchanta` and the like becomes `aṃ`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(gacchanta) | gacchaṃ | one going |
| 🚹①⨀(gacchanta) | gacchanto | one going (option not taken: `si` becomes `o` by §104) |
| 🚹①⨀(mahanta) | mahaṃ | great |
| 🚹①⨀(mahanta) | mahanto | great (by §104) |
| 🚹①⨀(caranta) | caraṃ | one wandering |
| 🚹①⨀(caranta) | caranto | one wandering (by §104) |
| 🚹①⨀(khādanta) | khādaṃ | one eating |
| 🚹①⨀(khādanta) | khādanto | one eating (by §104) |

|     | gacchanta + si (①⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | gaccha + (nta→aṃ) + si    | §186 |
| →   | gacchaṃ + ~~si~~          | §220 |
| →   | **gacchaṃ**               |      |

|     | gacchanta + si (①⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | gacchanta + (si→o)        | §104 |
| →   | gacchant + (~~a~~ + o)    | §83  |
| →   | **gacchanto**             |      |

> `gacchaṃ vadesi samaṇa ṭhitomhi` \
> [(10M 4.6: Aṅgulimālasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/10M/4/4.6#999) \
🚹①⨀(gacchanta) ▶️🤘⨀(vadati) 🚹⓪⨀(samaṇa) 🚹①⨀(ṭhita) ▶️👆⨀(atthi) \
going | you say | ascetic | standing | I am

> `carañce nādhigaccheyya, seyyaṃ sadisamattano` \
> [(18Dh 5.2: Mahākassapasaddhivihārikavatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Dh/5/5.2#66) \
🚹①⨀(caranta) 🔼(ce) 🔼(na) ⏯🤟⨀(adhigacchati) 🚹②⨀(seyya) 🚹②⨀(sadisa) 🚹⑥⨀(atta) \
wandering | if | not | one should find | a better | or an equal | to oneself

> `khādaṃ nāgassa maṃsāni, pivaṃ bhāgīrathodakaṃ` \
> [(23J 19.1.1: Soṇakajātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/23J/19/19.1/19.1.1#469) \
🚹①⨀(khādanta) 🚹⑥⨀(nāga) 🚻②⨂(maṃsa) 🚹①⨀(pivanta) 🚻②⨀(bhāgīrathodaka) \
eating | of the elephant | the flesh | drinking | the water of the Bhāgīrathī

In `carañce` the `aṃ` of `caraṃ` has become `ñ` before `ce` by [§31 (49): Vaggantaṃ vā vagge](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m31).

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `gacchantādīnaṃ` (of `gacchanta` and the like) the rule applies only to the participles in `anta`. Other words that happen to end in `nta` — `anta` ("end"), `danta` ("tamed"), `vanta` ("vomited", "given up") and `santa` ("calmed"; as the irregular participle of `atthi`, "existing", "a good man", it has its own rule in [§185 (112): Santasaddassa so bhe bo cante](#m185)) — are not of this group: their `nta` stays and `si` becomes `o` by [§104 (66): So](#m104).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(anta) | anto | end |
| 🚹①⨀(danta) | danto | tamed |
| 🚹①⨀(vanta) | vanto | vomited, given up |
| 🚹①⨀(santa) | santo | calmed, at peace |

> `danto seṭṭho manussesu` \
> [(18Dh 23.1: Attadantavatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Dh/23/23.1#346) \
🚹①⨀(danta) 🚹①⨀(seṭṭha) 🚹⑦⨂(manussa) \
the tamed one | is best | among humans

:::

---

<a name="m187"></a>

### §187 (108): Sesesu ntuva (in the remaining [inflections], like `ntu`) :badge[vidhyaṅga]{variant=tip}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | (gacchantādīnaṃ) | (ntasaddo) | (daṭṭhabbo) | ntu va | sesesu | (vibhattippaccayesu) |
|        | (of `gacchanta` and the like) | (the sound `nta`) | (is to be treated) | like `ntu` | in the remaining | (inflections and suffixes) |

`gacchantādīnaṃ ntasaddo` continues from [§186 (107): Simhigacchantādīnaṃ ntasaddo aṃ](#m186); the vutti runs the words together as `gacchantādīnaṃntasaddontuppaccayova`, that is `gacchantādīnaṃ ntasaddo ntuppaccayo va`. `ntu va` is `ntu` + `va` ("like"): the sutta states no result but an instruction (`daṭṭhabbo`, "is to be seen as") — an `atidesa`, a transfer by analogy — that extends the whole treatment of the possessive suffix `ntu` (`guṇavantu`: [§92 (100): Ntussanto yosuca](#m92), [§122 (99): Ntussa nto](#m122) to [§128 (104): Naṃmhi taṃ vā](#m128)) to the `nta` of the participles. `sesesu` ("in the remaining") excludes the one inflection §186 has already provided for, `si`. There is no option marker: wherever an `ntu` rule applies, it applies to `gacchanta`.

:::

> Gacchantādīnaṃntasaddontuppaccayova daṭṭhabbo sesesu vibhattippaccayesu.

> Gacchato, mahato,

> gacchati, mahati,

> gacchatā, mahatā.

> Sesesūti kimatthaṃ?

> Gacchaṃ, mahaṃ, caraṃ, khādaṃ.

In the remaining inflections and suffixes the sound `nta` of `gacchanta` and the like is to be treated as the suffix `ntu`.

:::tip[Examples]

The vutti's three pairs are the three replacements of [§127 (102): To titā sasmiṃnāsu](#m127) — `sa` → `to`, `smiṃ` → `ti`, `nā` → `tā` — applied to `nta` exactly as to `ntu`.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑥⨀(gacchanta) | gacchato | (of) one going |
| 🚹⑥⨀(mahanta) | mahato | (of) the great |
| 🚹⑦⨀(gacchanta) | gacchati | (in) one going (not the verb `gacchati`) |
| 🚹⑦⨀(mahanta) | mahati | (in) the great |
| 🚹③⨀(gacchanta) | gacchatā | (by) one going |
| 🚹③⨀(mahanta) | mahatā | (by) the great |

|     | gacchanta + sa (⑥⨀)        | rule |
| --- | -------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | gaccha + (nta = ntu) + sa  | §187 |
| →   | gaccha + (ntu + sa → to)   | §127 |
| →   | **gacchato**               |      |

The parallel with `guṇavantu` runs through the whole paradigm:

| `ntu` rule | 🚹(guṇavantu) | 🚹(gacchanta) |
| ---------- | ------------- | ------------- |
| [§127 (102): To titā sasmiṃnāsu](#m127) — `sa` → `to`, `smiṃ` → `ti`, `nā` → `tā` | guṇavato, guṇavati, guṇavatā | gacchato, gacchati, gacchatā |
| [§122 (99): Ntussa nto](#m122) — `ntu` + `yo` ① → `nto` | guṇavanto | gacchanto |
| [§123 (103): Ntassa se vā](#m123) — `ntu` + `sa` → `ntassa` | guṇavantassa | gacchantassa |
| [§128 (104): Naṃmhi taṃ vā](#m128) — `ntu` + `naṃ` → `taṃ` | guṇavataṃ | gacchataṃ |
| [§92 (100): Ntussanto yosuca](#m92) — `u` → `a` before `su`, `naṃ`, `hi`, `yo` | guṇavantesu, guṇavantānaṃ, guṇavantehi, guṇavantā, guṇavante | gacchantesu, gacchantānaṃ, gacchantehi, gacchantā, gacchante |
| [§125 (198): Aṃ napuṃsake](#m125) — neuter `si` → `aṃ` | guṇavaṃ (cittaṃ) | gacchaṃ (cittaṃ) |
| [§126 (101): Avaṇṇā ca ge](#m126) — in address | bho guṇavaṃ, guṇava, guṇavā | bho gacchaṃ, gaccha, gacchā |

Only [§124 (98): Ā simhi](#m124) is withheld: before `si` the participle follows §186, not `ntu` (below).

> `evarūpe te mahārāja mahati mahabbhaye samuppanne` \
> [(12S1 3.3.5: Pabbatūpamasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/12S1/3/3.3/3.3.5#756) \
🚹⑦⨀(evarūpa) ⚧️④⨀(tumha) 🚹⓪⨀(mahārāja) 🚹⑦⨀(mahanta) 🚹⑦⨀(mahabbhaya) 🚹⑦⨀(samuppanna) \
such | for you | great king | a great | peril | having arisen

> `paradāraṃ gacchato` \
> [(6D 2.3.1: Pūraṇakassapavāda)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/6D/2/2.3/2.3.1#244) \
🚹②⨀(paradāra) 🚹⑥⨀(gacchanta) \
to another's wife | of one going

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `sesesu` (in the remaining ones) the `nta` is not treated as `ntu` before `si`: there [§186 (107): Simhigacchantādīnaṃ ntasaddo aṃ](#m186) gives `gacchaṃ`, where the `ntu` rule [§124 (98): Ā simhi](#m124) would have given `gacchā`.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(gacchanta) | gacchaṃ | one going |
| 🚹①⨀(mahanta) | mahaṃ | great |
| 🚹①⨀(caranta) | caraṃ | one wandering |
| 🚹①⨀(khādanta) | khādaṃ | one eating |

:::

---

<a name="m188"></a>

### §188 (115): Brahmatta sakha rājādito amānaṃ (`ānaṃ` for `aṃ` after `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha`, `rāja` and the like) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| (vā)   | brahmatta sakha rājādito | aṃ (vacanassa) | (hoti) | ānaṃ | | |
| (optionally) | after `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha`, `rāja` and the like | the `aṃ` inflection | (becomes) | `ānaṃ` | | |

`brahmatta-sakha-rājādito` is `brahma` + `atta` + `sakha` + `rāja` + `ādi` + `to`: "after `brahma` ("Brahmā"), `atta` ("self"), `sakha` ("friend"), `rāja` ("king") and the like" — a group of masculine stems in `a` that the suttas down to [§198 (123): Uttaṃ sanāsu](#m198) treat together, and to which the vutti of §189 adds `ātuma` ("self"). `amānaṃ` is `aṃ` + `ānaṃ`: the second-form singular inflection ②⨀ and its replacement. The sutta has no marker; the vutti's `vā` is carried over from [§186 (107): Simhigacchantādīnaṃ ntasaddo aṃ](#m186) and opens an option: the plain `aṃ` of [§55 (63): Si yo, aṃ yo, nā hi, sa naṃ, smā hi, sa naṃ, smiṃ su](#m55) remains available, so `brahmānaṃ` and `brahmaṃ`, `rājānaṃ` and `rājaṃ` are both good Pāli.

:::

> Brahma atta sakha rājaiccevamādito aṃvacanassa ānaṃ hoti vā.

> Brahmānaṃ, brahmaṃ,

> attānaṃ, attaṃ,

> sakhānaṃ, sakhaṃ,

> rājānaṃ, rājaṃ.

> Amiti kimatthaṃ?

> Rājā.

Optionally, after `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha`, `rāja` and the like the `aṃ` inflection (②⨀) becomes `ānaṃ`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹②⨀(brahma) | brahmānaṃ | Brahmā |
| 🚹②⨀(brahma) | brahmaṃ | Brahmā (option not taken) |
| 🚹②⨀(atta) | attānaṃ | (one)self |
| 🚹②⨀(atta) | attaṃ | (one)self (option not taken) |
| 🚹②⨀(sakha) | sakhānaṃ | friend |
| 🚹②⨀(sakha) | sakhaṃ | friend (option not taken) |
| 🚹②⨀(rāja) | rājānaṃ | king |
| 🚹②⨀(rāja) | rājaṃ | king (option not taken) |

|     | brahma + aṃ (②⨀)          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | brahma + (aṃ→ānaṃ)        | §188 |
| →   | brahm + (~~a~~ + ānaṃ)    | §83  |
| →   | **brahmānaṃ**             |      |

The stem-final `a` is elided before the vowel of the replacement by [§83 (67): Saralopo’ mādesa paccayādimhi saralope tu pakati](#m83), just as it is before the plain `aṃ` (`brahm` + `aṃ`, `brahmaṃ`).

> `attānaṃ upamaṃ katvā, na haneyya na ghātaye` \
> [(18Dh 10.1: Chabbaggiyabhikkhuvatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Dh/10/10.1#139) \
🚹②⨀(atta) 🚺②⨀(upamā) 🔽(katvā) 🔼(na) ⏯🤟⨀(hanati) 🔼(na) ⏯🤟⨀(ghāteti) \
oneself | a comparison | having made | not | one should kill | not | one should have killed

> `rājānaṃ māgadhaṃ seniyaṃ bimbisāraṃ etadavoca` \
> [(1V 1.1.2: Dutiyapārājikasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.1/1.1.2#199) \
🚹②⨀(rāja) 🚹②⨀(māgadha) 🚹②⨀(seniya) 🚹②⨀(bimbisāra) ⚧️②⨀(eta) ⏮🤟⨀(vatti) \
the king | of Magadha | Seniya | Bimbisāra | this | he said

> `kiñci attaṃ vā attaniyaṃ vā samanupassatī` \
> [(13S3 1.2.4.7: Khemakasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/13S3/1/1.2/1.2.4/1.2.4.7#545) \
⚧️②⨀(kiñci) 🚹②⨀(atta) 🔼(vā) 🚹②⨀(attaniya) 🔼(vā) ▶️🤟⨀(samanupassati) \
anything | as self | or | as belonging to self | or | he regards

`sakhānaṃ` and `sakhaṃ` are not attested in the Tipiṭaka, which has `sakhāraṃ` ([§196 (133): Sunamaṃsu vā](#m196)).

:::

:::danger[Counter-example]

By saying `aṃ` (the `aṃ` inflection) the replacement is confined to the second form singular; before `si` the same stems take `ā` by the next rule, [§189 (113): Syā ca](#m189).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(rāja) | rājā | king |

:::

---

<a name="m189"></a>

### §189 (113): Syā ca (and `ā` for `si`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     | (brahmatta sakha rājādito) | si (vacanassa) | (hoti) | ā | | |
| and    | (after `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha`, `rāja` and the like) | the `si` inflection | (becomes) | `ā` | | |

`syā` is `si` + `ā`, the `i` having become `y` before the vowel by [§21 (21): Ivaṇṇo yaṃ navā](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m21): "and `ā` for `si`". `brahmatta sakha rājādito` continues from [§188 (115): Brahmatta sakha rājādito amānaṃ](#m188). By saying `ca` this rule is an extension of §188: the same group of stems receives a further replacement, now for `si`. Unlike §188 it carries no `vā` and the vutti adds none, so the replacement is obligatory — `rājā`, `brahmā`, `attā`, `sakhā` are the only forms of the first form singular ①⨀; the `o` of [§104 (66): So](#m104) does not reach these stems.

:::

> Brahma atta sakha rājaiccevamādito sivacanassa ā ca hoti.

> Brahmā, attā, sakhā, rājā, ātumā.

And after `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha`, `rāja` and the like the `si` inflection (①⨀) becomes `ā`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(brahma) | brahmā | Brahmā |
| 🚹①⨀(atta) | attā | self |
| 🚹①⨀(sakha) | sakhā | friend |
| 🚹①⨀(rāja) | rājā | king |
| 🚹①⨀(ātuma) | ātumā | self |

|     | rāja + si (①⨀)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | rāja + (si→ā)            | §189 |
| →   | rāj + (~~a~~ + ā)        | §83  |
| →   | **rājā**                 |      |

`ātumā` shows the `ādi` ("and the like") of the sutta at work: `ātuma` ("self", a poetic doublet of `atta`) is not named but follows the group.

> `attā hi attano nātho` \
> [(18Dh 12.4: Kumārakassapamātuttherivatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Dh/12/12.4#172) \
🚹①⨀(atta) 🔼(hi) 🚹⑥⨀(atta) 🚹①⨀(nātha) \
self | indeed | of self | the protector

> `yo ca atthesu jātesu, sahāyo hoti so sakhā` \
> [(8D 8.10: Ālasyassa cha ādīnavā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/8D/8/8.10#593) \
🚹①⨀(ya) 🔼(ca) 🚹⑦⨂(attha) 🚹⑦⨂(jāta) 🚹①⨀(sahāya) ▶️🤟⨀(hoti) 🚹①⨀(ta) 🚹①⨀(sakha) \
who | and | when needs | have arisen | a helper | is | he | a friend

:::

---

<a name="m190"></a>

### §190 (114): Yonamāno (`āno` for `yo`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | (brahmatta sakha rājādito) | yonaṃ | (ādeso) | āno | | |
|        | (after `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha`, `rāja` and the like) | the `yo` inflections | (replaced by) | `āno` | | |

`yonamāno` is `yonaṃ` + `āno`, the niggahīta becoming `m` before the vowel by [§34 (52): Madā sare](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m34): "`āno` for the `yo`s". `yonaṃ` is the sixth-form plural of `yo` and covers both `yo` inflections, ①⨂ and ②⨂. The stems continue from [§188 (115): Brahmatta sakha rājādito amānaṃ](#m188). No marker: the replacement is obligatory, and displaces the `ā` and `e` that [§107 (69): Sabbayonīnamāe](#m107) gives after other stems in `a` (`purisā`, `purise`). For `sakha` alone the next rule adds two more replacements.

:::

> Brahmaatta sakha rājaiccevamādito yonaṃ ānoādeso hoti.

> Brahmāno, attāno, sakhāno, rājāno, ātumāno.

After `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha`, `rāja` and the like the `yo` inflections (①⨂ and ②⨂) are replaced by `āno`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(brahma) | brahmāno | Brahmās |
| 🚹①⨂(atta) | attāno | selves |
| 🚹①⨂(sakha) | sakhāno | friends |
| 🚹①⨂(rāja) | rājāno | kings |
| 🚹①⨂(ātuma) | ātumāno | selves |

|     | rāja + yo (①⨂)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | rāja + (yo→āno)          | §190 |
| →   | rāj + (~~a~~ + āno)      | §83  |
| →   | **rājāno**               |      |

> `mayaṃ kho bhante rājāno nāma bahukiccā bahukaraṇīyā` \
> [(1V 1.1.2: Dutiyapārājikasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.1/1.1.2#201) \
⚧️①⨂(amha) 🔼(kho) 🔼(bhante) 🚹①⨂(rāja) 🔼(nāma) 🚹①⨂(bahukicca) 🚹①⨂(bahukaraṇīya) \
we | indeed | venerable sir | kings | as such | have many duties | have much to do

> `addasā kho so brahmā te brahmāno dūratova āgacchante` \
> [(12S1 6.1.6: Brahmalokasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/12S1/6/6.1/6.1.6#1064) \
⏮🤟⨀(passati) 🔼(kho) 🚹①⨀(ta) 🚹①⨀(brahma) 🚹②⨂(ta) 🚹②⨂(brahma) 🔼(dūrato eva) 🚹②⨂(āgacchanta) \
saw | indeed | that | Brahmā | those | Brahmās | from afar | coming

The second citation shows `brahmāno` as the second form ②⨂, the object of `addasā`, beside `brahmā` ①⨀ of §189. `attāno` and `sakhāno` are not attested in the Tipiṭaka.

:::

---

<a name="m191"></a>

### §191 (130): Sakhato cāyo no (and after `sakha`, `āyo` and `no`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     | sakhato | (yonaṃ) | (ādesā) | āyo \| no | | |
| and    | after `sakha` | (the `yo` inflections) | (replaced by) | `āyo` \| `no` | | |

`sakhato cāyo no` is `sakhato` + `ca` + `āyo` + `no` ("and after `sakha`: `āyo`, `no`"), the `a` of `ca` elided before `āyo` by [§12 (13): Sarā sare lopaṃ](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m12).[^87] `yonaṃ` and `ādesā` continue from [§190 (114): Yonamāno](#m190). By saying `ca` this rule is an extension of §190: `sakha` keeps the `āno` of that rule (`sakhāno`) and gains two further replacements of `yo`, so that `sakhāno`, `sakhāyo` and `sakhino` are all good Pāli — the `ca` does here the work an option marker would do, adding forms rather than replacing them. Before the `no` the stem-final `a` becomes `i` by [§194 (131): Sakhantassi no nā naṃ sesu](#m194).

:::

> Tasmā sakhato ca yonaṃ āyo no ādesā honti.

> Sakhāyo, sakhino.

> Yonamiti kimatthaṃ?

> Sakhā.

And after that `sakha` the `yo` inflections (①⨂ and ②⨂) are also replaced by `āyo` and `no`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(sakha) | sakhāyo | friends |
| 🚹①⨂(sakha) | sakhino | friends |

|     | sakha + yo (①⨂)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sakha + (yo→āyo)          | §191 |
| →   | sakh + (~~a~~ + āyo)      | §83  |
| →   | **sakhāyo**               |      |

|     | sakha + yo (①⨂)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sakha + (yo→no)           | §191 |
| →   | sakh + (a→i) + no         | §194 |
| →   | **sakhino**               |      |

> `ñātī suhajje sakhino samānayi` \
> [(8D 7.14: Lakkhaṇasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/8D/7/7.14#485) \
🚹②⨂(ñāti) 🚹②⨂(suhajja) 🚹②⨂(sakha) ⏮🤟⨀(samāneti) \
relatives | dear ones | friends | he brought together

`sakhāyo` is not attested in the Tipiṭaka; `sakhino` is, as the second form ②⨂ here (and, with `no` for `sa` by [§117 (124): Jhalato sassano vā](#m117), as ④⨀ and ⑥⨀).

:::

:::danger[Counter-example]

By saying `yonaṃ` (of the `yo`s) the two new replacements belong to the plural inflections `yo` only; the singular `si` keeps the `ā` of [§189 (113): Syā ca](#m189).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(sakha) | sakhā | friend |

:::

[^87]: Nandisena's edition records the Sinhalese reading `Sakhāto` for the sutta's `Sakhato`.

---

<a name="m192"></a>

### §192 (135): Smime (`e` for `smiṃ`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | (sakhato) | smiṃ (vacanassa) | (hoti) | e | | |
|        | (after `sakha`) | the `smiṃ` inflection | (becomes) | `e` | | |

`smime` is `smiṃ` + `e`, the niggahīta becoming `m` before the vowel by [§34 (52): Madā sare](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m34). `sakhato` continues from [§191 (130): Sakhato cāyo no](#m191). No marker: after `sakha` the seventh form singular ⑦⨀ is `sakhe`; the `e` that [§108 (90): Smāsmiṃnaṃ vā](#m108) gives optionally after other stems in `a` (`purise` beside `purisasmiṃ`) is here the only form taught.

:::

> Tasmā sakhato smiṃvacanassa ekāro hoti.

> Sakhe.

After that `sakha` the `smiṃ` inflection (⑦⨀) becomes the letter `e`.

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨀(sakha) | sakhe | (in) a friend |

|     | sakha + smiṃ (⑦⨀)         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sakha + (smiṃ→e)          | §192 |
| →   | sakh + (~~a~~ + e)        | §83  |
| →   | **sakhe**                 |      |

The form is not attested in the Tipiṭaka. `sakhe` also serves in address, as one of the five vocatives of [§113 (132): Sakhato gasse vā](#m113).

:::

---

<a name="m193"></a>

### §193 (122): Brahmato gassa ca (and `e` for `ga` after `brahma`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     | brahmato | gassa | (hoti) | (ekāro) | | |
| and    | after `brahma` | the `ga` inflection | (becomes) | (`e`) | | |

`ga` is the name of the `si` used in address (⓪⨀), given by [§57 (71): Ālapane si ga sañño](#m57); `gassa` is its sixth form, "of `ga`". `ekāro` continues from [§192 (135): Smime](#m192). By saying `ca` this rule is an extension of §192: the letter `e` that replaces `smiṃ` after `sakha` also replaces `ga` after `brahma`. No marker: the address form of `brahma` is `brahme`; the `ā` that [§244 (72): Akārapitādyantānamā](#m244) gives other stems in `a` in address (`bho purisā`, `bho rājā`) is not taught for it.

:::

> Tasmā brahmato gassa ca ekāro hoti.

> He brahme!

And after that `brahma` the `ga` inflection (the `si` of address, ⓪⨀) also becomes the letter `e`.

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⓪⨀(brahma) | he brahme | O Brahmā! |

|     | brahma + si = ga (⓪⨀)     | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | brahma + (ga→e)           | §193 |
| →   | brahm + (~~a~~ + e)       | §83  |
| →   | **brahme**                |      |

> `mayhampi kho brahme etadahosi` \
> [(3V 1.5: Brahmayācanakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/1/1.5#33) \
⚧️④⨀(amha) 🔼(pi kho) 🚹⓪⨀(brahma) ⚧️①⨀(eta) ⏮🤟⨀(hoti) \
to me | too | Brahmā | this | occurred

:::

---

<a name="m194"></a>

### §194 (131): Sakhantassi no nā naṃ sesu (`i` for the final of `sakha` before `no`, `nā`, `naṃ`, `sa`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        |        | sakhantassa | (hoti) | i | no nā naṃ sesu | |
|        |        | the final of `sakha` | (becomes) | `i` | before `no`, `nā`, `naṃ`, `sa` | |

`sakhantassi` is `sakhantassa` + `i`: "of the final (`anta`) of `sakha`, `i`", the `a` of `-assa` elided before `i` by [§12 (13): Sarā sare lopaṃ](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m12). `no nā naṃ sesu` is the locative plural of the list `no`, `nā`, `naṃ`, `sa`: "before these". `no` is not an inflection of [§55 (63): Si yo, aṃ yo, nā hi, sa naṃ, smā hi, sa naṃ, smiṃ su](#m55) but a replacement — of `yo` by [§191 (130): Sakhato cāyo no](#m191) and of `sa` by [§117 (124): Jhalato sassano vā](#m117). No marker: the change is obligatory, though before `naṃ` the `āra` of [§196 (133): Sunamaṃsu vā](#m196) offers a second form.

:::

> Tassa sakhantassa ikāro hoti nonānaṃsaiccetesu.

> Sakhino, sakhinā, sakhīnaṃ, sakhissa.

> Etesvīti kimatthaṃ?

> Sakhārehi.

The final of that `sakha` becomes the letter `i` before `no`, `nā`, `naṃ` and `sa`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(sakha) | sakhino | friends (`no` for `yo`, §191; also ④⨀ and ⑥⨀ with `no` for `sa`, §117) |
| 🚹③⨀(sakha) | sakhinā | (by) a friend |
| 🚹⑥⨂(sakha) | sakhīnaṃ | (of) friends |
| 🚹⑥⨀(sakha) | sakhissa | (of) a friend |

|     | sakha + naṃ (⑥⨂)          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sakh + (a→i) + naṃ        | §194 |
| →   | sakh + (i→ī) + naṃ        | §89  |
| →   | **sakhīnaṃ**              |      |

|     | sakha + sa (⑥⨀)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sakh + (a→i) + sa         | §194 |
| →   | sakhi + (s) + sa          | §61  |
| →   | **sakhissa**              |      |

The `ī` of `sakhīnaṃ` is the lengthening before `naṃ` of [§89 (87): Sunaṃhisu ca](#m89); the doubled `ss` of `sakhissa` is the augment `s` of [§61 (86): Sāgamo se](#m61). With `no` for `sa` by §117 the same stem gives `sakhino`.

> `kataṃ anariyaṃ sākhena, sakhinā te janādhipa` \
> [(22J 10.1.7: Nigrodhajātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/22J/10/10.1/10.1.7#1988) \
🚻①⨀(kata) 🚻①⨀(anariya) 🚹③⨀(sākha) 🚹③⨀(sakha) ⚧️⑥⨀(tumha) 🚹⓪⨀(janādhipa) \
done | an ignoble deed | by Sākha | by the friend | your | O lord of men

> `yo ñātīnaṃ sakhīnaṃ vā, dāresu paṭidissati` \
> [(18Sn 1.7: Vasalasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Sn/1/1.7#143) \
🚹①⨀(ya) 🚹⑥⨂(ñāti) 🚹⑥⨂(sakha) 🔼(vā) 🚹⑦⨂(dāra) ▶️🤟⨀(paṭidissati) \
who | of relatives | of friends | or | among the wives | is seen

`sakhissa` is not attested in the Tipiṭaka.

:::

:::danger[Counter-example]

By saying `etesu` (before these) the `i` is confined to `no`, `nā`, `naṃ` and `sa`; before `hi` the final of `sakha` becomes `āra` by [§195 (134): Āro himhi vā](#m195) or `e` by [§101 (80): Suhisvakāro e](#m101), never `i`.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨂(sakha) | sakhārehi | (by) friends |

:::

---

<a name="m195"></a>

### §195 (134): Āro himhi vā (optionally `āra` before `hi`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| vā     |        | (sakhantassa) | (hoti) | āro | himhi | (vibhattimhi) |
| optionally |    | (the final of `sakha`) | (becomes) | `āra` | before `hi` | (inflection) |

`sakhantassa` continues from [§194 (131): Sakhantassi no nā naṃ sesu](#m194). `āro` is the replacement `āra` (in the nominative, as the vutti has it): the final `a` of `sakha` becomes `āra`, giving a new stem `sakhāra` (the DPD lists it as a headword) that then inflects like any masculine in `a`. The option marker `vā` indicates this is an alternate to the ordinary treatment of a stem in `a` before `hi`, `a` → `e` by [§101 (80): Suhisvakāro e](#m101): `sakhārehi` and `sakhehi` are both good Pāli, and each may take `bhi` for `hi` by [§99 (81): Smāhismiṃnaṃ mhābhimhi vā](#m99).

:::

> Tassa sakhantassa āro hoti vā himhi vibhattimhi.

> Sakhārehi, sakhehi.

Optionally, the final of that `sakha` becomes `āra` before the `hi` inflection (③⨂ and ⑤⨂).

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨂(sakha) | sakhārehi | (by) friends |
| 🚹③⨂(sakha) | sakhehi | (by) friends (option not taken) |

|     | sakha + hi (③⨂)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sakh + (a→āra) + hi       | §195 |
| →   | sakhār + (a→e) + hi       | §101 |
| →   | **sakhārehi**             |      |

|     | sakha + hi (③⨂)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sakh + (a→e) + hi         | §101 |
| →   | **sakhehi**               |      |

Neither form is attested in the Tipiṭaka; the stem `sakhāra` is, in `sakhāraṃ` under the next rule.

:::

---

<a name="m196"></a>

### §196 (133): Sunamaṃsu vā (optionally [`āra`] before `su`, `naṃ`, `aṃ`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| vā     |        | (sakhantassa) | (hoti) | (āro) | sunamaṃsu | |
| optionally |    | (the final of `sakha`) | (becomes) | (`āra`) | before `su`, `naṃ`, `aṃ` | |

`sunamaṃsu` is the locative plural of the list `su`, `naṃ`, `aṃ` — `sunaṃ` + `aṃsu`, the niggahīta of `naṃ` becoming `m` before the vowel by [§34 (52): Madā sare](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m34). `sakhantassa` and `āro` continue from [§195 (134): Āro himhi vā](#m195). The option marker `vā` indicates this is an alternate to the forms the other rules give before these three inflections: before `su`, `sakhesu` with `e` by [§101 (80): Suhisvakāro e](#m101); before `naṃ`, `sakhīnaṃ` with `i` by [§194 (131): Sakhantassi no nā naṃ sesu](#m194); before `aṃ`, `sakhaṃ` with the plain inflection (or `sakhānaṃ` by [§188 (115): Brahmatta sakha rājādito amānaṃ](#m188)). All are good Pāli.

:::

> Tassa sakhantassa āro hoti vā sunaṃ aṃiccetesu.

> Sakhāresu, sakhesu, sakhārānaṃ, sakhīnaṃ, sakhāraṃ, sakhaṃ.

Optionally, the final of that `sakha` becomes `āra` before `su` (⑦⨂), `naṃ` (④⨂ and ⑥⨂) and `aṃ` (②⨀).[^88]

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨂(sakha) | sakhāresu | (among) friends |
| 🚹⑦⨂(sakha) | sakhesu | (among) friends (option not taken, §101) |
| 🚹⑥⨂(sakha) | sakhārānaṃ | (of) friends |
| 🚹⑥⨂(sakha) | sakhīnaṃ | (of) friends (option not taken, §194) |
| 🚹②⨀(sakha) | sakhāraṃ | friend |
| 🚹②⨀(sakha) | sakhaṃ | friend (option not taken) |

|     | sakha + naṃ (⑥⨂)          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sakh + (a→āra) + naṃ      | §196 |
| →   | sakhār + (a→ā) + naṃ      | §89  |
| →   | **sakhārānaṃ**            |      |

|     | sakha + aṃ (②⨀)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sakh + (a→āra) + aṃ       | §196 |
| →   | sakhār + (~~a~~ + aṃ)     | §83  |
| →   | **sakhāraṃ**              |      |

> `sakhā sakhāraṃ paritāyamāno` \
> [(22J 6.2.4: Suvaṇṇakakkaṭajātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/22J/6/6.2/6.2.4#1428) \
🚹①⨀(sakha) 🚹②⨀(sakha) 🚹①⨀(paritāyamāna) \
the friend | his friend | protecting

The line sets `sakhā` (①⨀, §189) beside `sakhāraṃ`. Of the six forms only `sakhāraṃ` and `sakhīnaṃ` are attested in the Tipiṭaka.

:::

[^88]: U Nandisena's vutti text and translation (2005) name the inflections `su`, `saṃ` and `aṃ`; the sutta has `naṃ` (`su-naṃ-aṃ-su`) and the examples `sakhārānaṃ`, `sakhīnaṃ` are `naṃ` forms, so `saṃ` is a slip.

---

<a name="m197"></a>

### §197 (125): Brahmato tu smiṃ ni (but after `brahma`, `ni` for `smiṃ`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}
