:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| tu     | brahmato | smiṃ | (ādeso) | ni | | |
| but    | after `brahma` | the `smiṃ` inflection | (replaced by) | `ni` | | |

`brahmato` ("after `brahma`") returns to the stem of [§193 (122): Brahmato gassa ca](#m193) after three rules on `sakha`. The marker `tu` ("but") marks the contrast with [§192 (135): Smime](#m192): after `sakha` the `smiṃ` became `e`, after `brahma` it becomes `ni`. The vutti reads the `tu` a second way as well (`tuggahaṇena`, "by the mention of `tu`"): the `ni` reaches stems other than `brahma` — `kamma` ("action"), `camma` ("hide"), `muddha` ("head"), which like `brahma` are old stems in `-an`. For `atta`, the other member of the group, `attani` comes by [§212 (129): Tato smiṃ ni](#m212) in the next section. There is no `vā`: `ni` is the only ⑦⨀ the rule teaches, though `brahmani` itself is not attested in the Tipiṭaka.

:::

> Tasmā brahmato smiṃvacanassa niādeso hoti.

> Brahmani.

After that `brahma` the `smiṃ` inflection (⑦⨀) is replaced by `ni`.

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨀(brahma) | brahmani | (in) Brahmā |

|     | brahma + smiṃ (⑦⨀)        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | brahma + (smiṃ→ni)        | §197 |
| →   | **brahmani**              |      |

:::

> Tuggahaṇena abrahmatopi smiṃ vacanassa ni hoti.

> Kammani, cammani, muddhani.

By the mention of `tu`, the `smiṃ` inflection becomes `ni` after [stems] other than `brahma` as well.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻⑦⨀(kamma) | kammani | (in) an action |
| 🚻⑦⨀(camma) | cammani | (in) a hide |
| 🚹⑦⨀(muddha) | muddhani | (on) the head |

> `anujānāmi bhikkhave muddhani` \
> [(3V 6.2: Mūlādibhesajjakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/6/6.2#1220) \
▶️👆⨀(anujānāti) 🚹⓪⨂(bhikkhu) 🚹⑦⨀(muddha) \
I allow | bhikkhus | [oil] on the head

`kammani` and `cammani` are not attested in the Tipiṭaka; `muddhani` is its usual form for "on the head".

:::

---

<a name="m198"></a>

### §198 (123): Uttaṃ sanāsu (`u` before `sa`, `nā`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | (brahmato) | (anto) | (āpajjate) | uttaṃ | sanāsu | |
|        | (of `brahma`) | (the final) | (becomes) | `u` | before `sa`, `nā` | |

`uttaṃ` is "`u`-ness", becoming `u`; `sanāsu` the locative plural of the pair `sa`, `nā`: "before `sa` (④⨀, ⑥⨀) and `nā` (③⨀)". The stem continues from [§197 (125): Brahmato tu smiṃ ni](#m197) — the vutti spells it out as `tassa brahmasaddassa anto`, "the final of that word `brahma`". No marker: the `u` is obligatory.[^89] Once the stem ends in `u` it is a `la` word ([§58 (29): Ivaṇṇuvaṇṇā jhalā](#m58)), so `sa` becomes `no` by [§117 (124): Jhalato sassano vā](#m117) — `brahmuno`, not `brahmassa` — and `nā` stays `nā` — `brahmunā`, not the `ena` of [§103 (79): Ato nena](#m103). By [§270 (120): Amha tumha ntu rāja brahmatta sakha satthu pitādīhi smā nāva](#m270) the `smā` of the fifth form ⑤⨀ follows `nā`: `brahmunā` again.

:::

> Tassa brahmasaddassa anto uttamāpajjate sanāiccetesu.

> Brahmuno, brahmunā.

> Sanāsūti kimatthaṃ?

> Brahmā.

The final of that word `brahma` becomes `u` before `sa` and `nā`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑥⨀(brahma) | brahmuno | (of) Brahmā |
| 🚹③⨀(brahma) | brahmunā | (by) Brahmā |

|     | brahma + sa (⑥⨀)          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | brahm + (a→u) + sa        | §198 |
| →   | brahmu + (sa→no)          | §117 |
| →   | **brahmuno**              |      |

|     | brahma + nā (③⨀)          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | brahm + (a→u) + nā        | §198 |
| →   | **brahmunā**              |      |

> `atha kho brahmuno sahampatissa` \
> [(3V 1.5: Brahmayācanakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/1/1.5#29) \
🔼(atha) 🔼(kho) 🚹⑥⨀(brahma) 🚹⑥⨀(sahampati) \
then | indeed | of Brahmā | Sahampati

> `mārena vā brahmunā vā kenaci vā` \
> [(3V 1.6: Pañcavaggiyakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/1/1.6#82) \
🚹③⨀(māra) 🔼(vā) 🚹③⨀(brahma) 🔼(vā) ⚧️③⨀(kiñci) 🔼(vā) \
by Māra | or | by Brahmā | or | by anyone | or

:::

:::danger[Counter-example]

By saying `sanāsu` (before `sa` and `nā`) the final stays `a` before the other inflections: before `si` it takes the `ā` of [§189 (113): Syā ca](#m189).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(brahma) | brahmā | Brahmā |

:::

[^89]: Nandisena's edition records that the Sinhalese manuscript adds to the vutti `uttamiti bhāvaniddeso katthaci abhāvaṃ dasseti, brahmassa` — the abstract `uttaṃ` ("`u`-ness") hinting that the change sometimes fails, with `brahmassa` as its example — and a parallel sentence after §200 (`ārattaggahaṇena katthaci aniyamaṃ dasseti, satthussa …`), which reads `ārattaṃ` the same way. The CST has neither sentence, and it is the CST that is translated here.

---

<a name="m199"></a>

### §199 (158): Satthupitādīnamā sismiṃ silopo ca (`ā` for the final of `satthu`, `pitu` and the like before `si`, and `si` elided) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | satthupitādīnaṃ | (anto) | (āpajjate) | ā | sismiṃ | |
| ca     |        | si     | lopo      | ~~si~~     |        | |
|        | of `satthu`, `pitu` and the like | (the final) | (becomes) | `ā` | before `si` | |
| and    |        | `si`   | elision   | ~~si~~     |        | |

`satthupitādīnamā` is `satthupitādīnaṃ` + `ā` ("of `satthu`, `pitu` and the like: `ā`"), the niggahīta becoming `m` before the vowel by [§34 (52): Madā sare](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m34). The group is the agent nouns and kinship terms in `u`, the Sanskrit stems in *-ṛ*: `satthu` ("teacher"), `kattu` ("doer"), `vattu` ("speaker"), `pitu` ("father"), `mātu` ("mother"), `bhātu` ("brother"), `dhītu` ("daughter"). `sismiṃ` is "before `si`", `silopo` "elision of `si`". The `ca` here does not extend a previous rule: it joins the second operation to the first — the final becomes `ā` *and* the `si` is dropped, so nothing is left to become `o`. The CST's vutti writes the change as `attaṃ`, "becoming `a`", where the sutta itself says `ā`; other editions read `āttaṃ`, and the length is the sutta's.[^90] No marker: obligatory. The rules down to [§209 (168): Pitādīnamasimhi](#m209) carry `satthupitādīnaṃ` over.

:::

> Satthupituādīnamanto attamāpajjate sismiṃ, silopo ca hoti.

> Satthā, pitā, mātā, bhātā, kattā.

> Sisminti kimatthaṃ?

> Satthussa, pitussa, mātussa, bhātussa, kattussa.

The final of `satthu`, `pitu` and the like becomes `ā` before `si`, and the `si` is elided.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(satthu) | satthā | teacher |
| 🚹①⨀(pitu) | pitā | father |
| 🚺①⨀(mātu) | mātā | mother |
| 🚹①⨀(bhātu) | bhātā | brother |
| 🚹①⨀(kattu) | kattā | doer |

|     | satthu + si (①⨀)          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | satth + (u→ā) + si        | §199 |
| →   | satthā + ~~si~~           | §199 |
| →   | **satthā**                |      |

> `satthā devamanussānaṃ buddho bhagavā` \
> [(1V Veranjakanda: Verañjakaṇḍa)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/Veranjakanda#2) \
🚹①⨀(satthu) 🚹⑥⨂(devamanussa) 🚹①⨀(buddha) 🚹①⨀(bhagavantu) \
teacher | of gods and humans | awakened | blessed

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `sismiṃ` (before `si`) the `ā` belongs to the first form singular alone; before `sa` the final stays `u` by [§203 (162): Usasmiṃ salopo ca](#m203), with the augment `s` of [§61 (86): Sāgamo se](#m61).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑥⨀(satthu) | satthussa | (of) the teacher |
| 🚹⑥⨀(pitu) | pitussa | (of) the father |
| 🚺⑥⨀(mātu) | mātussa | (of) the mother |
| 🚹⑥⨀(bhātu) | bhātussa | (of) the brother |
| 🚹⑥⨀(kattu) | kattussa | (of) the doer |

> `mātuyā ca pitussa ca` \
> [(3V 3.5: Sattannaṃappahitepianujānana)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/3/3.5#867) \
🚺⑥⨀(mātu) 🔼(ca) 🚹⑥⨀(pitu) 🔼(ca) \
of the mother | and | of the father | and

:::

[^90]: Nandisena's edition reads `āttamāpajjate`, the grammatically expected form; the CST's `attaṃ` is a short vowel of the printing rather than a doctrine.

---

<a name="m200"></a>

### §200 (159): Aññesvārattaṃ (`āra` in the other inflections) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | (satthupitādīnaṃ) | (anto) | (āpajjate) | ārattaṃ | aññesu (vacanesu) | |
|        | (of `satthu`, `pitu` and the like) | (the final) | (becomes) | `āra` | in the other inflections | |

`aññesvārattaṃ` is `aññesu` + `ārattaṃ`, the `u` becoming `v` before the vowel by [§18 (20): Vamodudantānaṃ](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m18): "in the others, `āra`-ness". `satthupitādīnamanto` continues from [§199 (158): Satthupitādīnamā sismiṃ silopo ca](#m199); "the others" are the inflections other than `si`. The final `u` becomes `āra`, giving a stem `satthāra` that then inflects like a masculine in `a` — `satthāraṃ`, `satthārehi`, `satthāresu` — except where §205–§208 replace the inflection itself. No marker: obligatory, but the following rules take two inflections out of its reach: `naṃ` (optional `āra`, [§201 (163): Vā naṃmhi](#m201); `a`, [§202 (164): Satthunattañca](#m202)) and `sa` (`u`, [§203 (162): Usasmiṃ salopo ca](#m203)). For `pitu` and the kinship terms the `āra` is shortened to `ara` by [§209 (168): Pitādīnamasimhi](#m209): `pitaraṃ`, not `pitāraṃ`.

:::

> Satthupituādīnamanto aññesu vacanesu ārattamāpajjate.

> Satthāraṃ, pitaraṃ, mātaraṃ, bhātaraṃ, kattāraṃ,

> satthārehi, pitarehi, mātarehi, bhātarehi, kattārehi.

> Aññesvīti kimatthaṃ?

> Satthā, pitā, mātā, bhātā, kattā.

The final of `satthu`, `pitu` and the like becomes `āra` in the other inflections.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹②⨀(satthu) | satthāraṃ | the teacher |
| 🚹②⨀(pitu) | pitaraṃ | the father |
| 🚺②⨀(mātu) | mātaraṃ | the mother |
| 🚹②⨀(bhātu) | bhātaraṃ | the brother |
| 🚹②⨀(kattu) | kattāraṃ | the doer |
| 🚹③⨂(satthu) | satthārehi | (by) teachers |
| 🚹③⨂(pitu) | pitarehi | (by) fathers |
| 🚺③⨂(mātu) | mātarehi | (by) mothers |
| 🚹③⨂(bhātu) | bhātarehi | (by) brothers |
| 🚹③⨂(kattu) | kattārehi | (by) doers |

|     | satthu + aṃ (②⨀)          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | satth + (u→āra) + aṃ      | §200 |
| →   | satthār + (~~a~~ + aṃ)    | §83  |
| →   | **satthāraṃ**             |      |

|     | pitu + aṃ (②⨀)            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pit + (u→āra) + aṃ        | §200 |
| →   | pit + (āra→ara) + aṃ      | §209 |
| →   | pitar + (~~a~~ + aṃ)      | §83  |
| →   | **pitaraṃ**               |      |

|     | satthu + hi (③⨂)          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | satth + (u→āra) + hi      | §200 |
| →   | satthār + (a→e) + hi      | §101 |
| →   | **satthārehi**            |      |

> `mātaraṃ pitaraṃ hantvā` \
> [(5V 20.2: Pārājikādipañhā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/5V/20/20.2#2364) \
🚺②⨀(mātu) 🚹②⨀(pitu) 🔽(hantvā) \
mother | father | having killed

> `pacchā kicce samuppanne, kattāraṃ nādhigacchatī` \
> [(22J 1.9.10: Akataññujātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/22J/1/1.9/1.9.10#198) \
🔼(pacchā) 🚻⑦⨀(kicca) 🚻⑦⨀(samuppanna) 🚹②⨀(kattu) 🔼(na) ▶️🤟⨀(adhigacchati) \
later | when a task | has arisen | a doer | not | he finds

The five `hi` forms are not attested in the Tipiṭaka.

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `aññesu` (in the others) the `āra` does not appear before `si`, where [§199 (158): Satthupitādīnamā sismiṃ silopo ca](#m199) has already given `ā`.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(satthu) | satthā | teacher |
| 🚹①⨀(pitu) | pitā | father |
| 🚺①⨀(mātu) | mātā | mother |
| 🚹①⨀(bhātu) | bhātā | brother |
| 🚹①⨀(kattu) | kattā | doer |

:::

---

<a name="m201"></a>

### §201 (163): Vā naṃmhi (optionally [`āra`] before `naṃ`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| vā     | (satthupitādīnaṃ) | (anto) | (āpajjate) | (ārattaṃ) | naṃmhi | (vibhattimhi) |
| optionally | (of `satthu`, `pitu` and the like) | (the final) | (becomes) | (`āra`) | before `naṃ` | (inflection) |

Everything but `vā naṃmhi` continues from [§200 (159): Aññesvārattaṃ](#m200). That rule made `āra` obligatory in every inflection but `si`; this one takes `naṃ` (④⨂ and ⑥⨂) out and makes the `āra` optional there. The option marker `vā` indicates this is an alternate to two other forms before `naṃ`: `a` lengthened to `ā` by [§202 (164): Satthunattañca](#m202) (`satthānaṃ`), and the bare stem with its `u` lengthened by [§89 (87): Sunaṃhisu ca](#m89) (`pitūnaṃ`). All three are good Pāli; `satthārānaṃ` and `pitūnaṃ` are both attested.

:::

> Satthupituādīnamanto ārattamāpajjate vā naṃmhi vibhattimhi.

> Satthārānaṃ, pitarānaṃ, mātarānaṃ, bhātarānaṃ.

> Vāti kimatthaṃ?

> Satthānaṃ, pitūnaṃ, mātūnaṃ, bhātūnaṃ.

Optionally, the final of `satthu`, `pitu` and the like becomes `āra` before the `naṃ` inflection (④⨂ and ⑥⨂).

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑥⨂(satthu) | satthārānaṃ | (of) teachers |
| 🚹⑥⨂(pitu) | pitarānaṃ | (of) fathers |
| 🚺⑥⨂(mātu) | mātarānaṃ | (of) mothers |
| 🚹⑥⨂(bhātu) | bhātarānaṃ | (of) brothers |

|     | satthu + naṃ (⑥⨂)         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | satth + (u→āra) + naṃ     | §201 |
| →   | satthār + (a→ā) + naṃ     | §89  |
| →   | **satthārānaṃ**           |      |

|     | pitu + naṃ (⑥⨂)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pit + (u→āra) + naṃ       | §201 |
| →   | pit + (āra→ara) + naṃ     | §209 |
| →   | pitar + (a→ā) + naṃ       | §89  |
| →   | **pitarānaṃ**             |      |

> `imesaṃ mahānāma tiṇṇaṃ satthārānaṃ ekā niṭṭhā udāhu puthu niṭṭhā` \
> [(15A3 3.3.4: Bharaṇḍukālāmasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/15A3/3/3.3/3.3.4#978) \
⚧️⑥⨂(ima) 🚹⓪⨀(mahānāma) ⚧️⑥⨂(ti) 🚹⑥⨂(satthu) 🚺①⨀(eka) 🚺①⨀(niṭṭhā) 🔼(udāhu) 🔼(puthu) 🚺①⨀(niṭṭhā) \
of these | Mahānāma | three | teachers | one | goal | or | separate | goals

`pitarānaṃ`, `mātarānaṃ` and `bhātarānaṃ` are not attested in the Tipiṭaka.

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

As the rule is optional, there are exceptions: where the `āra` is not taken, `satthu` gives `satthānaṃ` by [§202 (164): Satthunattañca](#m202), and `pitu`, `mātu`, `bhātu` keep their `u`, lengthened before `naṃ` by [§89 (87): Sunaṃhisu ca](#m89).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑥⨂(satthu) | satthānaṃ | (of) teachers |
| 🚹⑥⨂(pitu) | pitūnaṃ | (of) fathers |
| 🚺⑥⨂(mātu) | mātūnaṃ | (of) mothers |
| 🚹⑥⨂(bhātu) | bhātūnaṃ | (of) brothers |

|     | pitu + naṃ (⑥⨂)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pit + (u→ū) + naṃ         | §89  |
| →   | **pitūnaṃ**               |      |

> `dassāmi dānaṃ pitūnaṃ pitāmahānaṃ` \
> [(19Pv 2.8: Cūḷaseṭṭhipetavatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/19Pv/2/2.8#272) \
⏯👆⨀(dadāti) 🚻②⨀(dāna) 🚹④⨂(pitu) 🚹④⨂(pitāmaha) \
I shall give | a gift | to the fathers | to the grandfathers

:::

---

<a name="m202"></a>

### §202 (164): Satthunattañca (and `a` for the final of `satthu` [before `naṃ`]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca (vā) | satthuno | (anto) | (āpajjate) | attaṃ | (naṃmhi) | |
| and (optionally) | of `satthu` | (the final) | (becomes) | `a` | (before `naṃ`) | |

`satthunattañca` is `satthuno` + `attaṃ` + `ca`: `satthuno` is the sixth form of `satthu` itself (`sa` → `no` by [§117 (124): Jhalato sassano vā](#m117)), its `o` elided before `attaṃ` by [§12 (13): Sarā sare lopaṃ](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m12), and the niggahīta of `attaṃ` becomes `ñ` before `ca` by [§31 (49): Vaggantaṃ vā vagge](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m31): "and `a`-ness of `satthu`". `vā` and `naṃmhi` continue from [§201 (163): Vā naṃmhi](#m201). The sutta names `satthu` alone, but the vutti's last sentence explains that the `ca` is there to gather in the others (`aññesampi saṅgahaṇatthaṃ`) — `pitu`, `mātu`, `bhātu`, `kattu` — so by saying `ca` this rule is an extension of §201, adding a second optional form before `naṃ`. The `a` is then lengthened before `naṃ` by [§89 (87): Sunaṃhisu ca](#m89): `satthānaṃ`. The carried-over `vā` keeps the `satthārānaṃ` of §201 available as the alternative.

:::

> Tassa satthusaddassa anto attamāpajjate vā naṃmhi vibhattimhi.

> Satthānaṃ, pitānaṃ, mātānaṃ, bhātānaṃ, kattānaṃ.

> Vāti kimatthaṃ?

> Satthārānaṃ, pitarānaṃ, mātarānaṃ, bhātarānaṃ, dhītarānaṃ.

Optionally, the final of that word `satthu` becomes `a` before the `naṃ` inflection (④⨂ and ⑥⨂).

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑥⨂(satthu) | satthānaṃ | (of) teachers |
| 🚹⑥⨂(pitu) | pitānaṃ | (of) fathers |
| 🚺⑥⨂(mātu) | mātānaṃ | (of) mothers |
| 🚹⑥⨂(bhātu) | bhātānaṃ | (of) brothers |
| 🚹⑥⨂(kattu) | kattānaṃ | (of) doers |

|     | satthu + naṃ (⑥⨂)         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | satth + (u→a) + naṃ       | §202 |
| →   | satth + (a→ā) + naṃ       | §89  |
| →   | **satthānaṃ**             |      |

None of the five is attested in the Tipiṭaka, which uses `satthārānaṃ` and `pitūnaṃ` (§201).

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

As the rule is optional, there are exceptions: the `āra` of [§201 (163): Vā naṃmhi](#m201) remains available before `naṃ`.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑥⨂(satthu) | satthārānaṃ | (of) teachers |
| 🚹⑥⨂(pitu) | pitarānaṃ | (of) fathers |
| 🚺⑥⨂(mātu) | mātarānaṃ | (of) mothers |
| 🚹⑥⨂(bhātu) | bhātarānaṃ | (of) brothers |
| 🚺⑥⨂(dhītu) | dhītarānaṃ | (of) daughters |

:::

> Caggahaṇaṃ aññesampi saṅgahaṇatthaṃ.

The mention of `ca` is for the inclusion of the others too [`pitu`, `mātu`, `bhātu`, `kattu` and the rest of the group, though the sutta names only `satthu`].

---

<a name="m203"></a>

### §203 (162): Usasmiṃ salopo ca (`u` before `sa`, and `sa` elided) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| (vā)   | (satthupitādīnaṃ) | (antassa) | (hoti) | u | sasmiṃ | |
| ca     |        | sa     | lopo      | ~~sa~~     |        | |
| (optionally) | (of `satthu`, `pitu` and the like) | (the final) | (becomes) | `u` | before `sa` | |
| and    |        | `sa`   | elision   | ~~sa~~     |        | |

`usasmiṃ` is `u` + `sasmiṃ` ("`u`, before `sa`"); `salopo` "elision of `sa`". The group continues from the preceding rules, and the vutti carries the `vā` of [§201 (163): Vā naṃmhi](#m201) over. Before `sa` (④⨀ and ⑥⨀) the final does not become `āra` but stays — or, as the rule puts it, becomes — `u`; and the `ca`, as in [§199 (158): Satthupitādīnamā sismiṃ silopo ca](#m199), joins the second operation to the first: the `sa` is elided. The option `vā` ranges over that elision: with `sa` dropped, `satthu`; with `sa` kept, `satthussa` (with the augment `s` of [§61 (86): Sāgamo se](#m61)) or `satthuno` (`sa` → `no` by [§117 (124): Jhalato sassano vā](#m117), the stem in `u` being a `la` word). No form with `āra` before `sa` is taught.

:::

> Satthupituiccevamādīnamantassa uttaṃ hoti vā sasmiṃ, salopo ca.

> Satthu, satthussa, satthuno dīyate, pariggaho vā.

> Pitu, pitussa, pituno dīyate, pariggaho vā.

> Bhātu, bhātussa, bhātuno dīyate, pariggaho vā.

Optionally, the final of `satthu`, `pitu` and the like becomes `u` before `sa`, and the `sa` is elided.

:::tip[Examples]

Each form serves both the fourth form ④⨀ (`dīyate`, "is given to") and the sixth ⑥⨀ (`pariggaho`, "the possession of").

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(satthu) | satthu dīyate | it is given to the teacher |
| 🚹④⨀(satthu) | satthussa dīyate | it is given to the teacher |
| 🚹④⨀(satthu) | satthuno dīyate | it is given to the teacher |
| 🚹⑥⨀(satthu) | satthu pariggaho | the teacher's possession |
| 🚹⑥⨀(satthu) | satthussa pariggaho | the teacher's possession |
| 🚹⑥⨀(satthu) | satthuno pariggaho | the teacher's possession |
| 🚹④⨀(pitu) | pitu dīyate | it is given to the father |
| 🚹④⨀(pitu) | pitussa dīyate | it is given to the father |
| 🚹④⨀(pitu) | pituno dīyate | it is given to the father |
| 🚹④⨀(bhātu) | bhātu dīyate | it is given to the brother |
| 🚹④⨀(bhātu) | bhātussa dīyate | it is given to the brother |
| 🚹④⨀(bhātu) | bhātuno dīyate | it is given to the brother |

|     | satthu + sa (⑥⨀)          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | satthu + ~~sa~~           | §203 |
| →   | **satthu**                |      |

|     | satthu + sa (⑥⨀)          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | satthu + (s) + sa         | §61  |
| →   | **satthussa**             |      |

|     | satthu + sa (⑥⨀)          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | satthu + (sa→no)          | §117 |
| →   | **satthuno**              |      |

> `agamuṃ satthu dassanaṃ` \
> [(3V 4.26: Pavāraṇāsaṅgaha)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/4/4.26#1119) \
⏮🤟⨂(gacchati) 🚹⑥⨀(satthu) 🚻②⨀(dassana) \
they went | of the teacher | to the sight

> `ko nu kho bhante ānanda pituno dāyajjo` \
> [(1V 1.1.2.2: Vinītavatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.1/1.1.2/1.1.2.2#371) \
🚹①⨀(ka) 🔼(nu kho) 🔼(bhante) 🚹⓪⨀(ānanda) 🚹⑥⨀(pitu) 🚹①⨀(dāyajja) \
who | then | venerable sir | Ānanda | of the father | the heir

> `satthussa sāsanenāpi` \
> [(5V 15.7: Pucchāvibhāga)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/5V/15/15.7#1879) \
🚹⑥⨀(satthu) 🚻③⨀(sāsana) 🔼(api) \
of the teacher | by the teaching | also

:::

> Caggahaṇaṃ dutiyasampiṇḍanatthaṃ.

The mention of `ca` is for gathering in the second [operation, the elision of `sa`, alongside the first].

---

<a name="m204"></a>

### §204 (167): Sakkamandhātādīnañca (and for `sakkamandhātu` and the like) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     | sakkamandhātādīnaṃ | (anto) | (āpajjate) | (uttaṃ) | (sasmiṃ) | (niccaṃ) |
| ca     |        | (sa)   | (lopo)    | (~~sa~~)   |        | (niccaṃ) |
| and    | of `sakkamandhātu` and the like | (the final) | (becomes) | (`u`) | (before `sa`) | (invariably) |
| and    |        | (`sa`) | (elision) | (~~sa~~)   |        | (invariably) |

`sakkamandhātādīnañca` is `sakkamandhātu` + `ādīnaṃ` + `ca`, the niggahīta becoming `ñ` before `ca` by [§31 (49): Vaggantaṃ vā vagge](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m31). `Mandhātu` is the legendary universal monarch who shared Sakka's throne — hence `Sakkamandhātu`, "Mandhātā, Sakka's peer" — and "the like" are the agent nouns in `-tu`: `kattu` ("doer"), `gantu` ("goer"), `dātu` ("giver"). Everything the rule states — `u` before `sa` and the elision of `sa` — is already in [§203 (162): Usasmiṃ salopo ca](#m203), and the vutti asks why it is begun again (`punārambhaggahaṇaṃ`): to show that for these words the operation is invariable (`nicca`). The `vā` of §203 does not carry over: `sakkamandhātu` is the only sixth-form singular, with no `sakkamandhātussa` or `sakkamandhātuno` beside it. As in §203 the `ca` joins the second operation, the elision, to the first (`dutiyasampiṇḍanatthaṃ`).

:::

> Sakkamandhātuiccevamādīnamanto uttamāpajjate sasmiṃ, salopo ca hoti.

> Sakkamandhātu iva assa rājino vibhavo. Evaṃ kattu, gantu, dātu iccevamādī.

The final of `sakkamandhātu` and the like becomes `u` before `sa`, and the `sa` is elided [invariably].

:::tip[Examples]

> `sakkamandhātu iva assa rājino vibhavo` \
🚹⑥⨀(sakkamandhātu) 🔼(iva) 🚹⑥⨀(ima) 🚹⑥⨀(rāja) 🚹①⨀(vibhava) \
of Sakka-Mandhātā | like | of this | king | the wealth

"This king's wealth is like Sakka-Mandhātā's." `assa` is the sixth form of `ima` by [§177 (221): Imasaddassa ca](#m177), `rājino` that of `rāja` by [§135 (118): Rājassa rañño rājino se](#m135).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑥⨀(sakkamandhātu) | sakkamandhātu | (of) Sakka-Mandhātā |
| 🚹⑥⨀(kattu) | kattu | (of) the doer |
| 🚹⑥⨀(gantu) | gantu | (of) the goer |
| 🚹⑥⨀(dātu) | dātu | (of) the giver |

|     | mandhātu + sa (⑥⨀)        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | mandhātu + ~~sa~~         | §204 |
| →   | **mandhātu**              |      |

> `sabbeva dāsā mandhātu, ye pāṇā pathavissitā` \
> [(22J 3.1.8: Mandhātujātaka)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/22J/3/3.1/3.1.8#698) \
🚹①⨂(sabba) 🔼(eva) 🚹①⨂(dāsa) 🚹⑥⨀(mandhātu) 🚹①⨂(ya) 🚹①⨂(pāṇa) 🚹①⨂(pathavissita) \
all | indeed | slaves | of Mandhātā | whatever | beings | dwell on the earth

The compound `sakkamandhātu` and the bare `kattu`, `gantu`, `dātu` are not attested as separate words in the Tipiṭaka.

:::

> Punārambhaggahaṇaṃ kimatthaṃ?

> Niccadīpanatthaṃ.

> Sakkamandhātu.

> Caggahaṇaṃ dutiyasampiṇḍanatthaṃ.

:::tip[Purpose]

Why is the rule begun again, when [§203 (162): Usasmiṃ salopo ca](#m203) already gives `u` before `sa` and the elision of `sa`? To show that [for these words] it is invariable: `sakkamandhātu` has no alternative form with `ssa` or `no`. The mention of `ca` is, as in §203, for gathering in the second [operation, the elision].

:::

---

<a name="m205"></a>

### §205 (160): Tato yonamo tu (after that, `o` for `yo`; and elsewhere too) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| tu     | tato (ārādesato) | yonaṃ | (ādeso) | o | | |
| but    | after that (`āra` replacement) | the `yo` inflections | (replaced by) | `o` | | |

`yonamo` is `yonaṃ` + `o`, the niggahīta becoming `m` by [§34 (52): Madā sare](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m34): "`o` for the `yo`s". `tato` ("after that") points back to the `āra` of [§200 (159): Aññesvārattaṃ](#m200): once `satthu` has become `satthāra`, both `yo` inflections (①⨂ and ②⨂, `sabbesaṃ yonaṃ` in the vutti) are replaced by `o`. The marker `tu` ("but") contrasts this with what the new stem in `a` would otherwise take, the `ā` and `e` of [§107 (69): Sabbayonīnamāe](#m107); the vutti also reads it (`tuggahaṇena`) as reaching beyond the `āra` stems, and accounts by it for the `o` of `caturo` (`catu`, "four"), `gāvo` (`go`, "cow") and `ubho` (`ubha`, "both"). No `vā`: `satthāro` is the only plural taught here, though the Bālāvatāra also allows `satthāre` in the second form.

:::

> Tato ārādesato sabbesaṃ yo naṃ okārādeso hoti.

> Satthāro, pitaro, mātaro, bhātaro, kattāro, vattāro.

After that `āra` replacement, all the `yo` inflections (①⨂ and ②⨂) are replaced by the letter `o`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(satthu) | satthāro | teachers |
| 🚹①⨂(pitu) | pitaro | fathers |
| 🚺①⨂(mātu) | mātaro | mothers |
| 🚹①⨂(bhātu) | bhātaro | brothers |
| 🚹①⨂(kattu) | kattāro | doers |
| 🚹①⨂(vattu) | vattāro | speakers |

|     | satthu + yo (①⨂)          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | satth + (u→āra) + yo      | §200 |
| →   | satthāra + (yo→o)         | §205 |
| →   | satthār + (~~a~~ + o)     | §83  |
| →   | **satthāro**              |      |

|     | pitu + yo (①⨂)            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pit + (u→āra) + yo        | §200 |
| →   | pit + (āra→ara) + yo      | §209 |
| →   | pitara + (yo→o)           | §205 |
| →   | pitar + (~~a~~ + o)       | §83  |
| →   | **pitaro**                |      |

> `pañcime moggallāna satthāro santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ` \
> [(4V 7.1.3: Pañcasatthukathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/4V/7/7.1/7.1.3#1507) \
⚧️①⨂(pañca) ⚧️①⨂(ima) 🚹⓪⨀(moggallāna) 🚹①⨂(satthu) 🚹①⨂(santa) 🚹①⨂(saṃvijjamāna) 🚹⑦⨀(loka) \
five | these | Moggallāna | teachers | existing | are found | in the world

> `akhīṇeva kamme pitaro ca pitāmahā ca kālaṅkatā` \
> [(4V 7.1.1: Chasakyapabbajjākathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/4V/7/7.1/7.1.1#1492) \
🚻⑦⨀(akhīṇa) 🔼(eva) 🚻⑦⨀(kamma) 🚹①⨂(pitu) 🔼(ca) 🚹①⨂(pitāmaha) 🔼(ca) 🚹①⨂(kālaṅkata) \
unfinished | still | the work | fathers | and | grandfathers | and | have died

:::

> Tuggahaṇena aññasmāpi yonaṃ okāro hoti.

> Caturo janā, gāvo, ubho purisā.

By the mention of `tu`, the `yo` inflections become the letter `o` after other [stems] as well.[^91]

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️①⨂(catu) | caturo janā | four people |
| 🚹①⨂(go) | gāvo | cows |
| ⚧️①⨂(ubha) | ubho purisā | both men |

`caturo` stands beside the `cattāro` of [§133 (230): Ti catunnaṃ tisso catasso tayo cattāro tīṇi cattāri](#m133); `gāvo` has the `āva` of [§74 (169): Yosu ca](#m74) before its `o`.

> `caturo janā saṃvidhāya, garubhaṇḍaṃ avāharuṃ` \
> [(5V 20.1: Avippavāsapañhā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/5V/20/20.1#2355) \
⚧️①⨂(catu) 🚹①⨂(jana) 🔽(saṃvidhāya) 🚻②⨀(garubhaṇḍa) ⏮🤟⨂(avaharati) \
four | people | having conspired | a valuable article | stole

> `neva gāvo haññiṃsu, na ajeḷakā haññiṃsu` \
> [(6D 5.4.6: Soḷasākāra)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/6D/5/5.4/5.4.6#561) \
🔼(na eva) 🚹①⨂(go) 🔵⏮🤟⨂(haññati) 🔼(na) 🚹①⨂(ajeḷaka) 🔵⏮🤟⨂(haññati) \
not | cows | were killed | not | goats and sheep | were killed

:::

[^91]: Nandisena's edition records that the Sinhalese text adds a fourth example, `najjo`: `nadī` + `yo`, the `yo` becoming `o` by this `tu`, the `ī` then `y` and `dy` giving `jj` ([§269 (41): Yavataṃ ta la ṇa dakārānaṃ byañjanāni ca la ña jakārattaṃ](#m269)). It is a Tipiṭaka form, which the Rūpasiddhi derives by this rule's `tu` (`tato yonamo tū ti ettha tu saddena yonamokāro`), and it shows the extension reaching a feminine `ī`-stem.

---

<a name="m206"></a>

### §206 (165): Tato smimi (after that, `i` for `smiṃ`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | tato (ārādesato) | smiṃ (vacanassa) | (ādeso) | i | | |
|        | after that (`āra` replacement) | the `smiṃ` inflection | (replaced by) | `i` | | |

`smimi` is `smiṃ` + `i` by [§34 (52): Madā sare](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m34). `tato` again means "after the `āra` replacement" of [§200 (159): Aññesvārattaṃ](#m200); since the previous rule already supplied `tato`, its repetition (`puna tatogahaṇena`) is read as a signal that the `i` reaches another stem too: `bhū` ("earth"), whose `smiṃ` gives `bhuvi`. No marker: `satthari` is the only ⑦⨀. Before this `i` the `āra` is shortened to `ara` by [§208 (166): Āro rassamikāre](#m208).

:::

> Tato ārādesato smiṃvacanassa ikārādeso hoti.

> Satthari, pitari, mātari, dhītari, bhātari, kattari, vattari.

After that `āra` replacement, the `smiṃ` inflection (⑦⨀) is replaced by the letter `i`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨀(satthu) | satthari | (in) the teacher |
| 🚹⑦⨀(pitu) | pitari | (in) the father |
| 🚺⑦⨀(mātu) | mātari | (in) the mother |
| 🚺⑦⨀(dhītu) | dhītari | (in) the daughter |
| 🚹⑦⨀(bhātu) | bhātari | (in) the brother |
| 🚹⑦⨀(kattu) | kattari | (in) the doer |
| 🚹⑦⨀(vattu) | vattari | (in) the speaker |

|     | satthu + smiṃ (⑦⨀)        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | satth + (u→āra) + smiṃ    | §200 |
| →   | satthāra + (smiṃ→i)       | §206 |
| →   | satth + (āra→ara) + i     | §208 |
| →   | satthar + (~~a~~ + i)     | §83  |
| →   | **satthari**              |      |

> `satthari anupāhane caṅkamamāne` \
> [(3V 5.5: Ajjhārāmeupāhanapaṭikkhepa)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/5/5.5#1153) \
🚹⑦⨀(satthu) 🚹⑦⨀(anupāhana) 🚹⑦⨀(caṅkamamāna) \
while the teacher | without sandals | was walking

> `mātupi puttamhi puttassapi mātari` \
> [(8D 3.5: Dasavassāyukasamaya)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/8D/3/3.5#222) \
🚺⑥⨀(mātu) 🔼(pi) 🚹⑦⨀(putta) 🚹⑥⨀(putta) 🔼(pi) 🚺⑦⨀(mātu) \
of the mother | too | towards the son | of the son | too | towards the mother

:::

> Puna tatogahaṇena aññasmāpi smiṃvacanassa ikāro hoti.

> Bhuvi.

By the repeated mention of `tato`, the `smiṃ` inflection becomes the letter `i` after another [stem] as well.

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺⑦⨀(bhū) | bhuvi | on the earth |

`bhū` + `smiṃ`: the `smiṃ` becomes `i`, and the `ū` becomes `uv` before the vowel. The form is not attested in the Tipiṭaka.

:::

---

<a name="m207"></a>

### §207 (161): Nā ā (`ā` for `nā`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | (tato ārādesato) | nā (vacanassa) | (ādeso) | ā | | |
|        | (after that `āra` replacement) | the `nā` inflection | (replaced by) | `ā` | | |

The shortest sutta of the section: `nā` (the inflection, ③⨀) and `ā` (its replacement). `tato ārādesato` continues from [§206 (165): Tato smimi](#m206). No marker: after the `āra` the `nā` becomes `ā`, and the `ena` that [§103 (79): Ato nena](#m103) gives after other stems in `a` does not apply. By [§270 (120): Amha tumha ntu rāja brahmatta sakha satthu pitādīhi smā nāva](#m270) the `smā` of the fifth form ⑤⨀ follows `nā`, so `satthārā` serves both.

:::

> Tato ārādesato nāvacanassa āādeso hoti.

> Satthārā, pitarā, mātarā, bhātarā, dhītarā, kattārā, vattārā.

After that `āra` replacement, the `nā` inflection (③⨀) is replaced by `ā`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(satthu) | satthārā | (by) the teacher |
| 🚹③⨀(pitu) | pitarā | (by) the father |
| 🚺③⨀(mātu) | mātarā | (by) the mother |
| 🚹③⨀(bhātu) | bhātarā | (by) the brother |
| 🚺③⨀(dhītu) | dhītarā | (by) the daughter |
| 🚹③⨀(kattu) | kattārā | (by) the doer |
| 🚹③⨀(vattu) | vattārā | (by) the speaker |

|     | satthu + nā (③⨀)          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | satth + (u→āra) + nā      | §200 |
| →   | satthāra + (nā→ā)         | §207 |
| →   | satthār + (~~a~~ + ā)     | §83  |
| →   | **satthārā**              |      |

|     | pitu + nā (③⨀)            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pit + (u→āra) + nā        | §200 |
| →   | pit + (āra→ara) + nā      | §209 |
| →   | pitara + (nā→ā)           | §207 |
| →   | pitar + (~~a~~ + ā)       | §83  |
| →   | **pitarā**                |      |

> `satthārā vā satthāraṃ saṃghena vā saṃghaṃ` \
> [(6D 8.1: Samanuyuñjāpanakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/6D/8/8.1#635) \
🚹③⨀(satthu) 🔼(vā) 🚹②⨀(satthu) 🚹③⨀(saṃgha) 🔼(vā) 🚹②⨀(saṃgha) \
with a teacher | or | a teacher | with a community | or | a community

> `bhātāpi bhātarā vivadati` \
> [(4V 4.8: Adhikaraṇa)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/4V/4/4.8#918) \
🚹①⨀(bhātu) 🔼(pi) 🚹③⨀(bhātu) ▶️🤟⨀(vivadati) \
a brother | too | with a brother | quarrels

The first citation compares teacher with teacher and community with community; the second sets `bhātā` (①⨀, §199) beside `bhātarā`, and the same passage has `puttopi mātarā vivadati` and `puttopi pitarā vivadati`. `kattārā` and `vattārā` are not attested in the Tipiṭaka.

:::

---

<a name="m208"></a>

### §208 (166): Āro rassamikāre (`āra` shortened before `i`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        |        | āro (ārādeso) | rassaṃ (āpajjate) | (ara) | ikāre | |
|        |        | the `āra` replacement | (becomes) short | (`ara`) | before the letter `i` | |

`rassamikāre` is `rassaṃ` + `ikāre` by [§34 (52): Madā sare](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m34): "short, before the letter `i`". `āro` is the `āra` of [§200 (159): Aññesvārattaṃ](#m200), and the `i` is the one that [§206 (165): Tato smimi](#m206) puts in place of `smiṃ`. No marker: the shortening is obligatory, and it holds for every stem of the group, `satthu` and `kattu` included — `satthari`, never `satthāri`. For `pitu` and the kinship terms the next rule shortens the `āra` everywhere, so for them this rule adds nothing new.

:::

> Ārādeso rassamāpajjate ikāre pare.

> Satthari, pitari, mātari, dhītari, kattari, vattari.

The `āra` replacement becomes short when the letter `i` follows.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨀(satthu) | satthari | (in) the teacher |
| 🚹⑦⨀(pitu) | pitari | (in) the father |
| 🚺⑦⨀(mātu) | mātari | (in) the mother |
| 🚺⑦⨀(dhītu) | dhītari | (in) the daughter |
| 🚹⑦⨀(kattu) | kattari | (in) the doer |
| 🚹⑦⨀(vattu) | vattari | (in) the speaker |

|     | kattu + smiṃ (⑦⨀)         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | katt + (u→āra) + smiṃ     | §200 |
| →   | kattāra + (smiṃ→i)        | §206 |
| →   | katt + (āra→ara) + i      | §208 |
| →   | kattar + (~~a~~ + i)      | §83  |
| →   | **kattari**               |      |

> `pitupi puttamhi puttassapi pitari` \
> [(8D 3.5: Dasavassāyukasamaya)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/8D/3/3.5#222) \
🚹⑥⨀(pitu) 🔼(pi) 🚹⑦⨀(putta) 🚹⑥⨀(putta) 🔼(pi) 🚹⑦⨀(pitu) \
of the father | too | towards the son | of the son | too | towards the father

`kattari` and `vattari` are not attested in the Tipiṭaka; `satthari` is common (§206).

:::

---

<a name="m209"></a>

### §209 (168): Pitādīnamasimhi (for `pitu` and the like, before any inflection but `si`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | pitādīnaṃ | (ārādeso) | (rassaṃ āpajjate) | (ara) | asimhi | (vibhattimhi) |
|        | of `pitu` and the like | (the `āra` replacement) | (becomes short) | (`ara`) | before a non-`si` | (inflection) |

`pitādīnamasimhi` is `pitādīnaṃ` + `asimhi` by [§34 (52): Madā sare](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m34): "of `pitu` and the like, before [an inflection] that is not `si`". `ārādeso rassamāpajjate` continues from [§208 (166): Āro rassamikāre](#m208). Where §208 shortened the `āra` before `i` only, this rule shortens it for the kinship terms — `pitu`, `mātu`, `bhātu`, `dhītu` — before every inflection: `pitarā`, `pitaro`, `pitaraṃ`, `pitarehi`, `pitarānaṃ`. The agent nouns keep the long `āra` (`satthārā`, `kattāro`). `asimhi` rather than `aññesu` is chosen, the vutti says, as a hint (`ñāpana`) about the suffix `to` of [§248 (260): Kvaci to pañcamyatthe](#m248), which stands in the place of an inflection: before it the final is replaced by `i` — `mātito`, `pitito`. No marker: obligatory.

:::

> Pitādīnamārādeso[^41] rassamāpajjate asimhi vibhattimhi.

> Pitarā, mātarā, bhātarā, dhītarā[^40], pitaro, mātaro, bhātaro, dhītaro.

The `āra` replacement of `pitu` and the like becomes short before an inflection other than `si`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(pitu) | pitarā | (by) the father |
| 🚺③⨀(mātu) | mātarā | (by) the mother |
| 🚹③⨀(bhātu) | bhātarā | (by) the brother |
| 🚺③⨀(dhītu) | dhītarā | (by) the daughter |
| 🚹①⨂(pitu) | pitaro | fathers |
| 🚺①⨂(mātu) | mātaro | mothers |
| 🚹①⨂(bhātu) | bhātaro | brothers |
| 🚺①⨂(dhītu) | dhītaro | daughters |

|     | mātu + nā (③⨀)            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | māt + (u→āra) + nā        | §200 |
| →   | māt + (āra→ara) + nā      | §209 |
| →   | mātara + (nā→ā)           | §207 |
| →   | mātar + (~~a~~ + ā)       | §83  |
| →   | **mātarā**                |      |

|     | dhītu + yo (①⨂)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | dhīt + (u→āra) + yo       | §200 |
| →   | dhīt + (āra→ara) + yo     | §209 |
| →   | dhītara + (yo→o)          | §205 |
| →   | dhītar + (~~a~~ + o)      | §83  |
| →   | **dhītaro**               |      |

> `puttopi mātarā vivadati` \
> [(4V 4.8: Adhikaraṇa)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/4V/4/4.8#918) \
🚹①⨀(putta) 🔼(pi) 🚺③⨀(mātu) ▶️🤟⨀(vivadati) \
a son | too | with the mother | quarrels

> `tisso ca dhītaro` \
> [(2V 2.4.1.1: Lasuṇasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/2V/2/2.4/2.4.1/2.4.1.1#2568) \
⚧️①⨂(ti) 🔼(ca) 🚺①⨂(dhītu) \
three | and | daughters

:::

> Asimhiggahaṇaṃ tomhi pare ikārādesañāpanatthaṃ.

> Mātito, pitito, bhātito, duhitito.

The mention of `asimhi` (before a non-`si`) is to make known the replacement by `i` when the suffix `to` follows.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺⑤⨀(mātu) | mātito | on the mother's side |
| 🚹⑤⨀(pitu) | pitito | on the father's side |
| 🚹⑤⨀(bhātu) | bhātito | on the brother's side |
| 🚺⑤⨀(duhitu) | duhitito | on the daughter's side |

`duhitu` is an older doublet of `dhītu` ("daughter"). The suffix `to` has the sense of the fifth form ⑤ ([§248 (260): Kvaci to pañcamyatthe](#m248)); before it the `āra` is not merely shortened but replaced by `i`. Of the four, only `mātito` and `pitito` are attested in the Tipiṭaka.

> `ubhato sujāto hoti mātito ca pitito ca` \
> [(2V 1.5.9.1: Antepurasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/2V/1/1.5/1.5.9/1.5.9.1#1464) \
🔼(ubhato) 🚹①⨀(sujāta) ▶️🤟⨀(hoti) 🚺⑤⨀(mātu) 🔼(ca) 🚹⑤⨀(pitu) 🔼(ca) \
on both sides | well born | he is | on the mother's side | and | on the father's side | and

:::

[^40]: CST4 prints `mītarā pitaro` here; `mītarā` is not a word, and the set `pitu`, `mātu`, `bhātu`, `dhītu` that the rest of the vutti uses (`dhītari`, `dhītarā` in §206–§207, `dhītaro` in the same line) requires `dhītarā`.

[^41]: CST4 divides the word as `Pitādīna mārādeso`; it is `pitādīnaṃ` + `ārādeso`, "the `āra` replacement of `pitu` and the like".

---

<a name="m210"></a>

### §210 (239): Tayātayīnaṃ takāro tvattaṃ vā (optionally `tv` for the `t` of `tayā`, `tayi`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| vā     | tayātayīnaṃ | takāro | (āpajjate) | tvattaṃ | | |
| optionally | of `tayā` and `tayi` | the letter `t` | (becomes) | `tv` | | |

`tayātayīnaṃ` is the sixth-form plural of the pair `tayā`, `tayi` — the `i` of `tayi` lengthened before `naṃ` by [§89 (87): Sunaṃhisu ca](#m89) — the two forms of `tumha` ("you") that begin `tay-`: `tayā` for `nā` (③⨀) by [§145 (238): Nāmhi tayā mayā](#m145) and `tayi` for `smiṃ` (⑦⨀) by [§139 (245): Tumhamhākaṃ tayi mayi](#m139). `takāro` is their initial letter `t`, `tvattaṃ` "`tv`-ness". The option marker `vā` indicates this is an alternate to those two rules as they stand: `tvayā` and `tayā`, `tvayi` and `tayi` are all good Pāli, though only `tayā` and `tayi` are attested in the Tipiṭaka. The rule closes the section as an afterthought to the pronoun rules §139–§163, and the Rūpasiddhi places it among them, as 239 next to §145 (238).

:::

> Tayātayi iccetesaṃ takāro tvattamāpajjate vā.

> Tvayā, tayā,

> tvayi, tayi.

> Etesamiti kimatthaṃ?

> Tuvaṃ, tavaṃ.

Optionally, the letter `t` of `tayā` and `tayi` becomes `tv`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️③⨀(tumha) | tvayā | (by) you |
| ⚧️③⨀(tumha) | tayā | (by) you (option not taken) |
| ⚧️⑦⨀(tumha) | tvayi | (in) you |
| ⚧️⑦⨀(tumha) | tayi | (in) you (option not taken) |

|     | tumha + nā (③⨀)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (tumha + nā → tayā)       | §145 |
| →   | (t→tv) + ayā              | §210 |
| →   | **tvayā**                 |      |

> `āṇatto ahaṃ tayā` \
> [(1V 1.1.2: Dutiyapārājikasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.1/1.1.2#269) \
🚹①⨀(āṇatta) ⚧️①⨀(amha) ⚧️③⨀(tumha) \
ordered | I | by you

> `tayi gedhitacittosmi, cittaṃ vipariṇāmitaṃ` \
> [(7D 8.1: Pañcasikhagītagāthā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/7D/8/8.1#833) \
⚧️⑦⨀(tumha) 🚹①⨀(gedhitacitta) ▶️👆⨀(atthi) 🚻①⨀(citta) 🚻①⨀(vipariṇāmita) \
in you | with a heart of longing | I am | my mind | is transformed

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `etesaṃ` (of these) the change belongs to `tayā` and `tayi` alone; the `t` of the other forms of `tumha` — `tuvaṃ` (①⨀ by the `ca` of [§140 (232): Tvamahaṃ simhi ca](#m140), ②⨀ by [§146 (236): Tumhassa tuvaṃ tvamaṃmhi](#m146)) and `tavaṃ` (②⨀ by [§144 (234): Tavaṃ mamañca navā](#m144)) — is not touched.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️①⨀(tumha) | tuvaṃ | you |
| ⚧️②⨀(tumha) | tavaṃ | you |

> `mā kho paṭighaṃ janayi, sace anuvijjako tuvaṃ` \
> [(5V 12.1: Codanādipucchāvissajjanā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/5V/12/12.1#1772) \
🔼(mā kho) 🚹②⨀(paṭigha) ⏮🤘⨀(janeti) 🔼(sace) 🚹①⨀(anuvijjaka) ⚧️①⨀(tumha) \
do not | resentment | arouse | if | an investigator | you [are]

:::

---

> _Iti nāmakappe tatiyo kaṇḍo._

Thus ends the third section of the nāma chapter.

---

## Catutthakaṇḍa (Fourth Section)

<a name="m211"></a>

### §211 (126): Attanto hismimanattaṃ (`atta` final becomes `ana` before `hi`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        |        | attanto | (āpajjate) | anattaṃ | hismiṃ |         |
|        |        | the final of `atta` | (becomes) | `ana` | `hi` (③⨂, ⑤⨂) | |

The fourth section opens with `atta` ("self"), the last of the stems `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha`, `rāja` that [§188 (115): Brahmatta sakha rājādito amānaṃ](#m188)–[§190 (114): Yonamāno](#m190) treated together. `attanto` is `atta` + `anto`, "the final of `atta`". `hismiṃ` is the locative of the term `hi`, "before `hi`", exactly as `simhi` in [§94 (105): Simhi vā](#m94) is the locative of `si`; it does not name the inflection `smiṃ`, which gets its own rule in [§212 (129): Tato smiṃ ni](#m212). `anattaṃ` is `ana` + `ttaṃ`, "the state of being `ana`": the final `a` of `atta` is replaced by `ana`, and the last `a` of that `ana` then becomes `e` before `hi` by [§101 (80): Suhisvakāro e](#m101). There is no option marker: before `hi` the change is obligatory.

:::

> Tassa attano anto anattamāpajjate himhi vibhattimhi.

> Attanehi, attanebhi.

> Attantoti kimatthaṃ?

> Rājehi, rājebhi.

> Hisminti kimatthaṃ?

> Attano.

> Anattamitibhāvaniddesena attasaddassa sakādeso hoti sabbāsu vibhattīsu.

> Sako, sakā, sakaṃ, sake.

The final of that [word] `atta` becomes `ana` before the inflection `hi` (③⨂ and ⑤⨂).

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨂(atta) | attanehi | by themselves |
| 🚹③⨂(atta) | attanebhi | by themselves |

|     | atta + hi (③⨂)         | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | att + (a→ana) + hi     | §211 |
| →   | attan + (a→e) + hi     | §101 |
| →   | **attanehi**           |      |

|     | atta + hi (⑤⨂)         | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | att + (a→ana) + hi     | §211 |
| →   | attan + (a→e) + hi     | §101 |
| →   | attane + (hi→bhi)      | §99  |
| →   | **attanebhi**          |      |

Neither form is attested in the Tipiṭaka. The vutti itself uses the ⑥⨀ `attano` of [§213 (127): Sassa no](#m213) in its first words, `tassa attano`, "of that `atta`".

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `attanto` (the final of `atta`) the rule applies to `atta` alone. Another stem in `a` keeps its final before `hi`, where it becomes `e` by §101 as usual; for `rāja` these forms are admitted by the `ca` of [§169 (117): Rājassa rāju sunaṃhisu ca](#m169) beside `rājūhi`, `rājūbhi`.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨂(rāja) | rājehi | by kings |
| 🚹③⨂(rāja) | rājebhi | by kings |

By saying `hismiṃ` (before `hi`) the final is not changed before other inflections: before `sa`, which becomes `no` by [§213 (127): Sassa no](#m213), the `a` stays.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(atta) | attano | for oneself |

:::

:::note[Additional]

The vutti draws a further rule from the wording of the sutta. Because the change is stated as a state (`bhāvaniddesa`, "the being-`ana`", rather than "`ana` before `hi`"), the statement is taken to reach beyond its own context: the word `atta` may be replaced by `saka` ("one's own") in all inflections.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(saka) | sako | one's own |
| 🚹①⨂(saka) | sakā | one's own |
| 🚻①⨀(saka) | sakaṃ | one's own |
| 🚹②⨂(saka) | sake | one's own |

> `sake nivesane` \
> [(1V Veranjakanda: Verañjakaṇḍa)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/Veranjakanda#37) \
🚻⑦⨀(saka) 🚻⑦⨀(nivesana) \
in his own | house

The Bālāvatāra (Nāma ¶66) uses the same `bhāvaniddesa` differently, to carry `ana` over to the inflection `su` as well (`attanesu`).

:::

---

<a name="m212"></a>

### §212 (129): Tato smiṃ ni (after `atta`, `smiṃ` becomes `ni`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | tato (attato) | smiṃ | (ādeso) | ni      |        |         |
|        | after that (`atta`) | `smiṃ` (⑦⨀) | (replaced by) | `ni` | | |

`tato` ("after that") points back to the `atta` of [§211 (126): Attanto hismimanattaṃ](#m211); the vutti glosses it `attato`, "after `atta`", and it is carried on through [§213 (127): Sassa no](#m213) and [§214 (128): Smā nā](#m214). There is no option marker: `attani` is the only ⑦⨀. The same replacement is given for `brahma`, and by its `tu` for `kamma`, `camma` and `muddha`, by [§197 (125): Brahmato tu smiṃ ni](#m197).

:::

> Tato attato smiṃvacanassa ni hoti.

> Attani.

After that [stem] `atta`, the inflection `smiṃ` (⑦⨀) becomes `ni`.

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨀(atta) | attani | in oneself |

|     | atta + smiṃ (⑦⨀)   | rule |
| --- | ------------------ | ---- |
| →   | atta + (smiṃ→ni)   | §212 |
| →   | **attani**         |      |

> `attani kusalaṃ dhammaṃ` \
> [(1V 1.1.4: Catutthapārājikasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.1/1.1.4#557) \
🚹⑦⨀(atta) 🚹②⨀(kusala) 🚹②⨀(dhamma) \
in oneself | a wholesome | state

:::

---

<a name="m213"></a>

### §213 (127): Sassa no (after `atta`, `sa` becomes `no`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | (tato attato) | sassa | (ādeso) | no      |        |         |
|        | (after `atta`) | `sa` (④⨀, ⑥⨀) | (replaced by) | `no` | | |

`tato attato` continues from [§212 (129): Tato smiṃ ni](#m212). `sassa` is "of `sa`", the inflection shared by the fourth and sixth forms. There is no option marker: where the `jha` and `la` stems take `no` by choice ([§117 (124): Jhalato sassano vā](#m117), `aggino` beside `aggissa`), `atta` has `attano` alone and no `attassa`. Compare the companion stems: `rañño`, `rājino` ([§135 (118): Rājassa rañño rājino se](#m135)), `brahmuno` ([§198 (123): Uttaṃ sanāsu](#m198)) and `sakhino` ([§194 (131): Sakhantassi no nā naṃ sesu](#m194)).

:::

> Tato attato sassa vibhattissa no hoti.

> Attano.

After that [stem] `atta`, the inflection `sa` (④⨀ and ⑥⨀) becomes `no`.

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(atta) | attano | for oneself |
| 🚹⑥⨀(atta) | attano | of oneself |

|     | atta + sa (⑥⨀)    | rule |
| --- | ----------------- | ---- |
| →   | atta + (sa→no)    | §213 |
| →   | **attano**        |      |

> `attano ca parassa ca` \
> [(12S1 11.1.4: Vepacittisutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/12S1/11/11.1/11.1.4#1571) \
🚹⑥⨀(atta) 🔼(ca) 🚹⑥⨀(para) 🔼(ca) \
of oneself | and | of the other | and

:::

---

<a name="m214"></a>

### §214 (128): Smā nā (after `atta`, `smā` becomes `nā`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        | (tato attato) | smā | (ādeso) | nā        |        |         |
|        | (after `atta`) | `smā` (⑤⨀) | (replaced by) | `nā` | | |

`tato attato` continues from [§213 (127): Sassa no](#m213). The `smā` of the fifth form takes the shape of the `nā` of the third, so that `attanā` serves both ③⨀ and ⑤⨀ — the pattern that the chapter's last sutta, [§270 (120): Amha tumha ntu rāja brahmatta sakha satthu pitādīhi smā nāva](#m270), states for the whole group of stems. There is no option marker. The vutti then reads the repetition of `tato` as the warrant for a second, unrelated change, the `atra` of `atraja` (see Additional).

:::

> Tato attato smā vacanassa nā hoti.

> Attanā.

> Puna tatogahaṇena tassa attano takārasseva rakāro hoti sabbesu vacanesu.

> Atrajo, atrajaṃ.

After that [stem] `atta`, the inflection `smā` (⑤⨀) becomes `nā`.

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑤⨀(atta) | attanā | from oneself |

|     | atta + smā (⑤⨀)    | rule |
| --- | ------------------ | ---- |
| →   | atta + (smā→nā)    | §214 |
| →   | **attanā**         |      |

> `attanā paribhuñji` \
> [(1V 1.1.2.2: Vinītavatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.1/1.1.2/1.1.2.2#332) \
🚹③⨀(atta) ⏮🤟⨀(paribhuñjati) \
by himself | he used

In the texts the form is far more often the ③⨀ of the third form, as here; the ⑤⨀ is identical in shape.

:::

:::note[Additional]

By taking up `tato` a second time — the vutti of this rule repeats the `tato` already carried over from §212 — the `t` of that same `atta` becomes `r` in all inflections: `atraja` ("own-born", a son or daughter) for `attaja`.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(atraja) | atrajo | own son |
| 🚹②⨀(atraja) | atrajaṃ | own son |

|     | atta + ja + si (①⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | at + (t→r) + a + ja + si  | §214 |
| →   | atraja + (si→o)           | §104 |
| →   | **atrajo**                |      |

> `abhayassa hi atrajo` \
> [(3V 8.31: Aṭṭhacīvaramātikā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/8/8.31#1751) \
🚹⑥⨀(abhaya) 🔼(hi) 🚹①⨀(atraja) \
of Abhaya | indeed | the own son

:::

---

<a name="m215"></a>

### §215 (141): Jhalato ca (and after `jha`, `la`: `smā` becomes `nā`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     | jhalato | (smā) | (ādeso)   | (nā)       |        |         |
| and    | after `jha`, `la` stems | (`smā`, ⑤⨀) | (replaced by) | (`nā`) | | |

`smā` and `nā` continue from [§214 (128): Smā nā](#m214). By saying `ca` this rule is an extension of §214: the replacement of `smā` by `nā` given there for `atta` is extended to the `jha` stems (masculine `i`, `ī`) and `la` stems (masculine `u`, `ū`) of [§58 (29): Ivaṇṇuvaṇṇā jhalā](#m58). The vutti gives no `vā`, and the Bālāvatāra (Nāma ¶83) accordingly lists `agginā` alone for the ⑤⨀ of `aggi`.

:::

> Jhalaiccetehi smāvacanassa nā hoti.

> Agginā, daṇḍinā, bhikkhunā, sayambhunā.

> Smāti kimatthaṃ?

> Aggayo, munayo, isayo.

After these [stems termed] `jha` and `la`, the inflection `smā` (⑤⨀) becomes `nā`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑤⨀(aggi) | agginā | from the fire |
| 🚹⑤⨀(daṇḍī) | daṇḍinā | from the staff-bearer |
| 🚹⑤⨀(bhikkhu) | bhikkhunā | from the bhikkhu |
| 🚹⑤⨀(sayambhū) | sayambhunā | from the Self-existent One |

|     | aggi + smā (⑤⨀)    | rule |
| --- | ------------------ | ---- |
| →   | aggi + (smā→nā)    | §215 |
| →   | **agginā**         |      |

|     | daṇḍī + smā (⑤⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | daṇḍ + (ī→i) + smā     | §84  |
| →   | daṇḍi + (smā→nā)       | §215 |
| →   | **daṇḍinā**            |      |

The long finals of `daṇḍī` and `sayambhū` are shortened before the singular inflection by [§84 (144): Agho rassamekavacanayosvapi ca](#m84). The result coincides with the ③⨀, which is how the texts mostly use it:

> `agginā vā daḍḍhaṃ hoti` \
> [(1V 1.4.1.6: Aññātakaviññattisikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.4/1.4.1/1.4.1.6#1992) \
🚹③⨀(aggi) 🔼(vā) 🚻①⨀(daḍḍha) ▶️🤟⨀(hoti) \
by fire | or | burnt | is

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `smā` the rule touches no other inflection: before `yo` these stems take `a` by [§96 (148): Yosvakatarasso jho](#m96).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(aggi) | aggayo | fires |
| 🚹①⨂(muni) | munayo | sages |
| 🚹①⨂(isi) | isayo | seers |

> `ahiṃsakā ye munayo` \
> [(18Dh 17.5: Buddhapitubrāhmaṇavatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Dh/17/17.5#244) \
🚹①⨂(ahiṃsaka) 🚹①⨂(ya) 🚹①⨂(muni) \
harmless | those | sages

:::

---

<a name="m216"></a>

### §216 (180): Ghapato smiṃ yaṃ vā (optionally, after `gha`, `pa`: `smiṃ` becomes `yaṃ`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| vā     | ghapato | smiṃ  | (ādeso)   | yaṃ        |        |         |
| optionally | after `gha`, `pa` stems | `smiṃ` (⑦⨀) | (replaced by) | `yaṃ` | | |

`tasmā` in the vutti ("after that") points to the stems the sutta names: the `gha` stems (feminine `ā`, [§60 (177): Ā gho](#m60)) and the `pa` stems (feminine `i`, `ī`, `u`, `ū`, [§59 (182): Te itthikhyā po](#m59)). The option marker `vā` indicates this is an alternate to [§111 (179): Ghato nādīnaṃ](#m111) and [§112 (183): Pato yā](#m112), which turn the singular `nā`, `sa`, `smā`, `sa`, `smiṃ` into `āya` after `gha` and `yā` after `pa`: `kaññāyaṃ` and `kaññāya`, `rattiyaṃ` and `rattiyā` are both good Pāli, and the vutti lists them in pairs.

:::

> Tasmā ghapato smiṃ vacanassa yaṃ hoti vā.

> Kaññāyaṃ, kaññāya.

> Rattiyaṃ, rattiyā.

> Itthiyaṃ, itthiyā.

> Yāguyaṃ, yāguyā.

> Vadhuyaṃ[^50], vadhuyā.

After those [stems termed] `gha` and `pa`, the inflection `smiṃ` (⑦⨀) optionally becomes `yaṃ`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺⑦⨀(kaññā) | kaññāyaṃ | in the girl |
| 🚺⑦⨀(kaññā) | kaññāya | in the girl (by §111) |
| 🚺⑦⨀(ratti) | rattiyaṃ | at night |
| 🚺⑦⨀(ratti) | rattiyā | at night (by §112) |
| 🚺⑦⨀(itthī) | itthiyaṃ | in the woman |
| 🚺⑦⨀(itthī) | itthiyā | in the woman (by §112) |
| 🚺⑦⨀(yāgu) | yāguyaṃ | in the gruel |
| 🚺⑦⨀(yāgu) | yāguyā | in the gruel (by §112) |
| 🚺⑦⨀(vadhū) | vadhuyaṃ | in the daughter-in-law |
| 🚺⑦⨀(vadhū) | vadhuyā | in the daughter-in-law (by §112) |

|     | kaññā + smiṃ (⑦⨀)    | rule |
| --- | -------------------- | ---- |
| →   | kaññā + (smiṃ→yaṃ)   | §216 |
| →   | **kaññāyaṃ**         |      |

|     | itthī + smiṃ (⑦⨀)      | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | itth + (ī→i) + smiṃ    | §84  |
| →   | itthi + (smiṃ→yaṃ)     | §216 |
| →   | **itthiyaṃ**           |      |

The vutti's own `yaṃ` forms are not attested in the Tipiṭaka, but the rule is heard in the opening of almost every sutta, in the place names `Sāvatthiyaṃ` (a `pa` stem) and `Nāḷandāyaṃ` (a `gha` stem):

> `bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati` \
> [(10M 1.9: Bahuvedanīyasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/10M/1/1.9#237) \
🚹①⨀(bhagavantu) 🚺⑦⨀(sāvatthī) ▶️🤟⨀(viharati) \
the Blessed One | at Sāvatthī | dwells

> `bhagavā nāḷandāyaṃ viharati` \
> [(6D Kevattagahapatiputtavatthu: Kevaṭṭagahapatiputtavatthu)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/6D/11/Kevattagahapatiputtavatthu#827) \
🚹①⨀(bhagavantu) 🚺⑦⨀(nāḷandā) ▶️🤟⨀(viharati) \
the Blessed One | at Nāḷandā | dwells

:::

[^50]: CST4 prints `vakhuyaṃ`.

---

<a name="m217"></a>

### §217 (199): Yonaṃ ni napuṃsakehi (`yo` becomes `ni` after neuters) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| (vā)   | napuṃsakehi | (sabbesaṃ) yonaṃ | (ādeso) | ni |     |         |
| (optionally) | after neuter stems | (all) `yo` (①⨂, ②⨂) | (replaced by) | `ni` | | |

`sabbesaṃ` ("of all") in the vutti makes the rule cover both `yo`, the plural of the first and of the second form, and their vocative. The sutta carries no option marker; the vutti supplies `vā`, and the next rule cancels it for the stems in `a` with `niccaṃ`. The option is an alternate to [§118 (146): Ghapato ca yonaṃ lopo](#m118), which elides `yo` after the same stems and whose own examples include `aṭṭhī`, `aṭṭhīni`, `āyū`, `āyūni`. Whether `ni` is added or `yo` elided, the final vowel is lengthened by [§88 (147): Yosu katanikāralopesu dīghaṃ](#m88).

:::

> Sabbesaṃ yonaṃ ni hoti vā napuṃsakehi liṅgehi.

> Aṭṭhīni, aṭṭhī, āyūni, āyū.

> Napuṃsakehīti kimatthaṃ?

> Itthiyo.

After neuter stems, all the `yo` [inflections] (①⨂ and ②⨂) optionally become `ni`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻①⨂(aṭṭhi) | aṭṭhīni | bones |
| 🚻①⨂(aṭṭhi) | aṭṭhī | bones (by §118) |
| 🚻①⨂(āyu) | āyūni | lives |
| 🚻①⨂(āyu) | āyū | lives (by §118) |

|     | aṭṭhi + yo (①⨂)      | rule |
| --- | -------------------- | ---- |
| →   | aṭṭhi + (yo→ni)      | §217 |
| →   | aṭṭh + (i→ī) + ni    | §88  |
| →   | **aṭṭhīni**          |      |

|     | aṭṭhi + yo (①⨂)      | rule |
| --- | -------------------- | ---- |
| →   | aṭṭhi + ~~yo~~       | §118 |
| →   | aṭṭh + (i→ī)         | §88  |
| →   | **aṭṭhī**            |      |

> `kāpotakāni aṭṭhīni bhavanti` \
> [(6D 2.3.3: Ajitakesakambalavāda)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/6D/2/2.3/2.3.3#250) \
🚻①⨂(kāpotaka) 🚻①⨂(aṭṭhi) ▶️🤟⨂(bhavati) \
dove-grey | the bones | become

:::

:::danger[Counter-example]

By saying `napuṃsakehi` (after neuters) the rule leaves the `yo` of masculine and feminine stems alone: the feminine `itthī` keeps `yo`, its final shortened before it by [§84 (144): Agho rassamekavacanayosvapi ca](#m84).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺①⨂(itthī) | itthiyo | women |

> `tisso itthiyo` \
> [(1V 1.1.1.3: Santhatabhāṇavāra)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.1/1.1.1/1.1.1.3#108) \
🚺①⨂(ti) 🚺①⨂(itthī) \
three | women

:::

---

<a name="m218"></a>

### §218 (196): Ato niccaṃ (always, after neuter `a`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| niccaṃ | ato (napuṃsakehi) | (yonaṃ) | (ādeso) | (ni)  |        |         |
| always | after neuter stems in `a` | (`yo`, ①⨂ ②⨂) | (replaced by) | (`ni`) | | |

`yonaṃ`, `ni` and `napuṃsakehi` continue from [§217 (199): Yonaṃ ni napuṃsakehi](#m217); `ato` adds the condition "after `a`". `niccaṃ` ("always") is the explicit opposite of `vā`: the option that §217 leaves open for neuters in general is closed for the neuter stems in `a`, whose `yo` is always replaced by `ni` and is neither elided nor turned into `ā` and `e` as the `yo` of the masculine `a` stems is by [§107 (69): Sabbayonīnamāe](#m107). The vutti lists each example twice, once for the `yo` of the first form and once for the `yo` of the second.

:::

> Akārantehi napuṃsakaliṅgehi yonaṃ ni hoti niccaṃ.

> Yāni, yāni.

> Tāni, tāni.

> Kāni, kāni.

> Bhayāni, bhayāni.

> Rūpāni, rūpāni.

After neuter stems ending in `a`, the `yo` [inflections] (①⨂ and ②⨂) always become `ni`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻①⨂(ya) | yāni | which (things) |
| 🚻②⨂(ya) | yāni | which (things) |
| 🚻①⨂(ta) | tāni | those |
| 🚻②⨂(ta) | tāni | those |
| 🚻①⨂(kiṃ) | kāni | which? |
| 🚻②⨂(kiṃ) | kāni | which? |
| 🚻①⨂(bhaya) | bhayāni | dangers |
| 🚻②⨂(bhaya) | bhayāni | dangers |
| 🚻①⨂(rūpa) | rūpāni | forms |
| 🚻②⨂(rūpa) | rūpāni | forms |

|     | rūpa + yo (①⨂)      | rule |
| --- | ------------------- | ---- |
| →   | rūpa + (yo→ni)      | §218 |
| →   | rūp + (a→ā) + ni    | §88  |
| →   | **rūpāni**          |      |

The `a` is lengthened before the `ni` by [§88 (147): Yosu katanikāralopesu dīghaṃ](#m88). `kāni` is `kiṃ` with the `ka` of [§229 (226): Sesesu ca](#m229).

> `cattārimāni, bhikkhave, bhayāni` \
> [(10M 2.7: Cātumasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/10M/2/2.7#429) \
🚻①⨂(catu) 🚻①⨂(ima) 🚹⓪⨂(bhikkhu) 🚻①⨂(bhaya) \
four | these | bhikkhus | dangers

> `kāni kammāni brūhayaṃ` \
> [(18Sn 2.9: Kiṃsīlasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/18Sn/2/2.9#378) \
🚻②⨂(kiṃ) 🚻②⨂(kamma) 🚹①⨀(brūhayanta) \
which | deeds | fostering

:::

:::note[Additional]

The Bālāvatāra (Nāma, the inflection of `citta`) nevertheless reaches `cittā` (①⨂) and `citte` (②⨂): it lets the `ni` itself take the `ā` and `e` of §107 (`nissa ā`), a device that keeps `niccaṃ` intact while admitting the neuter plurals without `ni` that the texts do use:

> `rūpā ādittā` \
> [(3V 1.12: Uruvelapāṭihāriyakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/1/1.12#198) \
🚻①⨂(rūpa) 🚻①⨂(āditta) \
forms | are burning

:::

---

<a name="m219"></a>

### §219 (195): Siṃ (neuter `si` becomes `aṃ`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| (niccaṃ) | (akārantehi napuṃsakehi) | si | (ādeso) | aṃ    |        |         |
| (always) | (after neuter stems in `a`) | `si` (①⨀) | (replaced by) | `aṃ` | | |

The sutta is the single word `siṃ`, `si` + `aṃ`; everything else — `akārantehi napuṃsakaliṅgehi` and `niccaṃ` — continues from [§218 (196): Ato niccaṃ](#m218). The rule is the neuter counterpart of [§104 (66): So](#m104), which makes `si` `o` after the masculine stems in `a`; `niccaṃ` again marks it obligatory, so `rūpaṃ` is the only ①⨀ of a neuter stem in `a`. Compare the `aṃ` that the `ntu` stems take in the neuter by [§125 (198): Aṃ napuṃsake](#m125).

:::

> Akārantehi napuṃsakaliṅgehi sivacanassa aṃ hoti niccaṃ.

> Sabbaṃ, yaṃ, taṃ, kaṃ, rūpaṃ.

After neuter stems ending in `a`, the inflection `si` (①⨀) always becomes `aṃ`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻①⨀(sabba) | sabbaṃ | all |
| 🚻①⨀(ya) | yaṃ | which |
| 🚻①⨀(ta) | taṃ | that |
| 🚻①⨀(kiṃ) | kaṃ | what? |
| 🚻①⨀(rūpa) | rūpaṃ | form |

|     | rūpa + si (①⨀)       | rule |
| --- | -------------------- | ---- |
| →   | rūpa + (si→aṃ)       | §219 |
| →   | rūp + (~~a~~ + aṃ)   | §83  |
| →   | **rūpaṃ**            |      |

|     | kiṃ + si (①⨀)        | rule |
| --- | -------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (kiṃ→ka) + si        | §229 |
| →   | ka + (si→aṃ)         | §219 |
| →   | k + (~~a~~ + aṃ)     | §83  |
| →   | **kaṃ**              |      |

The final `a` of the stem is elided before the `aṃ` by [§83 (67): Saralopo’ mādesa paccayādimhi saralope tu pakati](#m83).

> `sabbaṃ, bhikkhave, ādittaṃ` \
> [(3V 1.12: Uruvelapāṭihāriyakathā)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/3V/1/1.12#198) \
🚻①⨀(sabba) 🚹⓪⨂(bhikkhu) 🚻①⨀(āditta) \
all | bhikkhus | is burning

:::

---

<a name="m220"></a>

### §220 (74): Sesato lopaṃ gasipi (after the rest, `ga` and `si` too are elided) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| pi     | sesato | gasi   | lopaṃ     | ~~ga~~ ~~si~~ |     |         |
| also   | after the remaining stems | `ga` (⓪⨀) and `si` (①⨀) | elision | ~~`ga`~~ ~~`si`~~ | | |

`sesato` ("after the rest") names the stems for which the preceding rules have prescribed no replacement of `si` or `ga`: not the masculine stems in `a` (`si` becomes `o`, [§104 (66): So](#m104)), the neuter stems in `a` (`aṃ`, [§219 (195): Siṃ](#m219)), `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha`, `rāja` (`ā`, [§189 (113): Syā ca](#m189)) or the `ntu` stems (`ā`, [§124 (98): Ā simhi](#m124)), and before `ga` not `sakha` ([§113 (132): Sakhato gasse vā](#m113)) or the `gha` stems (`e`, [§114 (178): Ghate ca](#m114)) — but the `jha`, `la` and `pa` stems, and the stems whose final has already become `ā`. `gasi` is `ga` and `si`, and `pi` ("also") makes the elision reach both. The rule states no condition beyond this remainder: it is the general rule that the special treatments of `si` and `ga` presuppose, and the last that a derivation applies. The sutta reads `gasipi`[^52].

:::

> Tato niddiṭṭhehi liṅgehi[^51] sesakhatā[^53] gasi iccete lopamāpajjante.

> Bhoti itthi, sā itthī.

> Bho daṇḍi, so daṇḍī.

> Bho sattha, so satthā.

> Bho rāja, so rājā.

> Sesatoti kimatthaṃ?

> Puriso gacchati.

> Gasīti kimatthaṃ?

> Itthiyā, satthussa.

After the stems that are reckoned the remainder (`sesa`) once those specified [in the rules above] are set aside, these [two inflections], `ga` (⓪⨀) and `si` (①⨀), undergo elision.[^92]

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺⓪⨀(itthī) | bhoti itthi | madam! |
| 🚺①⨀(itthī) | sā itthī | that woman |
| 🚹⓪⨀(daṇḍī) | bho daṇḍi | sir staff-bearer! |
| 🚹①⨀(daṇḍī) | so daṇḍī | that staff-bearer |
| 🚹⓪⨀(satthu) | bho sattha | O teacher! |
| 🚹①⨀(satthu) | so satthā | that teacher |
| 🚹⓪⨀(rāja) | bho rāja | O king! |
| 🚹①⨀(rāja) | so rājā | that king |

|     | daṇḍī + ga (⓪⨀)      | rule |
| --- | -------------------- | ---- |
| →   | daṇḍ + (ī→i) + ga    | §245 |
| →   | daṇḍi + ~~ga~~       | §220 |
| →   | **daṇḍi**            |      |

|     | itthī + si (①⨀)      | rule |
| --- | -------------------- | ---- |
| →   | itthī + ~~si~~       | §220 |
| →   | **itthī**            |      |

Before `ga` the `jha`, `la` and `pa` finals are shortened by [§245 (152): Jhalapā rassaṃ](#m245); before `si` a non-neuter stem keeps its long vowel ([§85 (150): Na sismimanapuṃsakāni](#m85)). `satthu` and `rāja` reach `ga` and `si` with a final `ā` — by [§199 (158): Satthupitādīnamā sismiṃ silopo ca](#m199) and [§244 (72): Akārapitādyantānamā](#m244) — which [§246 (73): Ākāro vā](#m246) optionally shortens again before `ga`, giving `bho rāja` beside `bho rājā`.

> `yena sā itthī tenupasaṅkami` \
> [(1V 1.2.4: Attakāmapāricariyasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/1V/1/1.2/1.2.4#1181) \
🔼(yena) 🚺①⨀(ta) 🚺①⨀(itthī) 🔼(tena) ⏮🤟⨀(upasaṅkamati) \
where | that | woman | there | he approached

:::

:::danger[Counter-examples]

By saying `sesato` (after the rest) the elision does not apply where a replacement has been prescribed: the masculine `purisa` takes `o` for `si` by §104.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(purisa) | puriso gacchati | the man goes |

By saying `gasi` (`ga` and `si`) other inflections are not elided: after a `pa` stem the singular inflections become `yā` by [§112 (183): Pato yā](#m112), and `sa` after `satthu` takes the inserted `s` of [§61 (86): Sāgamo se](#m61).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺③⨀(itthī) | itthiyā | by the woman |
| 🚹⑥⨀(satthu) | satthussa | of the teacher |

> `satthussa sāsanenāpi` \
> [(5V 15.7: Pucchāvibhāga)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/5V/15/15.7#1879) \
🚹⑥⨀(satthu) 🚻③⨀(sāsana) 🔼(api) \
of the Teacher | by the instruction | also

:::

[^51]: CST4 prints `ligehi`.

[^52]: CST4's vutti section prints `pasipi`; its sutta list and the Bālāvatāra read `gasipi`.

[^53]: `sesakhatā` is CST4's reading; it is taken here as `sesa` + `akkhātā`, "declared the remainder", which is how the Bālāvatāra paraphrases the rule (`sesā`). Nandisena's edition reads plain `sesato`, repeating the sutta's own word and giving the construction directly (`tato niddiṭṭhehi liṅgehi sesato`); CST4's `sesakhatā` looks like a corruption of it. The translation is the same either way.

[^92]: U Nandisena (2005) attaches `niddiṭṭhehi` ("shown") to the remaining stems — "after the remaining stems that are shown" — and comments that the stems shown by `Siṃ`, `So`, `Syā ca` are what `sesa` means here. `Tato niddiṭṭhehi liṅgehi` is rather an ablative of separation governed by `sesato`, "after the rest, apart from the stems specified [above]": the specified stems are excluded, not included, as his own counter-example `puriso` shows.

---

<a name="m221"></a>

### §221 (282): Sabbāsamāvuso pasagganipātādīhi ca (all [inflections elided] after `āvuso`, prefixes, particles) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| ca     | āvuso pasagganipātādīhi | sabbāsaṃ (vibhattīnaṃ) | (lopo) | ~~vibhatti~~ | | |
| and    | after `āvuso`, prefixes, particles and the like | all (inflections) | (elision) | ~~inflection~~ | | |

`lopaṃ` continues from [§220 (74): Sesato lopaṃ gasipi](#m220), and `ca` carries the elision on from `ga` and `si` after the remaining stems to all fourteen inflections after the words named. In the sutta `sabbāsam-āvuso-pasagga-nipāta-ādīhi` is a single sandhi string: `sabbāsaṃ` ("of all") before a vowel, and `upasagga` losing its first vowel after `āvuso` by [§13 (15): Vā paro asarūpā](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m13). `āvuso` ("friend") is a vocative frozen for both numbers; the twenty prefixes (`upasagga`) and the particles (`nipāta`) carry no inflection at all; and `ādi` ("and the like") keeps the list open. For this `ca` the vutti itself says `avadhāraṇa`: it does not merely add a case but fixes the elision as invariable after these words.

:::

> Sabbāsaṃ vibhattīnaṃ ekavacanabahuvacanānaṃ paṭhamā dutiyātatiyā catutthī pañcamī chaṭṭhī sattamīnaṃ lopo hoti, āvuso upasagga nipātaiccevamādīhi ca.

> Tvaṃ panāvuso, tumhe panāvuso, padaso dhammaṃ vāceyya, vihāraṃ sve upagaccheyya.

> Pa, parā, ni, nī, u, du, saṃ, vi, ava, anu, pari, adhi, abhi, pati, su, ā, ati, api, apa, upa, pahāro, parābhavo, nihāro, nīhāro, uhāro, duhāro, saṃhāro, vihāro, avahāro, anuhāro, parihāro, adhihāro, abhihāro, patihāro, suhāro, āhāro, atihāro, apihāro, apahāro, upahāro, evaṃ vīsati upasaggehi ca.

> Yathā, tathā, evaṃ, khalu, kho, tatra, atho, atha, hi, tu ca, vā, vo, haṃ, ahaṃ[^93], alaṃ, eva, ho aho, he, ahe, re, are, evamādīhi nipātehi ca yojetabbāni.

> Caggahaṇamavadhāraṇatthaṃ.

All the inflections, singular and plural, of the first, second, third, fourth, fifth, sixth and seventh forms are elided after `āvuso` ("friend"), after the prefixes (`upasagga`), after the particles (`nipāta`), and after words of that kind.

:::tip[Examples]

> `tvaṃ panāvuso gotama` \
> [(10M 1.6: Upālisutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/10M/1/1.6#134) \
⚧️①⨀(tumha) 🔼(pana) 🔼(āvuso) 🚹⓪⨀(gotama) \
you | but | friend | Gotama

> `tumhe panāvuso` \
⚧️①⨂(tumha) 🔼(pana) 🔼(āvuso) \
you (pl.) | but | friends

> `padaso dhammaṃ vāceyya` \
> [(2V 1.5.1.4: Padasodhammasikkhāpada)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/2V/1/1.5/1.5.1/1.5.1.4#152) \
🔼(padaso) 🚹②⨀(dhamma) ⏯🤟⨀(vāceti) \
word by word | the Dhamma | should make (him) recite

> `vihāraṃ sve upagaccheyya` \
🚹②⨀(vihāra) 🔼(sve) ⏯🤟⨀(upagacchati) \
the monastery | tomorrow | should go to

`āvuso` addresses one person or many alike; `padaso` (from `pada` with the `so` of [§105 (0): So vā](#m105)) and `sve` ("tomorrow") take no inflection. The twenty prefixes, each shown before `hāra` ("carrying"):

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(pa + hāra) | pahāro | a blow (`pa` "forth") |
| 🚹①⨀(parā + bhava) | parābhavo | ruin (`parā` "away") |
| 🚹①⨀(ni + hāra) | nihāro | carrying down (`ni` "down", "into") |
| 🚹①⨀(nī + hāra) | nīhāro | carrying out (`nī` "out") |
| 🚹①⨀(u + hāra) | uhāro | carrying up (`u` "up") |
| 🚹①⨀(du + hāra) | duhāro | carrying badly (`du` "ill") |
| 🚹①⨀(saṃ + hāra) | saṃhāro | gathering together (`saṃ` "together") |
| 🚹①⨀(vi + hāra) | vihāro | dwelling (`vi` "apart") |
| 🚹①⨀(ava + hāra) | avahāro | taking away, theft (`ava` "down", "off") |
| 🚹①⨀(anu + hāra) | anuhāro | carrying along (`anu` "along", "after") |
| 🚹①⨀(pari + hāra) | parihāro | carrying around, care (`pari` "around") |
| 🚹①⨀(adhi + hāra) | adhihāro | carrying over (`adhi` "over") |
| 🚹①⨀(abhi + hāra) | abhihāro | bringing towards, an offering (`abhi` "towards") |
| 🚹①⨀(pati + hāra) | patihāro | carrying back (`pati` "back", "against") |
| 🚹①⨀(su + hāra) | suhāro | carrying well (`su` "well") |
| 🚹①⨀(ā + hāra) | āhāro | food, what is brought in (`ā` "to") |
| 🚹①⨀(ati + hāra) | atihāro | carrying beyond (`ati` "beyond") |
| 🚹①⨀(api + hāra) | apihāro | carrying over (`api` "over", "also") |
| 🚹①⨀(apa + hāra) | apahāro | taking away (`apa` "away") |
| 🚹①⨀(upa + hāra) | upahāro | bringing near, a gift (`upa` "near") |

The particles the vutti lists:

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🔼(yathā) | yathā | as |
| 🔼(tathā) | tathā | so |
| 🔼(evaṃ) | evaṃ | thus |
| 🔼(khalu) | khalu | indeed |
| 🔼(kho) | kho | indeed |
| 🔼(tatra) | tatra | there |
| 🔼(atho) | atho | and then |
| 🔼(atha) | atha | then |
| 🔼(hi) | hi | for, indeed |
| 🔼(tu) | tu | but |
| 🔼(ca) | ca | and |
| 🔼(vā) | vā | or |
| 🔼(vo) | vo | indeed |
| 🔼(haṃ) | haṃ | ah! |
| 🔼(ahaṃ) | ahaṃ | ah! (an interjection, not the pronoun) |
| 🔼(alaṃ) | alaṃ | enough |
| 🔼(eva) | eva | just, only |
| 🔼(ho) | ho | ho! |
| 🔼(aho) | aho | oh! |
| 🔼(he) | he | hey! |
| 🔼(ahe) | ahe | friend! |
| 🔼(re) | re | you there! |
| 🔼(are) | are | away with you! |

:::

:::note[Additional]

The vutti closes its two lists with a direction and a remark: "thus with the twenty prefixes; and [the rule] is to be applied with particles such as these." Then: "The word `ca` is for `avadhāraṇa` (making definite)" — the elision after these words admits no alternative. The Bālāvatāra (Nāma ¶196–¶197) gives the same rule with `tvaṃ āvuso`, `tumhe āvuso` and a much longer list of particles.

:::

[^93]: CST4 prints `abhaṃ`, which is no Pāli word. The list runs in `a`-prefixed doublets — `ho`/`aho`, `he`/`ahe`, `re`/`are` — so `haṃ`/`ahaṃ` is the pair expected, and the Bālāvatāra's own list of particles (Nāma ¶197: `… ciraṃ huraṃ ahaṃ sahaṃ …`) has `ahaṃ` among them: an interjection, distinct from the pronoun `ahaṃ` ("I").

---

<a name="m222"></a>

### §222 (342): Pumassa liṅgādīsu samāsesu (final of `puma` elided before `liṅga` etc. in compounds) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
|        |        | pumassa (anto) | (lopaṃ) | ~~a~~   | liṅgādīsu | samāsesu |
